The data tests run on each of them and on SQLite: `FLUENTCMS_TEST_PROVIDER` names the provider, Testcontainers starts the server once per run, and every test gets a database of its own. CI runs the four `Data tests` jobs, and a failure on any of them fails the run. The runs settled four choices ([ADR 0001](../decisions/0001-storage-spike-outcome.md#decisions-from-the-provider-runs)). PostgreSQL keeps JSON as `jsonb`, which rewrites the text, so reads put each value back in the form a write gives it (`StoredOrder`, where `IStorageConventions.ReordersJson` is true) and rich text compares as JSON. A `DateTimeOffset` is kept to the microsecond on every provider, because `timestamptz` keeps no more: a convention cuts it in front of the provider's converter, and values written with raw SQL take the same cut. A lease has ended once `lease_until <= now`, in the job queue, the outbox, the distributed lock and every claim dialect. SQL Server stores JSON as checked `nvarchar(max)` on every version, as "Column mapping" says.

**Generation rules.** Each schema gets its own EF model, so `IModelCacheKeyFactory` is replaced with one keyed on the schema hash. Migrations are always scaffolded on a fresh context, because a context that has already called `HasPendingModelChanges()` silently emits `CreateTable` for every table. Generated migration files are marked `<auto-generated />`, because EF marks only the designer and snapshot files and the composite index columns in the migration file fail the build with CA1861. On SQLite, `Migrate()` creates `__EFMigrationsLock`, which all streams share; it is the one exception to the `cms_` prefix rule.

**Development loop.** With `FluentCms:Database:SchemaSync = Auto` (default in Development on SQLite only) the content stream is skipped and additive changes (new tables, new nullable columns, new indexes) are applied by diffing the model against the database at startup. Destructive changes are reported, never applied. This gives "schema changes apply without restart" for new fields and keeps production on explicit migrations.

**Development loop outside Development, as built.** A host whose types are data has no compiled content migrations, which is the reference host with a schema file or the sample (Q2). `cms.AllowSchemaSyncOutsideDevelopment()`, in code only so that no configuration key turns it on, makes `SchemaSync` default to `Auto` on SQLite in every environment, with the rules of Development: additive changes apply at start and destructive ones are logged and left undone. `FluentCms:Database:SchemaSync = Off` still turns it off. A provider without schema sync (PostgreSQL, SQL Server) refuses the opt-in when `AddFluentCms` runs (`FCMS3154`), so their content tables stay on migrations. The startup report lists `schema-sync-outside-development` when sync runs outside Development. This is how Q2 works in Production on SQLite. On PostgreSQL and SQL Server a schema file is applied with `fluentcms schema apply` (below), in place of migrations generated from a schema file (10.3, P4).

**Schema apply as built (B-98, [#3021](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3021)).** `fluentcms schema apply` (6.1) is schema sync's engine, run by an operator rather than at start, on every provider. Nothing changes a server database when a host starts unless `MigrateOnStartup` is set (decision 55), and no migration files are made from a schema file: an image cannot load compiled migrations (decision 11), and files beside a schema file would be a second source of truth. `ContentSchemaDiff.PlanAsync` makes schema sync's comparison of the host's model with the database and turns each difference into steps of EF migration operations, which the provider's `IMigrationsSqlGenerator` writes as SQL:

- Additive, applied: a missing table with its indexes, a missing column, and a missing index. A required column with no default added to a table that has rows gets the value a read gives a field that has none, `''` for text and the empty document for rich text, and is refused otherwise (`FCMS3338`). A new column's check constraint is added with it, which SQLite does by rebuilding the table.
- A rename, applied: a missing column whose field lists a former name, or whose component above it does, while the table has a column of that name and the schema does not (`StoredField.FormerColumns`), is renamed and keeps its values. An index named for the old column is then dropped and made again under the new name.
- An index that differs or that the schema no longer has, applied: it is dropped and, if the schema has it, made again. An index holds no data.
- Destructive, refused unless `--allow-destructive` is given (`FCMS3337`): a stale content table dropped, a column the schema no longer has dropped, and a column whose store type or nullability differs altered. On SQL Server the check constraints that read such a column are dropped first and the model's added after.

The command also compares the newest row of the schema history with the host's schema (`SchemaSnapshotDiff`), which tells what the tables alone do not, and counts as lost: every change `schema diff` marks destructive (a removed type or field, a changed field type, list or localizable, a changed kind, localization or scope of a type), and a field renamed with `formerNames` whose values are not a column of a content type of its own, which is a relation, kept in `cms_reference` under the old name, or a field of a component or block, which may be kept in JSON; with `--allow-destructive` the columns of a flattened component's field are renamed all the same. A history whose newest snapshot cannot be read counts as lost too, since renames cannot be checked. A refused run applies nothing. Before it plans, the command refuses a host with content migrations, whose content tables belong to them and whose snapshot a change beside them would leave wrong (`FCMS3335`), and a pending system or plugin migration (`FCMS3336`). A run that applies holds the `IDistributedLock` named `fluentcms:schema-sync`, which schema sync takes too, from the plan to the history row, so that runs started together apply each operation once. EF's migration command executor runs the operations in one transaction, which it commits early only before a command the provider runs outside one, such as the foreign key pragma of a SQLite table rebuild; a database that refuses an operation rolls back the others (`FCMS3339`). The run then adds the row of the schema history as the runner does, failing with `FCMS3340` if it cannot, and a run with nothing to apply adds it too. `--dry-run` prints the plan and applies nothing. `--script` writes the SQL for the database as it is now, in a transaction in the provider's dialect, with the steps as comments, and applies nothing; the next run finds nothing to do and records the history, and after a destructive script it needs `--allow-destructive` too, since the history still says the change loses data. The PostgreSQL and SQL Server readers now return indexes from their catalogs, through queries the providers pass to `InformationSchemaReader`, and SQL Server's its check constraints (`DatabaseTable.CheckConstraints`). Startup validation still compares columns only.

Known gaps: the snapshots in `cms_version` keep a renamed field under its former name, as a content migration that renames a column leaves them; a check constraint keeps the name of a renamed column on SQL Server; and an altered column takes the provider's own conversion, so a change between types that do not convert fails and is rolled back.

**Startup validation.** The runner compares the content model with the database and fails with actionable errors (`FCMS1301: table cms_article is missing column subtitle; run 'fluentcms migrations add AddSubtitle'`). For a host with no content migrations at all it names schema apply first: `run 'fluentcms schema apply', or 'fluentcms migrations add AddSubtitle' to give the host content migrations`.

Related decisions: Q5 (property bags, validated by the spike), Q7 (JSON filtering depth).

### 4.4 Content service and query model

Rows 2.3 to 2.8, 2.14, 2.15, 2.19, 1.7, 1.8.

**Service.** `IContentService` works on `ContentDocument` (an ordered dictionary with typed accessors, used by the Management API, MCP and import) and `IContentService<T>` wraps it with a generated or reflection-based mapper. Both expose:

| Operation | Notes |
| --- | --- |
| `GetAsync(id, GetOptions)` | status, locale, fallback, select, populate |
| `QueryAsync(ContentQuery)` | returns `PagedResult<T>` with items, `total` (optional), `nextCursor` |
| `CreateAsync(entry)` | creates the draft; returns the entry with system fields |
| `UpdateAsync(id, entry, expectedVersion)` | full update of the draft: a field left out is cleared, except one the principal cannot read or write, which keeps its value. Conflict throws `VersionConflictException` with the current version. `expectedVersion: 0` adds a locale the entry does not have yet, and needs `update`, not `create` |
| `PatchAsync(id, patch, expectedVersion)` | partial update of the draft with a JSON merge patch; a null removes a field. Its own method, because a class cannot express absence |
| `DeleteAsync(id)` | trash, in every locale |
| `RestoreAsync(id)` | from trash, in every locale |
| `QueryTrashAsync(ContentQuery)`, `PurgeAsync(id)` | list the trash; delete an entry in it for good (4.5) |
| `PublishAsync(id, LocaleWriteOptions?)`, `UnpublishAsync(id, LocaleOptions?)`, `DiscardDraftAsync(id, LocaleWriteOptions?)` | publishing transitions (4.5); publish returns the entry and `warnings` |
| `ScheduleAsync(id, ScheduleRequest, LocaleOptions?)`, `CancelScheduleAsync(id, action, LocaleOptions?)` | per locale |
| `GetVersionsAsync(id, LocaleOptions?)`, `GetVersionAsync(id, number, LocaleOptions?)`, `RestoreVersionAsync(id, number, LocaleWriteOptions?)`, `DiffAsync(id, a, b, LocaleOptions?)` | versions |
| `BulkAsync(BulkRequest)` | create many; update, publish, unpublish, delete by id list or by filter with a required cap; per-item result |
| `CountAsync(CountOptions?)` | the count alone, by filter and status, with the filter, row rule and site of a list and no page or reference read |

**Contract as built.** Every method of `IContentService` takes the type name first. On a save `expectedVersion` is a required `int`, so no caller skips it; on publish, discard and version restore it is optional, in `LocaleWriteOptions`, for a caller that wants to act only on what it reviewed.

- **Refusals.** A principal that lacks the permission on the type gets `ContentAccessDeniedException` (HTTP 401 or 403). An entry that does not exist, is in another site or of another type, or is refused by its row rule gives `EntryNotFoundException` (404), so a client cannot probe; a read returns null. Delete and restore need the operation on every locale of the entry: none reached is not found, some reached is denied. An unreadable type on a read stays the engine's `ValidationException` on `type`, the same as an unknown type.
- **System operations.** An operation with no ambient `OperationContext` throws. `Principal.System` is accepted only from `Origin.Job`, `Origin.Cli` and `Origin.Seed`, entered through `SystemOperation.Begin`; from `Api`, `Admin` or `Mcp` it throws.
- **Audit fields.** `created_by`, `updated_by`, `deleted_by` and `author_id` come from `OperationContext.AuditPrincipal` (4.12). Authorization reads `Principal` alone.
- **Row rule and grants after the write.** A create or update that puts the entry outside the row rule that allowed it is refused and rolled back. A field the client sent or a hook changed must be allowed by a grant whose row rule holds for the new entry.
- **Hooks.** A before hook that changes a field outside the principal's grants fails the operation (`FCMS1406`); a change to a `Hidden` or `ReadOnly` field is allowed, since that is what a hook fills.
- **Uniqueness counts the trash**, because the partial unique index does; only a purge frees a value.
- **Bulk.** `BulkAsync(type, BulkRequest)` runs the single call for each entry (`CreateAsync`, `PatchAsync`, `PublishAsync`, `UnpublishAsync`, `DeleteAsync`), each in its own transaction, so every entry gets its own permission check, hooks, validation, publish policy, version, events and activity row, and one that fails rolls back only itself. Its result per entry is `Succeeded`, `Failed` with a `BulkProblem` that holds the code, message and validation errors of the problem the single call would have answered, and the current entry for a version conflict (an entry the principal cannot reach has the same `FCMS3104` as a missing id), `Duplicate` for an id named again, or `NotRun` after cancellation, which is checked between entries. A request by filter needs a cap of at most `BulkRequest.MaxItems` (1000): the filter is read against the drafts, keeps only entries the principal may read and may also perform the operation on, and is read in full before the first write, so one over the cap is refused (`FCMS2207` on `cap`) with nothing written. The operation's row rule joins the read rule in the selection query, on the entries selected and not on their relation targets, so the database returns only entries that can be changed, and a filter that still reaches more than ten times the cap of entries the single check refuses is refused (`FCMS2207` on `filter`). An update by filter applies one merge patch, and a publish by filter publishes, at the version the filter read, so a draft changed since is a conflict; an unpublish or a delete by filter acts on what matched when the filter was read, and both can be undone. A failure that is none of the single call's refusals is logged and reported as `Failed` with `FCMS3199` and a message that names no cause, and the next entry runs. An entry that committed is `Succeeded` even when reading it back fails, which is logged. A value the operation does not use, such as a cap on a request by id, is refused rather than ignored, and a bulk call inside a transaction throws, since there a failed entry could undo the others.
- **One write at a time.** The service is scoped, and the state of the write in progress (its changes, savepoints and commits, which bulk reads to report each entry) belongs to the scope. A hook that calls the service runs in the flow of that write and joins it. A write or a bulk call from another flow while one runs throws `InvalidOperationException` rather than mixing the two, so concurrent operations each need a scope of their own. This answers item 7 of [#2869](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2869).

**Query model.** `ContentQuery { Filter, Sort[], Page, Cursor, Select[], Populate[], Status, Locale, Fallback, Site, RichTextFormat }`. The filter is an AST (`FieldFilter(path, op, value)`, `And`, `Or`, `Not`) with operators `eq ne in nin lt lte gt gte between contains startsWith null notNull`. The same AST is produced by the REST bracket parser, the GraphQL input types, the MCP `query` tool and the typed builder from the source generator.

REST grammar (decision 21):

```text
GET /api/delivery/v1/article
    ?filter[category][eq]=news
    &filter[author.name][contains]=amir
    &filter[or][0][title][startsWith]=A&filter[or][1][title][startsWith]=B
    &filter[sections][hasBlock]=hero
    &q=dotnet                      (needs a search index, 5.7)
    &sort=-publishedOn,title
    &page=2&pageSize=20            (or offset=20&limit=20, or cursor=...)
    &fields=title,slug,author.name
    &populate=author,tags          (depth 1 by default; populate=*&depth=2 up to the cap)
    &status=published              (draft requires a preview key or the draft scope)
    &locale=fr-CA&fallback=none
    &richText=html                 (json | html | markdown)
    &withTotal=false
```

**Compilation.** The AST compiles to LINQ over the property-bag entities. Relation paths join through `cms_reference` to the target type's row in the same state and locale (decision 44: no fallback-aware filtering). Block and JSON paths go through `IJsonSqlDialect`. Row rules from the evaluator are appended as an `And` node before compilation, so authorization and filtering are one query. `state` is inlined as a literal and every user value is bound as a parameter, by reading a member of a holder object, because a bare expression constant becomes a SQL literal. SQLite does not need the inlined `state`, but a literal matches the `WHERE state = 1` index predicate as written, and [#2711](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2711) checks the other providers.

**Population.** Default depth 1, cap configurable (default 3). Beyond the depth a relation is returned as `{ "id": "...", "type": "author" }`. A target the principal cannot read, by type or by row rule, is returned as null at any depth, or left out of a multiple relation. Cycles stop at the cap. Field selection applies to populated entries with dotted paths.

**Population of other targets, as built (B-67).** A reference whose target is no content type is resolved by the `IReferenceResolver` (SDK, `FluentCMS.Content`) that a plugin registers, scoped, for that target: a plugin field type stored as `Reference` targets its own name, and a rich text `media` node targets `media`. Core knows no target by name; a target named like a content type is that type's, and two resolvers of one target stop the host at start. A read gathers the references it populates at every level, after the relations are filled, and asks each resolver once, in the locale of the read and for the principal of the operation. A reference within the populate depth, named (`populate=hero`, `populate=author.avatar`), under `*` or under a populated relation, becomes the `ResolvedReference.Value` the resolver returns, which holds `id` and `type`, and one it leaves out, because the principal may not read it or it does not exist, is null or leaves its list. A populate path that names a block list or a repeated component (`populate=sections`) resolves the references of other targets inside it at every depth, and `*` does too; relations inside blocks stay references, as before, and a path cannot go further into one (`FCMS2208`). Without populate, beyond the depth, or without a resolver for its target, a reference stays `{ "id", "type" }` (Appendix A). `richText=html` and `markdown` ask the resolver of `media` once for every media node of the read, populated entries included, and the renderers write its `Url` as `src` and, for a node without `alt`, its `Text`, escaped; a node it leaves out has no `src`. A write asks the resolver which of the targets it adds, in fields, blocks, components and rich text, do not exist in the site, whoever may read them (`FindMissingAsync`), and refuses those with `FCMS2113` (`ValidationErrorCodes.UnknownReference`, 422) on the path of the field that holds them. Targets the entry already had are not asked again, so an entry whose block or rich text still holds an asset deleted with `force=true` (B-66) saves, and without a resolver nothing is checked.

**Reverse relations as built.** A reverse side is metadata only in v1, and no path of a query reads it. A filter, sort, `fields` or `populate` path that names one, on the queried type or through a relation (`filter[articles.title]` on an author, `populate=author.articles` on an article), is refused with `FCMS2210` (400), whose message names the reverse side and the relation field it mirrors. The code is given to a caller who can read that field, the one introspection shows the reverse side to; for any other caller the path is the unknown field it is to them (`FCMS2202`). The entries that point to an entry are a list of their own type filtered by the relation, such as `/article?filter[author][eq]={id}`. Reverse traversal stays additive: a later version can read it with one reference query by `target_id` per level, which `ix_cms_reference_target` serves, and the field and row filtering of population, so a path refused today can start to work.

**Search as built.** `ContentQuery.Search`, `q=` on the Delivery and Management lists, is a seam the search plugin fills, and core has no index. The engine checks the query as usual, then asks `ISearchIndex.SearchAsync` (4.13) with a `SearchRequest` of the type, the text, the locale (empty for a type that is not localized), the site (null for a global type) and the status, and narrows the query to the document ids it returns, bound as one parameter. The filter, row rule, site, locale, status, sort and page then apply to those entries as to any list, so the index cannot widen what a caller reads, and the total counts the matches the rest of the query lets through. The index returns at most `FluentCms:Query:MaxSearchResults` ids (1,000), its best matches, and the engine reads no more; the order is the query's sort, as relevance ranking is not part of the seam. A text that is blank or longer than 256 characters is refused (`FCMS2204`, `FCMS2207` on `q`). Core fills the slot with `NoSearchIndex`, so until the plugin or the host replaces it, a query with `q=` is refused with `FCMS2211` (400) before anything runs. A single-entry read and a bulk request refuse `q` as they refuse the other list parameters, and the OpenAPI document lists it on every list. The plugin adds search with `Providers.Use<ISearchIndex, T>()` and no core change.

**Pagination.** Offset and page with `total` (skip the count with `withTotal=false`). Keyset cursor: base64 of the sort key values plus the document id, which is always appended as the final sort key for stability. A v7 `document_id` makes that key stable, but it does not give creation order within one millisecond.

**Pagination as built.** A total is a second query on every list. The Management API counts unless `withTotal=false`, and Delivery counts only with `withTotal=true`, since a site rarely shows one. `CountAsync` runs the count alone. A page or an offset may skip at most `FluentCms:Query:MaxOffset` entries, 10,000 by default, which is a hundred pages of the largest size: the database reads and drops every skipped row, so a deeper page costs as much as reading that far, while a cursor seeks. A deeper one is refused with `FCMS2207` on `page` or `offset`, and its message points at cursors. A cursor is base64url JSON of the sort values and is neither opaque nor signed. That leaks nothing, since its values were in the page that gave it and decoding binds each one as a parameter, but a client sends it back as it came and does not read or build one, since its form may change; the API reference says so. This answers items 2, 3 and 9 of [#2869](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2869).

**Concurrency.** Each `(document, locale)` has an integer `version`. Writes carry `expectedVersion` (`If-Match: "7"` on REST). A mismatch is rejected with 412 and the current entry in the problem details `current` extension.

**Unique races, as built.** Each of these refusals comes from a check before the write: an existing singleton, a locale the entry already has, a taken `[Unique]` value, a taken site slug. The database enforces the same keys: the singleton key and the published side of `[Unique]` (4.3), `uq_cms_document_document_locale`, and `uq_cms_site_slug`. On SQLite the content service's transaction takes the write lock when it begins, so a second writer waits and its checks see the first. On PostgreSQL and SQL Server under READ COMMITTED, two writers can pass their checks together, and the second then breaks the key at its insert. `IDataProvider.IsUniqueViolation` recognises that error, in each provider package: SQLSTATE 23505 on PostgreSQL, errors 2601 and 2627 on SQL Server, and `SQLITE_CONSTRAINT_UNIQUE` or `SQLITE_CONSTRAINT_PRIMARYKEY` on SQLite (a primary key counts on the other two as well). `ContentService` runs a write that meets it once more, in a new transaction and on the context as it was before the first run (what that run left tracked is detached, and the caller's entries go back to the state and values they had), and its checks then see the winner, so a loser gets exactly what a writer that came second gets and never a 500:

- a second singleton: `FCMS2401` (409);
- a locale added twice, as with a stale `expectedVersion`: `VersionConflictException` (412, `FCMS3105`) with the current entry;
- a publish of a value another entry published first: `NotUnique` (`FCMS2111`, 422) on the field.

A deadlock victim runs again the same way, up to five times after a jittered pause, recognised by `IDataProvider.IsDeadlock` (error 1205 on SQL Server, SQLSTATE 40P01 on PostgreSQL, never on SQLite, whose writers queue). Under SQL Server's locking reads two updates of one entry could deadlock between the read and the write of their transactions; `READ_COMMITTED_SNAPSHOT` (4.3) takes the reads out of that, and the reruns stay for two writers that lock rows in different orders. The victim's next run sees the winner's version and gets `VersionConflictException`, as a sequential loser does ([#2711](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2711)). A write inside a transaction the caller began takes a savepoint; where there is none (SQL Server with MARS), a failed write rolls the caller's whole transaction back, and the transaction stays ended, so a write the caller goes on with, as a hook that caught the failure would, fails as its commit does instead of committing on its own.

A hook that ran before the failed insert runs again in the second run, inside its transaction, unless a check refuses first; what the first run raised is never delivered, since its events and outbox rows went with its rollback. A second unique violation in that run is a key no check covers, and it fails as it is. A write joined to a transaction the service began runs again with it; one inside a transaction the caller began fails with the database error, because only the owner can start over. The draft side of `[Unique]` has no index, so on PostgreSQL and SQL Server two drafts saved together may share a value; each is refused at publish while the other holds it, and an empty value, which drafts do not clash on, goes to whichever publish commits first. `SiteManager.CreateAsync` answers a slug taken between its check and its insert with the error of a taken slug (`FCMS2304` on `slug`). This settles decision 2 of [#2847](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2847): the database enforces the singleton, and a lost race answers as a sequential loser does. Only SQLite has run these paths; the race tests in `FluentCMS.Data.Tests` move to the provider matrix with [#2711](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2711).

**Round trips of a write, as built.** A save reads the entry's documents, its draft rows and references, and the entry as the principal's read shows it, checks each `[Unique]` value, advances the version, replaces the references, and saves the rows and the version in one save. That save writes over the rows the transaction read without reading them again, and has no savepoint of its own, since the service's transaction or savepoint already takes back a save that fails. A check of the entry the service has just found or written in its site does not look for it in that site again (`AccessTarget.Found`), so with no row rule the check before the write sends nothing, and the check after it is not made. The read the principal is shown is made from the documents, draft rows and references the save has just read, so it asks the database only which relation targets are readable; under a row rule on the read it reads the rows. The response reads the entry's rows again, and takes the references the save wrote and the targets the shown read found readable, unless another write ran in the scope since (`KnownEntry`). The reads inside a write use the locale its operation resolved. A read asks once per target type which relation targets, at the top level and nested, the principal may read. The versions of an entry are counted only once its draft version passes the cap, since versions are numbered by it. The entry is copied into JSON once before the hooks and once after each kind of hook that ran, and the version snapshot is the last copy. A save of a `Modified` locale also reads its published rows to decide its status. A save that changes a shared field reads the draft and published state of each other published locale (4.5, "Modified, as built"). Neither makes a new copy of the entry. `ContentRoundTripTests` holds a typical update to these numbers: on a non-localized type with three relations, blocks and a `[Unique]` slug, an editor with no row rule makes 12 round trips, counting a save as one, where it made 33 before [#2863](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2863) and 16 before [#2864](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2864), and copies the entry twice, where it copied it ten times.

**Validation pipeline.** Field type coercion and declarative rules, custom field validators, entry validators, uniqueness (both states, per site and locale), then, on publish, `Required`, `RequiredWhen` and reference integrity. On a draft the content service writes `''` and the empty rich text document for missing `NOT NULL` values (4.3), and publish treats `''` as missing.

**Hook pipeline.** `Before` hooks run inside the transaction in `[HookOrder]` order and may mutate the entry or cancel (`HookRejectedException`, mapped to 422). `After` hooks run inside the transaction after the write so plugin tables stay consistent. Events (4.11) are dispatched after commit. The pipeline resolves and orders the hooks of a kind once per scope and content class, not on every call, so a hook registered as transient lives as long as the scope (item 8 of [#2869](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2869)).

**Response shape.** Flat JSON, system fields at the top level under reserved names, relations inlined by depth:

```json
{
  "id": "0192f2a4-7b1c-7d2e-9f10-2a3b4c5d6e7f",
  "status": "published",
  "locale": "fr-CA",
  "version": 7,
  "createdAt": "2026-11-01T09:12:00Z",
  "updatedAt": "2026-11-01T10:40:00Z",
  "publishedAt": "2026-11-01T10:41:00Z",
  "title": "Bonjour",
  "slug": "bonjour",
  "author": { "id": "0192f2a4-...", "type": "author", "name": "Amir" },
  "tags": [{ "id": "0192f2a4-...", "type": "tag" }],
  "sections": [{ "type": "hero", "id": "V1StGXR8_Z5j", "heading": "..." }],
  "fallbacks": { "subtitle": "fr" }
}
```

Lists return `{ "items": [...], "total": 120, "page": 2, "pageSize": 20, "nextCursor": "..." }`. A nested `sys` object for system fields was considered and rejected (Q14).

**Other surfaces as built (B-75).** `IContentSurface` (`FluentCMS.Content`, in the SDK) is what a surface over content that is not REST asks, so that it answers as REST does and cannot drift from it. Core implements it, scoped, with what the REST APIs use: `DefaultPageSize`, `MaxPageSize` and `MaxPopulateDepth` from `FluentCms:Query`; `ReadableFieldsAsync(type, locale)`, the system and content fields of a type that the principal of the operation may read in its site, or null for a type it may not read or that does not exist, from the access answers the query engine asks; and `ToJson(entry)`, the response shape above. For the MCP tools (5.4, B-72) it also writes a list, a publish result and a schedule as the Management API answers them (`ToJson` of a `PagedResult`, a `PublishResult` and a `ScheduleInfo`), reads the parameters of a list (`filter[...]`, `sort`, `pageSize` and the rest) as a `ContentQuery` with `ParseQuery`, and the body of the schedule route with `ParseSchedule`, all through the code of the routes. For the `bulk` tool (5.4, B-74) it writes a `BulkResult` as the bulk route answers it, with the problem of each failed entry. The problem of an exception is `IProblemDescriber`'s (4.13). The GraphQL plugin (5.3) is its first caller.

### 4.5 Versioning, publishing, scheduling and trash

Rows 6.1 to 6.11; decisions 38, 39, 40, 41.

**States.** Per `(document, locale)`: `Draft` (never published), `Published` (draft equals published), `Modified` (draft content differs from published), `Unpublished` (was published, now withdrawn). Status is stored on `cms_document` and recomputed on every transition.

**Modified, as built.** Whether a published locale is `Published` or `Modified` depends only on its content: its draft fields, shared and localized, compared with its published fields, never on version numbers. Save, publish, discard and version restore decide it this way. So does the recompute of the other published locales that follows a change to the shared row. A save of a `Published` locale compares with its draft, which is what is published. A save of a `Modified` locale reads the published rows. A save that changes a shared field recomputes the other published locales. An edit that puts back the published value therefore gets the same status, `Published`, whether it comes from a save, a discard or a recompute.

**Transitions.**

| Operation | Effect | Validation | Event |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Save draft | upsert state 0 rows, `draft_version++`, write `cms_version` | draft rules | `EntryDraftSavedEvent` |
| Publish | copy state 0 rows to state 1, set `published_version`, `published_at` | full rules plus reference integrity | `EntryPublishedEvent` |
| Unpublish | delete state 1 rows, status `Unpublished` | permission | `EntryUnpublishedEvent` |
| Discard draft | copy state 1 rows to state 0, as a new version; needs `update` | permission | `EntryDraftDiscardedEvent` |
| Restore version | new draft from snapshot; needs `update` | draft rules | `EntryVersionRestoredEvent` |
| Trash | set `deleted_at`; excluded by the global query filter | permission | `EntryTrashedEvent` |
| Restore from trash | clear `deleted_at` | permission | `EntryRestoredEvent` |
| Purge | delete all rows; separate permission `content.{type}.purge` | | `EntryPurgedEvent` |
| Schedule | row in `cms_schedule`, job enqueued | | `EntryScheduledEvent` |

**Versions.** Every save writes an immutable snapshot (decision 38) with author, origin, client id and an optional message. A per-type cap (default 50, `[PublishPolicy(MaxVersions = n)]`) prunes oldest drafts when a version is written; versions that were published are never pruned, and only a publish marks one so, in `status_after`. Diff is computed on demand as a field-level structural diff (blocks and rich text compared as trees) and returned as a list of `{ path, kind, before, after }`. A list whose items all carry an `id` on both sides, as blocks do ([ADR 0002](../decisions/0002-stable-block-ids.md)), pairs them by id: an item kept in its order is compared at its index in the later version, and one that is new, gone or moved is added at its new index and removed at its old one, so a block inserted at the top is one change. Any other list, such as a repeated component, a multiple select or the content of rich text, pairs by position (item 4 of [#2869](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2869)). A snapshot holds hidden fields, so reads and diffs go through the reader's field limits as they are today, and need the Management scope as well as the draft read. Access to versions is decided on the entry's current row: history is part of the entry, so a reader the row rule lets in also sees versions from before the entry came within the rule. A restore is a write of the principal's own: what it may not write or read, and what is hidden or read-only, keeps the value the draft holds, except for the system principal. A principal that can write a field it cannot read may not discard or restore at all, whatever that field holds, because the status and version those answer with would check a saved guess against the value.

**Trash.** `DeleteAsync` sets `deleted_at` and `deleted_by` on every locale of the entry, and the global query filter on `cms_document` hides it from reads, queries, versions, publishing and reference checks; the content rows, references and versions stay as they were, so a restore brings back the entry as it was. `QueryTrashAsync` reads the trash as drafts through the query engine, in one locale, so the read grant, its row rule, its locale and field limits and the site hold; it needs `restore` or `purge` on the type and lists only what the principal may read. Every entry carries `deletedAt`, and `deletedBy` where the principal may read who changed an entry. The query filter is off for that whole query, so every row reached through a relation says explicitly that it is not in the trash: a trashed target never matches a filter or a row rule and is never populated. A filter on the id of a relation matches only a target the same read would show: of that type, not in the trash, in the site of a site-scoped type, and published on a published read. `PurgeAsync` needs `content.{type}.purge`, deletes the content rows of every state and locale, the references the entry holds and every version of it, published ones included, and raises `EntryPurgedEvent` per locale. It follows the rules of restore: an entry that is not in the trash, in another site or type, or refused by the row rule of the grant is `EntryNotFoundException`, the same for all of them; one the principal reaches in some locales only is `ContentAccessDeniedException`. References from other entries to a purged one are the values of their fields and stay; a read leaves out a target that is not there, and publishing warns with `FCMS2404` as it did for the trashed one. The values the entry held are free again, since uniqueness counted the trash.

**Retention.** `[PublishPolicy(TrashRetentionDays = n)]` (`PublishPolicy(trashRetentionDays: n)` on the builder, `trashRetentionDays` in schema JSON) sets how long a type keeps its trash; the default is 30 days, the least 1 and the most 36500. The job `content.trash.purge` runs daily at 03:17 UTC from a recurring schedule stored at every startup. Each run purges, per type and site, the entries trashed longer ago than the type keeps them, through `PurgeAsync` as `Principal.System` with `Origin.Job`, one transaction per entry, at most 500 purges per type per run (`Limit` in the payload). Due entries are read oldest first in pages (`PageSize`), so an entry that fails is logged and left for the next run without using up the limit or hiding those behind it, and a type that fails is logged while the others still run. The purge checks inside its transaction that the entry went to the trash before the cutoff, so one trashed again since is left alone. A run logs the number purged and failed. A run that finds nothing due does nothing, so it is safe to run again.

**Reference integrity.** On publish, references to unpublished or trashed entries produce warnings in the response (`warnings[]`) by default; `[PublishPolicy(UnpublishedReferences = Block)]` on a type turns them into a 422. A target counts as published when it has a published row in the entry's locale or its fallback chain. A self-reference is exempt. A cycle is not: under `Block` neither side can go first, and the way out is to publish one side without the reference and set it afterwards.

The answer must not be a way to ask about an entry the principal may not read, since a write takes any id:

| Target, for a principal other than the system principal | Warn | Block |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Published, and it may read it | nothing | publishes |
| Unpublished, and it may read it | `FCMS2403` with the id | `FCMS2403` on the field |
| Trashed, missing, in another site or of another type; refused by its row rule, asked of the locale that serves the target; or with rows only in locales its grant does not cover | `FCMS2404`, no id, all identical | `FCMS2404` on the field |
| Of a type it may not read, or of no type at all | nothing | always one refusal, no path |
| In a field it can write but not read, which is where it chooses the id | nothing | always one refusal, no path |
| In a field it can neither read nor write, or hidden: it did not choose the id | nothing | a refusal with no path, only if the target really is a problem |

The system principal is told the id on `FCMS2404`.

**Reference checks, as built.** Publishing, scheduling, a diff and a version restore ask about an entry's targets per type, not per reference: one query reads the rows of every target of a type, and one for each locale that serves them asks the row rule (`IAuthorizationEvaluator.AccessibleEntriesAsync`), as a read does (4.4). A diff asks about the targets of both versions at once. `ContentRoundTripTests` checks that 2 and 20 references cost the same number of queries, with and without a row rule on the target type.

**Relation targets on a read.** The same rules hold for a top-level relation, a relation inside a block or component, an entry link inside rich text, and a relation in the `data` of an inline block, which also count in `cms_reference` and reference integrity. A target the principal may not read by type or row rule, in another site, of another type than the field's, or with rows only in locales its grants do not cover, is returned as null or left out of a list. A row rule is asked only of the locale being read, so where that locale has no rule, only grants with no rule count as covering a locale: a rule on another locale's grant cannot vouch for a row there, and such a target is left out even if the principal could read it directly in that locale. A rich text link keeps its text and loses its target, and a link to an unknown type is treated as one to an unreadable type. A published read shows only what is published: a target that is trashed or has no published row in any locale goes too, and one published only in another locale stays `{ id, type }`, since a reader with a fallback can follow it. A draft read keeps a trashed target of a type the principal may read as a reference, because that is what its author attached. A filter on the relation still matches a target left out: it answers about the source entry and a value the caller named.

A write answers what a read showed, so what the read left out stays: a nested target goes back in its block, and a rich text document keeps its links when it comes back as it was read. Rich text nodes have no ids, so an edited document loses the links the principal could not see. Keeping must not test whether the target exists, or a later warning would say so: a principal keeps every target its read left out, whatever the id is. Only one whose read grant on that type has no row rule and no locale limit, which a read tells anyway, loses a target that does not exist in that type and site, trashed counting as existing. So a limited principal cannot clear a ghost id, and under `Block` the entry stays refused until a wider principal does. The system principal keeps nothing it did not send.

**Read-only values sent back.** A full update may send back a top-level field the principal can see but not change: it is no write while it is the same as the read showed. The same holds inside a component, a block, the `data` of an inline block and an inline block inside a block, on an update and a patch alike, since such a field is sent whole: a read-only value that is the same as the read showed in the stored item it pairs with is taken out of the write, as if the client had left it out, and the stored value stays. A single component is its own pair. The items of a block list or a repeated component, and the inline blocks of a document, pair as the write keeps their hidden values ([ADR 0002](../decisions/0002-stable-block-ids.md)). A block or an inline block sent with its `id` pairs with the stored block of the same id and type anywhere in the same top-level field, so it may move into or out of a nested list or the document of another inline block. One whose id is not stored is new and pairs with none. An item without an id, as every item of a repeated component is, pairs within its list or document among the stored items whose ids the write does not send: first one of the same type with the same visible content, then the next one of that type in order. A block then takes the id of its pair. So a read-only value that moves with its block, or stays on a block whose neighbour was added or removed, is no change, and one that is not the value of the stored item its item pairs with is refused (`FCMS1406`): kept in place while the content around it moved, sent on an added item that pairs with none, set where its pair has none, or sent as null where its pair has one. A client that edits blocks may leave the read-only values out, and they follow their blocks as hidden values do. The comparison is with what the read showed, so the answer tells the principal nothing it could not read, and a value that names a hidden field is never the same. Only a field the principal may write is looked into, and a new locale compares only shared fields, since a localizable field has nothing in that locale to show. A client that drops ids still gets a guess. A block moved and edited without its id, or a new block without one in a write that removes a block of its type, may take the read-only and hidden values of another block, whether or not they are sent. A client that wants a block treated as new sends an id of its own.

**Shared fields.** Non-localizable fields live on one row per state, so publishing one locale publishes them for every published locale. A grant limited to some locales may publish only when those fields have not changed, or no other locale is published. The other locales' status is recomputed after a transition that moves the shared row. So `published_version` names the version a locale last published, and what that locale serves can differ from that snapshot in its shared fields.

**Scheduling.** `cms_schedule (id, document_id, locale, action, run_at_utc, time_zone, status, job_id, created_by)`. The job runner executes `ScheduledPublishJob` and `ScheduledUnpublishJob`; `cms_document` mirrors the next scheduled times for list views. Cancel and reschedule update the row and the job.

**Schedules as built.** `ScheduleAsync(type, id, ScheduleRequest, LocaleOptions?)` and `CancelScheduleAsync(type, id, action, LocaleOptions?)`, per locale and action. A request is an instant, `ScheduleRequest.At(action, DateTimeOffset)`, or a wall-clock time with an IANA zone, `ScheduleRequest.AtLocalTime(action, DateTime, timeZone)`, whose `DateTime.Kind` is ignored; a Windows zone id is refused (`FCMS2108` on `timeZone`), and the zone's rules on that day give the instant, a time the clocks skip is refused (`FCMS2408`), a time they repeat runs the first time, and a time not in the future is refused (`FCMS2407`). `cms_schedule` also holds `version` (the draft version when scheduled), `created_at`, the `claims` of the scheduling principal, `completed_at` and `error`. `status` is `pending`, `executed`, `cancelled`, `skipped` or `failed`; at most one row is pending per document, locale and action, so a reschedule moves it, and the others stay as history. `cms_document.schedule_publish_at` and `schedule_unpublish_at` mirror the pending times of that locale.

The schedule and its `cms_job` row are written in the transaction of the call, so there is never one without the other, and cancel and reschedule remove the job if it has not been claimed. The row is what counts: a job runs only if its schedule is pending and names that job, and it claims the schedule with a guarded update, so a cancelled or moved schedule is never run by the job it left behind, and two workers run it once. A schedule needs the permission, row rule, locale, site and field limits of doing it now, and for a publish under `Block` the reference rules of the principal, with a target that is not published yet let through, since it may be by then. An entry the principal cannot reach, or a request that is wrong, answers the same whatever the id, and cancel does not tell a hidden entry with a schedule from one without.

The jobs `content.schedule.publish` and `content.schedule.unpublish` run as the system principal with the scheduler as `OperationContext.Actor`, so versions, events and the activity log name that user and the origin is `Job`. Before it acts the job reads the scheduler's claims again, looks up the grants of its roles as they are now, and applies the checks of a schedule in full, the reference rules included, so a role that has lost the permission, or been deleted, publishes nothing. A refusal is final: the schedule is `failed` with the codes and paths of the refusal, and the job succeeds. Any other failure is retried by the runner, and recorded as `failed` when the last attempt is used, unless the attempt was stopped by the job's own token. A job the runner dead-letters some other way (an attempt that ran out of time on the last try, a worker that died) is heard through `JobDeadLetteredEvent` by an in-process handler, which closes its schedule as `failed` and clears the mirror (an outbox handler would give every host an outbox; a crash between the settling of the job and the event leaves the schedule pending and its job in the dead-letter list). A `failed` or `skipped` schedule is final: bringing its job back from the dead-letter list finds the schedule closed and does nothing, and the entry must be scheduled again. An entry in the trash is `skipped` (one restored before it is due still runs), a purge removes its schedules and their jobs, and a publish of an entry that is already published or an unpublish of one that is not is `skipped`. A scheduled publish publishes the draft version that was scheduled (`version`), not whatever the draft has become: if the draft has been saved since, or a field shared by every locale has (`shared_hash` holds a hash of those fields when a publish is scheduled, since a save through another locale leaves this locale's version as it was), the schedule is `skipped` with `FCMS2409`, the mirror is cleared and nothing is published, so an editor who may only update cannot have text published under another's name; the entry is scheduled again to publish the new draft. The write that moves the draft does this at once rather than leave a publish that will be skipped: an update, a patch, a discard or a version restore ends a pending scheduled publish of its locale, and that of another locale when it changed the shared fields, as `skipped` with `FCMS2409`, removes its job and clears the mirror in its own transaction, and raises `EntryScheduleCancelledEvent`, so the activity log has a `content.schedule_cancelled` row naming the editor. A write that fails leaves the schedule as it was (item 1 of [#2869](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2869)). An edit to another locale's own fields does not stop it, and the publish passes the scheduled version as `expectedVersion`, so a draft saved after the check fails the attempt with a conflict, which the runner retries and the next attempt skips. An unpublish does not depend on the draft. Cancelling a schedule needs the checks that creating it needed (the grant's field limits as well), after the same reach as any call, so it tells nothing of an entry the principal cannot read. An admin user's roles are read from `cms_user_role` as they are when the job runs, and a deactivated user holds none; the claims kept at scheduling still give the scopes and the claims row rules read, and the roles of a principal that is not a stored user, such as an API key until keys are stored.

**Agent-safe publishing.** Nothing in core treats `Origin.Mcp` differently: publish needs `content.{type}.publish` for everyone. The safety comes from the MCP plugin exposing publish as a separate tool and from roles given to agent principals. Origin is recorded on every version and activity row, so agent writes are filterable.

### 4.6 Localization and sites in core

Rows 7.1 to 7.6, 7.8, 7.10 to 7.12 (model and API parts); decisions 42 to 45.

**Tables.** `cms_site (id, name, slug, default_locale, is_default, created_at)`, `cms_site_locale (site_id, code, position, fallbacks json, enabled)`. The first system migration seeds the default site with locale `en`.

**Locale resolution.** `locale` parameter or `X-Cms-Locale` header, else the site's default. `locale=all` returns every locale of an entry as a map. No `Accept-Language` negotiation, to keep Delivery responses cacheable by URL.

**Fallback.** Per site an ordered chain (`fr-CA` to `fr` to `en`), applied per field on read; `fallbacks` in the response names the locale that served each fallen-back field; `fallback=none` disables. Lists filter on the requested locale only (decision 44).

**Sites.** `site_id` is on `cms_document`, `cms_*` content tables, roles, API keys, settings and activity rows. Resolution order: API key binding, `X-Cms-Site` header, `site` parameter, hostname (multi-site plugin), default site. Content types are site-scoped by default; `[Collection("tag", Scope = ContentScope.Global)]` makes one entry set shared by all sites (`site_id` null) and allowed as a relation target from any site.

**Management endpoints.** Sites and locales CRUD live in core (`/api/management/v1/sites`, `/api/management/v1/sites/{id}/locales`) because the data model is core; the multi-site plugin adds hostnames and anything beyond the default site (Q6a).

**Site and locale caching as built.** Every request resolves its site and locale before its own work, so `SiteCache` keeps, on each instance, the default site, the site of each slug, that a site exists, and each site's locales (its default and its `cms_site_locale` rows), for 30 seconds (`SiteCache.CacheDuration`). A write through `SiteManager` (creating or deleting a site, a site's name or default locale, adding, changing or removing a locale, `EnsureLocaleAsync`) drops all of it at once, and so does a `SiteUpdatedEvent` raised on the instance, which is how a plugin that changes a site, such as the multi-site plugin, does the same; a read that was under way during the write is not kept. What is not found is never kept, so a site that another instance created is found on the next request. No signal reaches another instance (an outbox delivery runs on one instance only), so another instance sees a change to an existing site or its locales when its copy expires, 30 seconds at the most, as plugin enablement (4.13) and settings already do. This answers [#2857](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2857) of the review of 7 Nov 2026 ([#2847](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2847)); the round trips it saves are in 4.10.

**Locale changes as built.** A site's locales decide its fallbacks, so adding a locale, changing its position, whether it is enabled or its fallbacks, and removing it raise `SiteUpdatedEvent` after the commit, as a change to the site's name or default locale does, whether through the Management API or `EnsureLocaleAsync`, which the host and the seeder call. Its outbox handler evicts the site's Delivery copies and raises `CacheInvalidatedEvent` (4.10), and a webhook on `site.updated` sees the change. A seeder may add a locale while other instances serve the site, so `EnsureLocaleAsync` raises the event too. A `PATCH` that leaves the locale as it was raises nothing and writes no row, and neither does `EnsureLocaleAsync` for a locale the site has. The activity log has the `site.locale-added`, `site.locale-updated` or `site.locale-removed` row, which names the locale, beside the event's `site.updated` row. This answers [#2932](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2932).

**Sites beyond the default as built (B-80).** `ISiteAdministration` (`FluentCMS.Sites`, in the SDK) is how a plugin reaches sites, since it references no table of core (3.4). Core implements it over `SiteManager`: `FindAsync(site)` reads a site as `SiteInfo` (the record the Management API answers, now in the SDK), `CreateAsync(name, slug, defaultLocale)` creates one with its default locale and its Public role, and `DeleteAsync(site)` deletes one, with the refusals core has (`FCMS2301` to `FCMS2304`, `FCMS2311`; a name is 1 to 128 characters). Each call needs `sites.manage` for the instance, which a role of one site or a grant limited to sites does not hold, and runs inside an operation. A create raises `SiteCreatedEvent` with the new site as its source, and a delete the new `SiteDeletedEvent` with no site as its source, since no one could read the log of a site that is gone; their activity rows are `site.created` and `site.deleted`. The multi-site plugin maps the routes (5.6). The outbox delivery of `SiteDeletedEvent` evicts the site's Delivery copies (`cache.site.deleted`), as `SiteUpdatedEvent` does, and each first-party plugin removes what it keeps for the site (5.6, "Deleted sites as built").

**Host names as built (B-80).** The order is as above: API key binding, `X-Cms-Site`, `site`, host name, default site. A key bound to several sites that names none reads the site of the host name when that is among them (4.10). A host name that no site has reads the default site, and so does one whose site no longer exists: a key, a header or a parameter that names no site is refused (`FCMS2301`), but a resolver may still hold the host of a site that was deleted, so that is not an error. `GET /api/management/v1/me/sites` lists the sites in which the caller holds a grant, the default site first, as `{ "id", "name", "slug", "defaultLocale", "isDefault" }`, so the admin shows a user only its sites (row 7.13). A grant counts when its role is the site's or the instance's and its own site limit, the binding of a key and the role of an MCP client reach the site, whatever it permits there, so a user with roles in two sites reads both without `sites.manage`.

### 4.7 Identity and the authorization server

Rows 4.1, 4.2, 4.9 to 4.11, 4.13, 4.15, 4.16, 2.12, 2.13; decisions 7, 30, 33.

**Foundation.** ASP.NET Core Identity with `CmsUser` and `CmsRole` (GUID keys) in `cms_user*` and `cms_role*` tables, and OpenIddict server with its stores in `cms_oidc_*`.

**Endpoints.** OpenIddict defaults: `/connect/authorize`, `/connect/token`, `/connect/userinfo`, `/connect/introspect`, `/connect/revoke`, `/connect/logout`, `/.well-known/openid-configuration`. Flows: Authorization Code with PKCE for public clients (admin, MCP clients, CLI), rotating refresh tokens, client credentials for service accounts. Access tokens are JWTs signed with keys from a configured certificate or, when none is configured, a generated key persisted in `cms_signing_key` through Data Protection. Audiences: `fluentcms:management` for admins and keys, `fluentcms:members` for members.

**Authorization server as built.** OpenIddict 7 with its EF Core stores on the system model in `cms_oidc_application`, `cms_oidc_authorization`, `cms_oidc_scope` and `cms_oidc_token` (GUID keys, snake_case columns), and `cms_signing_key`. The endpoints are in the `Connect` route group, which takes the `auth` rate limit, the Management and the Members CORS origins and the security headers; discovery and the key set are at `/.well-known/openid-configuration` and `/.well-known/jwks` in a `WellKnown` group with the same origins. PKCE is required of every client, not only public ones, and only `S256` is offered. A refresh token rotates, and a used one is refused once OpenIddict's reuse leeway of 30 seconds is over, which lets two refreshes that cross, as two tabs of one application make, both succeed; a reuse after it revokes the session's tokens. A zero leeway was the first choice and was given up (#2854), since it signed users out on such a race. The token endpoint reads the user again for a code or a refresh token and refuses one who is deactivated or locked out, or whose security stamp, which codes and refresh tokens carry, has changed, so a password change or reset ends them. A bearer token is read from the `Authorization` header only. Access tokens are signed, not encrypted, JWTs with the audience `fluentcms:management`, and carry `sub`, `name`, `fcms_kind` (`AdminUser`, or `ServiceAccount` for client credentials, whose `sub` is the client's id), `scope`, `client_id` and, for a service account only, `roles`; a user's roles are read from `cms_user_role` on each request as before. `fluentcms:members` is the audience of a member's tokens, and the bearer scheme accepts both (members as built). Scopes are `openid`, `profile`, `email`, `offline_access`, `draft`, `management` and `mcp`, and narrow what roles allow as 4.8 says. The `FluentCms` scheme's bearer scheme is OpenIddict's validation scheme on the local server, which rewrites a valid token's principal to those claims and checks the token and its authorization in the database on each request, so a revoked session stops its access tokens at once. The request bridge sets `OperationContext.ClientId` from `client_id`, and a request from the client named by `cms.Clients.AdminClientId` (`fluentcms-admin` by default) runs with `Origin.Admin`. A refused credential keeps its handler's 401 code over HTTP (`FCMS3016` or `FCMS3019`), and `FCMS3102` otherwise. Tokens are signed with `FluentCms:Security:SigningCertificate` (a PKCS#12 file with an RSA key; `FCMS3022` when it cannot be read) or else with a generated RSA key kept in `cms_signing_key` and protected with Data Protection, which is what `ISigningKeyStatus` reports for `FCMS1102`; the symmetric key that encrypts codes and refresh tokens is always generated and kept there. The first key of each use has a fixed id, so instances that start together on an empty table insert one row and the others read it; the primary key settles that race with no wait, so it does not take `IDistributedLock` (4.15). The Data Protection key ring is in `cms_data_protection_key` under the application name `FluentCMS` unless the host persists it elsewhere. Clients are registered at start from `cms.Clients.Applications`, bound from `FluentCms:Clients:Applications:{clientId}` (`FCMS3021` for a bad one): a client with redirect URIs uses the code flow and refresh tokens, one with a secret is confidential and may use client credentials with the instance-wide roles it names, a listed client is replaced by its options at each start, one the options registered and no longer list is deleted with its sessions and tokens and a warning, unless the options list none at all, and others are left alone. Consent is implicit for these clients unless one sets `RequireConsent` (see the hosted pages as built). The authorize endpoint challenges the Identity application cookie, whose login page is `/account/login`, and answers `login_required` to `prompt=none`; logout ends that cookie on the POST of the logout page, which carries an antiforgery token, or with a valid `id_token_hint` of the user the cookie belongs to; any other request, a cross-site POST or a POST with a hint but without the cookie included, goes to the logout page, which asks, and a request without the cookie only returns to the client, with no expiring cookie (#2854). `UserSessions` lists a user's valid authorizations that still hold a token in use as sessions and revokes one with its tokens, for the Management API (#2786), and the `oidc.prune` job removes unusable tokens and authorizations after 14 days. The Data Protection ring is stored unencrypted unless the host adds an encryptor, which `IDataProtectionStatus` reports, and Production refuses to start without one (`FCMS1106`, `UnencryptedKeyRingCheck`) unless `FluentCms:Security:AllowUnencryptedKeyRing` is true. A host adds it with Data Protection's own builder, `AddDataProtection().ProtectKeysWithCertificate(...)` or `ProtectKeysWithAzureKeyVault(...)` or any other `IXmlEncryptor`, which it reaches from `IServiceCollection` like any host, so Core has no pass-through for it. Export and backup packages must never include `cms_data_protection_key`, `cms_signing_key` or `cms_oidc_*`. Codes `FCMS3021` and `FCMS3022`.

**Protected resources as built** ([#2977](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2977)). A plugin's group mapped with `MapProtectedResource(prefix, options)` (`FluentCMS.Plugins.Abstractions`) is an OAuth protected resource of its own (RFC 9728), which is how the MCP plugin maps `/mcp` (5.4). The group gets what `MapGroup` gives (4.13), with four differences. Its operations enter with the origin the plugin names, `Api` or `Mcp`. It takes the Management rate limit and CORS policy, since it takes writes. No endpoint of it opts out of the signed-in caller with `AllowAnonymous()`. And a bearer token needs the scope the plugin names, one of the scopes that say which surface a token is for, which is `mcp` alone today. An API key holds no OAuth scope and is taken by its class, which the evaluator reads; a preview token carries the kind of the principal that minted it, a key's included, so it is told apart by its entry claim and refused as a token without the scope. A request without credentials is a 401 (`FCMS3102`) with `WWW-Authenticate: Bearer resource_metadata="{metadata URL}", scope="{scope}"`, one whose credentials are refused carries `error="invalid_token"` as well, and a token without the scope is a 403 (`FCMS3103`) with `Bearer error="insufficient_scope", scope="{scope}", resource_metadata="{metadata URL}"`. Both come before the per-site gate, as on any group of a plugin. Core serves the metadata, so a plugin still maps nothing under `/.well-known` (`FCMS1524`): `GET /.well-known/oauth-protected-resource{prefix}` answers `{ resource, authorization_servers, scopes_supported, bearer_methods_supported, resource_name }` to anyone, where `resource` is the public URL of the group, `authorization_servers` holds the issuer exactly as the discovery document names it, `scopes_supported` the scope of the resource and `bearer_methods_supported` `header`. `GET /.well-known/oauth-protected-resource`, the document a client tries when it does not know the path of the resource, answers the same while the host has one protected resource and 404 (`FCMS3104`) once it has several, since it could describe one of them only. The documents are endpoints of the `WellKnown` group, so they take its CORS origins and count as the `connect` surface, and they are not gated per site, since an anonymous caller learns nothing of where a plugin is off. The URLs are built from the public origin and the path base (4.15), and from the request when no origin is set, as the issuer is. Under a path base the documents are under it, where RFC 9728 would put the well-known segment before it; the challenge names the URL, which is what a client follows. The authorization server takes the `resource` parameter of RFC 8707 on the authorization request and on the token request when every value is the public URL of a protected resource of the host, compared as an absolute URI with the path as written, and refuses any other with `invalid_target`; like every refusal while the authorization request is checked, it is shown on the error page rather than sent to the client. OpenIddict's own check holds the resources in a list fixed in its options, which cannot name a URL that follows the request, so it is off (`DisableResourceValidation`) and a handler of core's stands in its place. The resource permissions of clients are off too (`IgnoreResourcePermissions`), because the parameter grants nothing: the audience of a token stays `fluentcms:management`, and the scope of the resource, which a client does need a permission for, is what holds a token to it. A token with the `mcp` scope alone manages through `Origin.Mcp` only (4.8) and is refused by the Management API (403). The consent page describes `mcp` as reading and changing content as an AI agent or tool, as far as the user's roles allow. A scope that is not one of those, an origin other than `Api` or `Mcp`, and a prefix mapped as a protected resource twice stop the host with `FCMS1527`; a prefix under a group of core or with a route parameter anywhere in it is `FCMS1524`. Code `FCMS1527`.

**Dynamic client registration as built** ([#2978](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2978)). `POST /connect/register` (RFC 7591) is an endpoint of the `Connect` group, so it takes the `auth` rate limit, and core serves it only while a host has an `IMcpClientRegistrationPolicy` (`FluentCMS.Agents`, in the SDK), which the MCP plugin implements from its settings (5.4): without one, or while its `Mode` is `Off`, the endpoint is a 404 problem (`FCMS3104`) and the discovery document does not name it; otherwise the document carries `registration_endpoint`, built from the public origin as its other endpoints are. RFC 8414's `/.well-known/oauth-authorization-server` is not served, since the protected resource metadata names the issuer and a client reads the OpenID discovery document from it. Anyone may call the endpoint, so a registration is held to what a public MCP client needs and everything else it sends is ignored and not echoed. The body is a JSON object of at most 16 KB. `redirect_uris` is 1 to 10 absolute URIs of at most 2048 characters, each `https`, or `http` on `localhost`, `127.0.0.1` or `[::1]`, with no wildcard, fragment or user information, compared exactly at the authorize endpoint, the port included (`invalid_redirect_uri`). `token_endpoint_auth_method` is `none`; RFC 7591 reads a missing one as `client_secret_basic`, and since the server gives no secret a missing one is registered as `none`, which the answer says, and any other value is refused. `grant_types` holds `authorization_code` and may hold `refresh_token`, `response_types` holds `code` alone, `scope` holds only `mcp`, `openid`, `profile`, `email` and `offline_access`, so neither `management` nor `draft`, and `client_name` is 1 to 128 characters without a control character, `MCP client` when left out. A refusal of the metadata is a 400 with the `{ error, error_description }` body of RFC 7591 (`invalid_client_metadata` unless it is about a redirect URI), not a problem document. The answer is a 201 with `client_id` (`mcp-` and 32 random hex characters), `client_id_issued_at` and the metadata as registered, with `Cache-Control: no-store` and no secret; there is no registration access token and no management of a registration by its client (RFC 7592). The client is an OpenIddict application that is public, asks the user's consent (`ConsentTypes.Explicit`), requires PKCE and holds the permissions of the code flow, of the grants it asked for and of its scopes. Its state is one property of the application, `fcms_mcp`, a JSON object with `status` (`pending`, `approved` or `rejected`), `registeredAt`, `roleId`, `approvedBy` and `approvedAt`, so there is no migration; a property that cannot be read, or that says approved without a role, counts as pending. With the policy `Approval` a client is pending. With `Open` it is approved at once with `DefaultRole`, a role id or the name of an instance-wide role, and is pending while that is unset or names no role, since approving it without a role would give it the user's whole reach. `MaxPendingClients` (100) caps the clients that wait: a registration beyond it is a 503 with the error `temporarily_unavailable`, `Retry-After` and log event 9005, and the count is read before the insert, so registrations that cross may pass the cap by the few in flight. `RegistrationsPerHour` (10) is how many clients one client address registers in an hour under either policy, counted in memory on each instance after the metadata is accepted, so a refused registration spends nothing; one beyond it is the 429 problem of the rate limits (`FCMS3110`) with `Retry-After`. A client that is not approved gets nothing. The authorize endpoint refuses it before the user is asked to sign in, on the hosted error page with `client_pending_approval` or `client_rejected`, never at its redirect URI, which is as unvetted as the client; the consent page records no consent for it; and the token endpoint, which reads the client again for a code or a refresh token, refuses it with `unauthorized_client`. `IMcpClients` (`FluentCMS.Agents`), which core implements over the application manager, lists the clients that registered themselves with the newest token of each as its last activity, approves a pending one with a role, rejects a pending one, removes one with its authorizations and tokens, which the store deletes with the application, so that an access token is refused from the next request on (token entry validation, above), and removes those that are not approved, rejected ones included, and registered before a time. It checks no permission, as `IJobDeadLetters` checks none: the MCP plugin's service does. A change and its event commit together (`IdentityChanges`); two changes of one client at once are told apart by the application's concurrency token, and the second is answered as not pending. A client the options registered is not an MCP client and is neither listed nor changed. Registering, approving, rejecting and removing raise `McpClientRegisteredEvent`, `McpClientApprovedEvent`, `McpClientRejectedEvent` and `McpClientRevokedEvent` (`FluentCMS.Events`), which are the activity rows `mcp.client_registered` (the anonymous actor, `Origin.Mcp`, the new client as its client, the address in `ip`, and the `status` and `role` it got), `mcp.client_approved` (with `role` and `roleName`), `mcp.client_rejected` and `mcp.client_revoked`, of no site, with the target type `mcp_client` and the detail `clientId`. The job that removes stale clients raises none. The tokens of an approved client carry its role, which the evaluator holds them to (4.8, "Client roles as built"). Log event id 9005; no new codes.

**Sign-ins and sessions as built.** The follow-ups of the code review of 7 Nov 2026 (#2854). Lifetimes are `cms.Clients`, bound from `FluentCms:Clients`, and keep the libraries' defaults: `AccessTokenLifetime` (1 hour) and `RefreshTokenLifetime` (14 days, how long a refresh token lasts unused, since using one gives a new one) set OpenIddict's, `SignInCookieLifetime` (14 days, sliding) sets the `ExpireTimeSpan` of the Identity application cookie, and `AbsoluteSessionLifetime` (unset, no limit) is how long a session lasts after the sign-in however often it is refreshed. Each is more than zero and at most 365 days, and the access token lasts no longer than the refresh token (`FCMS3021`). `CmsSignInManager.SignInWithClaimsAsync` stores the time of the sign-in in the cookie's properties (`.fcms.signed_in_at`), which the cookie's renewals and the stamp check keep while they move its issue time; a cookie from before falls back to its issue time. The authorize endpoint signs the user out and challenges, as for a second factor, on `prompt=login`, when the sign-in is older than the request's `max_age`, and when it is older than `AbsoluteSessionLifetime`; with `prompt=none` it answers `login_required`. The request the login page returns with drops `login` from `prompt` and drops `max_age`, which the new sign-in answers, so the user is not asked again. The identity token carries `auth_time`. Codes and refresh tokens carry `fcms_session_start`, the time of the authorize request, and the token endpoint refuses a refresh past `AbsoluteSessionLifetime` with `invalid_grant`; a refresh token from before counts from its first refresh. A wrong recovery code counts towards the lockout of `cms.Users` and a locked-out user's code is refused unspent, as for the authenticator, since Identity's `TwoFactorRecoveryCodeSignInAsync` does neither. A password sign-in of a user who may not sign in, deactivated or locked out, runs the password hash before it is refused, as an unknown name does, so the time of the answer does not tell them apart. Token and authorization entry validation stay on for every bearer request: a cache keyed by token id would let a revoked token through until the cache expired, and instant revocation is worth the lookup; it can be revisited with the database work of #2857. Data Protection's hosted service is taken out by the full name of an internal type, and a unit test fails when the framework no longer has it.

**Hosted pages.** The Authorization Code flow needs server-rendered pages: login, logout, consent (third-party MCP clients), two-factor challenge and recovery codes, forgot and reset password, accept invitation, external login callback, error. They ship as Razor Pages in `FluentCMS.Core` with minimal CSS, brandable through settings (logo, colours, name) and overridable by the host. This is the one piece of UI in the backend.

**Hosted pages as built.** Razor Pages of the `FluentCms` area in `FluentCMS.Core`, which builds with the Razor SDK, at `/account/login`, `/account/logout`, `/account/consent`, `/account/two-factor`, `/account/recovery-code`, `/account/enable-two-factor`, `/account/forgot-password`, `/account/reset-password`, `/account/accept-invitation`, `/account/change-password`, `/account/external-login` and `/account/error`. `MapFluentCms` maps them with `MapRazorPages`, on the host's data source so that a host that maps its own pages gets each once, and gives the pages of the area, and only those, the `/account` conventions: the FluentCMS headers with `frame-ancestors`, the `auth` rate limit by client address (which the check of the path before authentication counts) and the `account` surface; the builder it returns passes a host's conventions on to them. No script runs on the pages: they send `Content-Security-Policy: default-src 'none'; style-src 'unsafe-inline'; img-src 'self' https:; base-uri 'none'` unless a page sets its own, and every form carries an antiforgery token, the logout form included, which posts to `/connect/logout` and is checked there (#2854). The page models are public (`FluentCMS.Core.Api.Account`), so a host overrides a page or the layout with its own at the same path, such as `Areas/FluentCms/Pages/Account/Login.cshtml`, on the same model. Branding is the settings area `branding` (`BrandingSettings`: `Name`, `LogoUrl`, `PrimaryColor`, `BackgroundColor`, `TextColor`), read from the instance defaults since the pages belong to no site; a colour is a hex colour and the logo an https URL or a path of the host, so neither can leave the style sheet or the markup. Login takes a user name or an email address, signs in `IdentityConstants.ApplicationScheme` with lockout, gives every refusal the same answer, and returns to a local `ReturnUrl` only, which is the authorize request the endpoint challenged from. The authorize endpoint sends a user who must change the password to `/account/change-password` (`interaction_required` for `prompt=none`), and, for a client with `RequireConsent`, a user who has not allowed it the scopes it asks to `/account/consent` (`consent_required` for `prompt=none`): consent is a permanent OpenIddict authorization that the client's later codes are issued under, so ending that session ends the consent, and refusing returns `access_denied` to the client. A logout without the logout page's token or the session's hint goes to the logout page, which asks and posts the request back; the server then returns to the client's post-logout URI or to the logout page. An error of authorize or logout that cannot go back to the client, such as an unknown client or redirect URI, goes to the error page with the server's code and description (OpenIddict's error passthrough). An exception on a page answers with a plain HTML page that names its `FCMS` code, written without the layout or the database, instead of a problem document. A reset link goes through `IEmailSender` with a token of Identity's Data Protection provider that carries the security stamp, which the reset changes, so it works once, and expires after `cms.Users.PasswordResetLifetime` (an hour); an unknown or deactivated address, or a send that fails (event 9004), gets the same answer and no email.
