The page only enqueues a `users.send_password_reset` job on the `mail` queue with the address and the link, whatever the address, and the job finds the user and sends, so the time of the answer does not depend on the address either (#3002). The link, like an invitation's, is built from the public origin when one is set, and otherwise from the request's scheme, host and path base, as the issuer is, which Production allows only with `AllowRequestOrigin` (public origin as built, 4.15). An invitation is accepted on `/account/accept-invitation` with the token of its link, with a password or through a provider (linking as built). The two-factor pages check an authenticator code, with Identity's authenticator provider, or a recovery code; enrolment and enforcement per role are in two-factor as built. The external login page signs in a user linked to the external login, creates one through a provider of `cms.Sso` (admin SSO as built), and otherwise says so. Every step of a sign-in raises `LoginSucceededEvent` or `LoginFailedEvent` from `CmsSignInManager`, so a host's own pages raise them too, with the step (`LoginMethods`), the reason of a refusal (`LoginFailureReasons`) and the client address; a refusal that matched no user keeps no name. They are the activity rows `user.login_succeeded`, whose actor is the user, and `user.login_failed`, whose actor is the anonymous principal, of no site, with the `Admin` origin and the address in `ip`. No new codes.

**Admin users.** Invitation by email (`cms_invitation` with hashed token and expiry), password policy from options, lockout, forced reset flag, deactivate, session list from OpenIddict authorizations with revoke. Password hashing uses Identity's PBKDF2 hasher behind the replaceable `IPasswordHasher<CmsUser>`; Argon2id was considered and deferred to an optional package if demand appears (Q3, which amends row 4.2 of the specification).

**Admin users as built.** `CmsUser` in `cms_user`, on Identity's EF stores for `cms_user_claim`, `cms_user_login` and `cms_user_token`. There is no `CmsRole`: a user holds roles of `cms_role` through `cms_user_role`, by id through `CmsUserManager` (instance-wide or of a site), or by name through Identity among the instance-wide roles only, since a name is unique only within its site. Roles stay with the authorization model, so there is no role store and no role claims, and no `cms_role_claim`. Password policy and lockout come from `cms.Users`, bound from `FluentCms:Users` (`FCMS3001` names a bad key), which keeps Identity's defaults with a minimum of 12 characters; an email address belongs to one user. A user is deactivated (`deactivated_at` and a new security stamp), never deleted: it cannot sign in (`CanSignInAsync`) and holds no roles. A password change clears `must_change_password`. An invitation is 32 random bytes sent through `IEmailSender` and stored as SHA-256; it lasts 7 days by default (`cms.Users.InvitationLifetime`), is used once, ends when a new one goes to the same address or when the user who sent it is deactivated, and becomes the user with its roles in one transaction. Whether the sender may give those roles is checked by `InvitationService` (4.10). `IEmailSender` defaults to a sender that writes to the log in Development and refuses to send anywhere else, unless `FluentCms:Email:Smtp:Host` is set: then every environment sends through SMTP with MailKit (`EmailOptions`: `Host`, `Port` 587, `Security` `StartTls` by default, `SslOnConnect` or `None`, `UserName`, `Password` and `From`, which must parse as a mailbox; `FCMS1101` otherwise), a connection per message. User created, updated (with the names of what changed, `UserChanges`; a rehash at sign-in is none), deactivated and role changed are events staged in the outbox with the change, and `user.*` activity rows of no site. Codes `FCMS3001` to `FCMS3009`.

**The last Super Admin as built.** At least one active user always holds `*` with no constraints through an instance-wide role, which is what Super Admin holds, so that the instance can be administered without editing the database (#2850). The code review of 7 Nov 2026 asked whether this was intended or whether lockout recovery belonged to the command line tool and the system principal; it is intended (#2847, decision 5). `SuperAdminGuard` (`FluentCMS.Core.Authorization`) refuses a change that would leave no such user with `FCMS3052` (409), whoever asks, a Management API key or a service account included: deactivating a user and taking a role from it (`UserAdministration`), the roles that follow a provider's groups (admin SSO as built), and a role edit that drops the grant (4.8). The change and its events roll back, and the refused attempt is the activity row `user.change_refused` or `role.change_refused` of no site, with the `change` (`deactivate`, `remove_role`, `follow_groups` or `update`) and the `code`. Each of these changes takes the `IDistributedLock` named `fluentcms:super-admins` (4.15) before it opens a transaction, waiting up to 45 seconds. A change of a user counts the holders before and after its write inside its identity transaction, and a role edit counts, before it saves, whether anyone keeps the grant through another role. So two changes at once, on one instance or on several, run one after the other on every provider, not only on SQLite, which serialises writers anyway, and the second finds no other holder. A change that adds a holder takes no lock. An invitation can carry Super Admin (4.10), and since it only adds a holder it is unchanged. Users are not deleted (`FCMS3007`), and a role is deleted only while no user holds it (4.10), so neither can take the grant away. `CmsUserManager` does not check, so host code that calls it directly keeps a holder itself.

**Two-factor.** TOTP through Identity's authenticator support, recovery codes, enforcement per role: a role flagged `RequireTwoFactor` makes the authorize endpoint refuse a session without an `mfa` authentication method reference.

**Two-factor as built.** `TwoFactorService` (`FluentCMS.Core.Identity`) runs it on Identity's authenticator provider (RFC 6238: SHA-1, 30-second steps, six digits), which keeps the key and the recovery codes in `cms_user_token`, unhashed, as Identity does. A user enrols a key, which waits until a code of it turns two-factor on; setting a waiting key keeps the security stamp, so the user's sessions go on, and a new enrolment replaces it. Turning two-factor on returns ten recovery codes, shown once, and gives a new security stamp, which ends the user's codes and refresh tokens, so the next sign-in asks for the second factor. A recovery code signs in once and is then spent. Replacing the recovery codes needs an authenticator code, and turning two-factor off needs an authenticator or a recovery code, so a stolen access token alone cannot undo the second factor; turning it off removes the key and the codes and gives a new stamp. A wrong code counts as a failed sign-in towards the lockout of `cms.Users`. Two-factor is on when the flag is set and a key exists, which is when Identity's sign-in asks for it; the flag alone counts as off. `cms_role.require_two_factor` is the flag, `requireTwoFactor` on the `/roles` routes (4.10). A user who holds a flagged role, instance-wide or of a site, needs the authentication method reference `mfa` on the Identity cookie, which Identity writes after the authenticator or a recovery code, and `pwd` after a password alone. Without it, the authorize endpoint signs out a user who has two-factor and challenges again (`login_required` for `prompt=none`), and sends one who has none to `/account/enable-two-factor` (`interaction_required` for `prompt=none`). That page enrols a key, turns two-factor on with a code of it, shows the recovery codes once and signs the cookie in again with `mfa`, since the user has just given both factors. The check comes before the forced password change, and the token endpoint refuses a code or refresh token without `mfa` of such a user (`invalid_grant`), so a session from before the flag ends at its next refresh. **Remembered devices.** The two-factor page's "Do not ask again on this device" (Identity's `TwoFactorRememberMe` cookie) skips the second factor and signs in with `pwd`, so a remembered device does not satisfy `RequireTwoFactor`: `CmsSignInManager.IsTwoFactorClientRememberedAsync` answers false for a user who holds a flagged role, who is therefore asked for the second factor at every sign-in, and the page does not offer that user the option. `amr` stays accurate this way. An external sign-in carries no `amr`, so a flagged role asks for the local second factor after it too, unless its provider is trusted for its own `mfa` (admin SSO as built). Identity's stamp check rebuilds the cookie's principal every half hour, and keeps `amr` through `SecurityStampValidatorOptions.OnRefreshingPrincipal`. The authorize endpoint copies `amr` from the cookie into the code, and the code and refresh tokens carry it into the access and identity tokens. **Resetting another user's two-factor.** A user who lost the app and every recovery code, or whose role requires two-factor and who cannot finish enrolling, is reset by an administrator with `POST /users/{id}/two-factor/reset` (4.10, #2851). `UserAdministration.ResetTwoFactorAsync` needs `users.manage` for the instance and every grant of the user's roles, as any change of a user does (`FCMS3103`), and a caller that is a user whose access token carries the `amr` `mfa` (`FCMS3064`, 403). A Management API key or a service account has no second factor and is refused, and so is a user signed in with a password alone. The request principal carries the token's `amr` values as the claim `amr` for this check. A user does not reset its own two-factor (`FCMS3049`): it turns it off with a code through `/me/two-factor/disable`. A user with neither the flag nor a key has nothing to reset (`FCMS3061`). The reset removes the key, the recovery codes and the flag with a new security stamp in one save, which ends the user's codes, refresh tokens and cookie, and then revokes every session of the user through `UserSessions`, so its access tokens stop at once too. A role that requires two-factor then sends the user to `/account/enable-two-factor` at its next sign-in. The reset raises `UserTwoFactorResetEvent`, staged in the outbox with the change, which is the activity row `user.two_factor_reset` of no site with the administrator as its actor; the store's `user.updated` row names `twoFactor` too when the flag was on. Codes `FCMS3060` to `FCMS3064`.

**Admin SSO.** External OIDC providers (Entra ID, Okta, Keycloak and generic OIDC are the same handler with presets) configured in the host configuration and shown read-only in the admin in the first release (runtime editing is a Should). Federation happens on the hosted login page: auto-provisioning, default role, group claim to role mapping, optional local-login disable.

**Admin SSO as built.** `cms.Sso`, bound from `FluentCms:Sso` (`FCMS3070` names a bad key), lists the providers under `Providers:{name}`. A name is 1 to 64 letters, digits, `-` or `_`, and is the provider's authentication scheme, its login provider in `cms_user_login` and its callback path, `/account/sso/{name}`. A name is found whatever its case: `CmsUserStore` finds a login of a provider under the name as it is written and, failing that, under another case of it (other schemes match as written), so a change of case in configuration keeps the users linked ([#3010](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3010)), and a name that is another authentication scheme's in any case, added before the providers or after them, stops the host (`FCMS3070`). Every provider is ASP.NET Core's OpenID Connect handler, added to the authentication options when the host starts. `Preset` (`Oidc`, `EntraId`, `Okta` or `Keycloak`) only builds the authority and names the button: from `TenantId` (`https://login.microsoftonline.com/{tenant}/v2.0`; `common`, `organizations` and `consumers` are refused, since their tokens come from any tenant), from `Domain` and `AuthorizationServerId` (`https://{domain}`, or `https://{domain}/oauth2/{id}`), or from `BaseUrl` and `Realm` (`{base}/realms/{realm}`). `Authority` overrides it, and generic OIDC requires it. The handler runs the code flow with PKCE, with `ClientSecret` when one is set, asks for `openid profile email` and the `Scopes` listed, takes the answer as a form post, and signs into Identity's external cookie with the token's claims under their own names; a host tunes a provider with `services.Configure<OpenIdConnectOptions>(name, ...)`. The login page offers a button per provider, and the external login page asks `SsoSignIn`. A user linked to the login signs in. Someone with no user gets one when `AutoProvision` is on (the default), named and addressed by the `EmailClaim` (`email`), with no password, linked in `cms_user_login` and holding `DefaultRole` and the mapped roles, in one transaction, so the user created and role changed events are staged with it (event 9040). A role is named by its id or by the exact name of an instance-wide role; one that does not exist is not given (`FCMS3071`, event 9042). `RoleMappings` is a list of `Group` and `Role`, matched exactly against the values of `GroupsClaim` (`groups`). No group gives a role by its name, so Super Admin is given only by a mapping or a default role that names it. At every later sign-in of a user who is neither deactivated nor locked out, the mapped roles follow the token's groups: a mapped role whose group is gone is taken away, and the default role and the roles the provider does not map stay. A token with no groups claim at all takes the mapped roles away too, and logs event 9044, since Entra ID's group overage or a missing scope looks the same. Groups that would take Super Admin from its last active holder change no role and refuse the sign-in (`not_allowed`, event 9045, `user.change_refused` with `follow_groups`), since the provider no longer vouches for that user and the instance keeps its last Super Admin (admin users as built). A token without an email address, or that does not say `email_verified` is true while the provider's `RequireVerifiedEmail` is on (the default; off, only a false one is refused) (`FCMS3072`), or whose address belongs to a user not linked to the provider (`FCMS3073`), creates no user, and a creation that fails (`FCMS3074`, with Identity's codes only) leaves none, so an address is never taken over unless the provider links existing users (linking as built). Those refusals log event 9043 and raise `LoginFailedEvent` (`external`, `not_allowed`, no user). The provider's remote sign-out path, `/account/sso/{name}/signout`, signs out the Identity application cookie (`SignOutScheme`), so an IdP-initiated logout ends the admin session (#2854); the cookie holds no session id of the provider for the handler to match, so only a sign-in made through that provider, which the cookie's authentication method names and the stamp check keeps, is ended, and another is left as it is. A provider's error, or a callback that belongs to no sign-in started here, goes back to the login page as a failed sign-in (`invalid_credentials`, event 9041) rather than to an error page. **The provider's `amr`.** It counts only from a provider with `TrustMfaClaim`, which is off by default: a token of such a provider whose `amr` holds `mfa` signs in with `amr=mfa` and without the local second factor. Any other external sign-in carries no `amr`, so a role with `RequireTwoFactor` asks for the local second factor after the provider, or sends the user to `/account/enable-two-factor`, as two-factor as built says. `DisableLocalLogin`, which needs a provider, hides the password form and the forgotten-password link, and `CmsSignInManager` refuses every password sign-in (`not_allowed`). A user created by a provider has no password; with local login on, a reset link gives it one. With local login off, an invitation is accepted through a provider (linking as built). Providers come from configuration only: runtime editing and a read-only list in the admin are not built. Codes `FCMS3070` to `FCMS3074`.

**Linking external logins as built.** The page that accepts an invitation offers a button per provider of `cms.Sso` next to the password form, and with `DisableLocalLogin` the providers only, since `InvitationService` then refuses a password (`FCMS3093`). The provider comes back to that page, and `SsoSignIn` accepts the invitation with the external login when the address of the provider's `EmailClaim` is the invitation's, as Identity normalizes addresses, and the token does not say `email_verified` is false (`FCMS3072`); another address is refused (`FCMS3092`) and the invitation stays pending. The user is named by the invitation's address, has no password, holds the invitation's roles and is linked in the transaction that uses the invitation up; it then signs in as at any sign-in through the provider, so the mapped roles follow its groups and `TrustMfaClaim` and `RequireTwoFactor` apply as before, and the default role is not given. An error or a cancel at the provider returns to the invitation. `LinkExistingUsers` on a provider, off by default, links at its first sign-in through the provider the user whose address the token carries and who has no login of that provider, only when the token says `email_verified` is true, so a provider that sends no such claim links no one; a user who cannot sign in now is not linked, and without the option or the claim the address is refused as before. An invitation needs no explicit `email_verified` because its link already showed that the person reads the mailbox, and an existing user has no such proof. Linking and removing a login raise `UserLoginLinkedEvent` and `UserLoginUnlinkedEvent` from `CmsUserStore` with the change, the activity rows `user.login_linked` and `user.login_unlinked` with the provider. `GET /users/{id}/logins` (`users.manage`) and `GET /me/logins` list `{ "provider", "providerKey", "displayName" }`, and `DELETE /users/{id}/logins/{provider}` (with every grant of the user's roles too) and `DELETE /me/logins/{provider}` remove the user's logins of that provider (`FCMS3091` when it has none) with a new security stamp, which ends the user's sessions. The last way to sign in is not removed (`FCMS3090`, 409): a login counts while its provider is offered on the login page, and a password while local login is on. With a public origin (4.15), the `redirect_uri` a provider is given, and the code is redeemed with, is built from it in `OnRedirectToIdentityProvider`. Codes `FCMS3090` to `FCMS3093`; event ids 9060 to 9062.

**API keys.** `cms_api_key (id, name, class, key_prefix, key_hash, role_id, site_ids json, type_names json, expires_at, last_used_at, rate_limit, revoked_at, created_by)`. Classes: `delivery` (read published), `preview` (read drafts too), `management` (whatever the bound role allows). Format `fcms_dk_...`, `fcms_pk_...`, `fcms_mk_...` with 32 random bytes, shown once, stored as SHA-256. Sent as `Authorization: ApiKey <key>` or `X-Api-Key`. A dedicated authentication handler produces a principal with the role's claims and the key class as scope.

**API keys as built.** `cms_api_key` also has `created_at`. A key is stored with its SHA-256 and its first 16 characters, which find the row; the hash is compared in constant time. The key is returned once, by the create call, and no event, activity row or log holds it or its hash. A key is bound to one role, and to sites and content types when the lists are given; an empty list is refused (`FCMS3020`), since a key must not read it as every site. A role is not deleted while a key that is not revoked uses it (4.10). The `ApiKey` scheme reads `Authorization: ApiKey <key>` or `X-Api-Key` and puts these claims on the request: `sub` (key id), `name`, `fcms_kind` (`ApiKey`), `roles` (the role id), `scope` (the class), and `fcms_sites`, `fcms_types` and `fcms_rate_limit` when the key has them. A wrong, expired or revoked key fails authentication with `FCMS3016` alike, so the answer does not tell that a secret was ever a key, and so does a request that carries a key and any other credential, two keys included (`FCMS3019`). The reason stays on the server (#2854): each refusal logs event 9010 (`ApiKeyRefused`, Information) with its code (`FCMS3016`, `FCMS3017` expired, `FCMS3018` revoked or `FCMS3019`), the client address and the id of a key that was found, never the key, and adds one to the counter `fluentcms.api_keys.refused` by `fluentcms.code`. It writes no activity row: a database write per refused key would let a flood of keys write as fast as it is sent, and the rate limit before authentication already bounds it. The `FluentCms` scheme, which the FluentCMS endpoints authenticate with, takes a request with a key to the `ApiKey` scheme and any other to the bearer scheme the authorization server registers, and leaves it anonymous when a host has none. It never falls back to the host's default scheme, which may be a cookie and would open the APIs to cross-site requests, and a scheme that forwards back to it gets no result instead of recursing. `last_used_at` is written at most once every five minutes. The binding is enforced by the evaluator, not only over HTTP: a principal with `fcms_sites` reaches only operations in those sites, so it writes no global type, and one with `fcms_types` holds only the content permissions of those types; a claim that names nothing usable reaches nothing. The evaluator reads a key's role from `cms_api_key` on each request, so a revoked or expired key holds no role at once. A schedule made by a key checks the key as it is when the job runs, its whole binding read again, so a revoked, expired, deleted or rebound key publishes nothing and the schedule ends `failed`. Expiry can be brought forward, never extended, by a conditional update. Key created, key revoked and key expiry changed are events, staged in the outbox with the change, and `api_key.*` activity rows of no site. `SecretScrubber` masks an `ApiKey` credential and anything shaped like a key. Codes `FCMS3010` to `FCMS3020`.

**API key addresses and rotation as built** ([#2936](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2936)). `cms_api_key` also has `allowed_addresses json`, `previous_key_prefix`, `previous_key_hash` and `previous_key_expires_at`, indexed on `previous_key_prefix`. *Allowed addresses.* A key may name up to 64 IP addresses and CIDR ranges it is accepted from (`allowedAddresses`); left out or null it is accepted from any address, and an empty list is refused (`FCMS3020`) as for sites and types. An entry is an IPv4 address in its dotted form of four numbers, an IPv6 address, or either with a prefix (`203.0.113.0/24`, `2001:db8::/32`); a range with bits set past its prefix, an IPv4-mapped IPv6 entry, a zone and anything else are refused with `FCMS3024`, and the entries are stored in their plain form without duplicates. The handler checks the client address the host trusts, `Connection.RemoteIpAddress` after the forwarded headers as `FluentCms:ReverseProxy` configures them, once the key is found valid and active; an IPv4 client seen as IPv4-mapped IPv6 is matched as IPv4, and a request with no known address is refused. A refused address fails with `FCMS3016` like a wrong key, so a stolen key does not tell that it would work from elsewhere, and the log has `FCMS3023` (event 9010) with the address and the key's id. Since only a holder of a valid key reaches this check, it is the one refusal that writes an activity row: `ApiKeyAddressRefusedEvent`, the row `api_key.address_refused` of no site with the anonymous actor, the address in `ip` and the detail `code`, at most once per key and address and ten addresses per key every five minutes on each instance; a row that cannot be written is logged (event 9011) and the request is still a 401. `PUT /api-keys/{id}/allowed-addresses` replaces the list (null for any address); it needs what creating the key would, its role's grants included, since widening where a key is accepted is a decision like a new key's, and raises `ApiKeyAllowedAddressesChangedEvent` (`api_key.allowed_addresses_changed`, with the list). A job a key scheduled has no client address and is not held to the list. *Rotation.* `POST /api-keys/{id}/rotate` gives the key a new secret of its class and keeps its id, role, binding, expiry and list; the answer is the key with its `secret`, as on create, and no other answer has it. The secret it replaces moves to `previous_key_*` and is still accepted until `previous_key_expires_at`, `cms.ApiKeys.RotationOverlap` ahead (`FluentCms:ApiKeys:RotationOverlap`, 24 hours by default as for webhook secrets, from zero to 30 days, `FCMS3001` otherwise), or `{ "overlapSeconds" }` ahead when the caller asks for a shorter one; zero refuses it at once, and a longer overlap than the option's is refused with `FCMS3026`. A second rotation within the overlap replaces the previous secret with the one it replaces, so a key has at most two. The previous secret is found by its own prefix and compared in constant time like the current one, and once its overlap has passed it is a wrong key (`FCMS3016`); revoking or expiring the key refuses both. A revoked or expired key is not rotated (`FCMS3025`, 409), and the update is conditional on the secret that was read, so of two rotations at once the second gets `FCMS3025` instead of returning a secret that never worked. While the previous secret is accepted, the answers carry `previousKeyExpiresAt`. Rotating raises `ApiKeyRotatedEvent` (`api_key.rotated`, with `overlapEndsAt`), which, like the activity row and the log, holds neither secret nor its hash. Codes `FCMS3023` to `FCMS3026`.

**Preview tokens as built** ([#2934](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2934)). `POST /api/management/v1/content/{type}/{id}/preview-token` mints a short-lived token that reads the draft of that one entry on the Delivery API, so a front end that renders drafts holds no preview key. The body is optional: `{ "minutes", "locale" }`. `minutes` is from 1 to `FluentCms:PreviewTokens:MaximumLifetime` (one hour by default, at most a day) and `DefaultLifetime` (ten minutes) when left out, both whole minutes; another value is refused with `FCMS3027` (422), and options out of range stop the host with `FCMS3001`. `locale` binds the token to that one locale, and a type that is not localized takes none (`FCMS2108`). Minting needs the `management` scope and reads the draft first as the caller, in the named locale with no fallback when there is one, so a caller who may not read that draft, by its roles, a row rule or a binding, gets the 404 of the read and no token. The answer is `{ "token", "scheme": "Preview", "expiresAt", "type", "id", "site", "locale" }` with `Cache-Control: no-store`. The token is a JWT signed with the authorization server's signing key (RS256), with the audience `fluentcms:preview` and the header `typ` `fcms-preview+jwt`, so an access token is never read as one and it is never read as an access token. It carries the caller's `sub`, `name` and `fcms_kind`, the scope `preview` alone, `fcms_sites` (the request's site), `fcms_types` (the type), `fcms_entry` and, when bound, `fcms_locale`, and a service account's `roles` and a user's `amr` as the caller's token had them, so a role that requires two-factor still holds. It is sent as `Authorization: Preview <token>`, which the `FluentCms` scheme gives to the `Preview` handler; with any other credential it is refused (`FCMS3019`), a token that does not validate is `FCMS3029` and one past its `exp` is `FCMS3030`, both 401, checked against the `TimeProvider` with no clock skew. The principal is the caller with those claims: an admin user's and an API key's roles are read again on every request, so deactivating the user, revoking the key or taking the role away stops the token at once. The engine holds it to its binding whatever reads it: the site and type bindings of API keys, and the entry, which `EffectivePermissions` adds to the row rule as `id = {entry}`, so a populated relation or another surface, such as a plugin's, reaches no other entry. The locale is held by Delivery alone, since the values a locale falls back to are read in other locales. Without the `management` scope it writes nothing and mints nothing (403, 4.10). *Stateless.* Nothing is stored and there is no migration; a token cannot be revoked by itself, and signing out does not end it. Its lifetime cap bounds that, and the role and deactivation checks end it for a caller who loses access. A token store with revocation was left out, as a table for a credential that lasts minutes. A token minted by an API key with allowed addresses is not held to them, since it is meant for a browser elsewhere; it reads one draft for at most the cap. Codes `FCMS3027` to `FCMS3030`.

**Members.** A separate pool (decision 33): `cms_member*` tables with their own `UserManager<CmsMember>`, `cms_member_group` and membership table. REST endpoints under `/api/members/v1`: `register`, `verify-email`, `resend-verification`, `forgot-password`, `reset-password`, `me`. Tokens come from OpenIddict with the members audience; login runs through the hosted pages (code with PKCE) and, when `Members:AllowDirectLogin` is on, through a custom `member_password` grant for first-party apps (Q4). External providers for members: Google and Microsoft (in-box handlers), GitHub and Apple (Q10), generic OIDC (member providers as built). Member groups appear in row rules and public access.

**Members as built** ([#2992](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2992)). `CmsMember` in `cms_member (id, email, normalized_email, email_confirmed, password_hash, security_stamp, concurrency_stamp, display_name, lockout_end, access_failed_count, deactivated_at, created_at, last_login_at)`, on Identity's EF store for `cms_member_login` and `cms_member_token`, in the system stream. The pool is one for the instance: a member belongs to no site, an address is one member, unique by its normalized form, and the address is the sign-in name. `CmsMember` derives from Identity's user, and what a member has not is fixed in the class and has no column: no user name of its own, no phone number, no second factor, no claims, and lockout that is always on. `MemberManager` (the pool's `UserManager<CmsMember>`) and `MemberSignInManager` run on `MemberIdentityOptions`, made from `cms.Members`, bound from `FluentCms:Members` (`FCMS3160` names a bad key), and not on the `IdentityOptions` of the container, which are the admin users': the pool has its own password policy (Identity's, with a minimum of 12 characters), its own lockout, sign-in with a verified address only, and token providers of its own. The hasher is `IPasswordHasher<CmsMember>`, PBKDF2 as for the admin users (Q3) and replaceable the same way. A member is deactivated (`deactivated_at` and a new security stamp) and, unlike an admin user, can be deleted: its logins, tokens and group memberships go with it, and activity rows keep its id. Nothing of the pool is the admin pool's: the managers, the token providers, the sign-in cookie and the pages are types, purposes and schemes of their own.

*Registration and mail.* `MemberAccountService` is behind `/api/members/v1` (4.10) and the member pages alike. `AllowRegistration` is false by default, and registering then answers 404 (`FCMS3104`) on the route and on the page. Registering, asking for the verification mail again and asking for a reset return nothing whatever the address, so the answer never tells whether it is a member's; what is wrong with the request itself, an address that is none or a password the policy refuses, is told to anyone, since it says nothing of who is a member. A registration of an unknown address makes the member, not verified, and sends the verification mail. One of an address that is registered with a password and not verified sets no password, because nobody has shown yet that the address is theirs: keeping the first password would let whoever registered first sign in once the owner verifies, and taking the new one would let whoever registered last, by registering between the owner's registration and the owner's click. So the password of neither is kept. The member loses its password, gets one new security stamp, which ends the links sent so far, leaves every group it was put in, and counts as made now, so that the job below leaves the new link its time; the mail carries the link that sets a password, which is the reset link and verifies the address too, so the owner of the mailbox is the one who chooses the password. This happens once: every later registration of the address finds a member without a password and changes nothing, so the stamp stops there and nobody can keep ending the owner's links. A member that a provider made without saying its address is verified (member providers as built) is such a first registration too: it has an external login in place of a password, which goes the same way when the address is registered, with the same mail. A sign-in through a provider with such an address, by a login that is not the member's, is a second claim like a registration and does the same (`ContestAsync`). One of a verified or a deactivated address, or of a member without a password, which is also what a member an administrator made is until it uses its link, changes nothing and sends a mail that says the address has an account; a registration never gives such a member a password, which only a link sent to its mailbox does. Two registrations of a pending address at once end as one, and both are answered alike. The password is hashed in each case, and a mail goes out in each, so the time of the answer tells little. `resend-verification` and `forgot-password` only check that the address is one and enqueue a `members.send_mail` job on the `mail` queue (4.12), whatever the address, as the admin pool's reset does; the job finds the active member, makes the token and sends, and sends nothing to an address that is no member's. So the request does the same work for every address, and the time of the answer tells nothing of it (#3002). The job's payload holds the address; the Management API never shows a payload, and the job retention removes it with a job that succeeded, while a dead-lettered one keeps it. Every address of the right form enqueues a job, so requests from many client addresses, each within its `auth` limit, can make the queue wait; a job of an unknown address is one lookup. The tokens are Identity's Data Protection tokens with purposes of the pool (`FluentCmsMemberEmail`, `FluentCmsMemberPasswordReset`), so a token of the admin pool is never a member's, and they carry the security stamp: `EmailVerificationLifetime` (24 hours) and `PasswordResetLifetime` (1 hour). Verifying gives a new stamp, where Identity's own confirmation keeps it, and a reset changes it, so each token works once; a wrong, used or expired token and an unknown address are answered alike (`FCMS3166`). A reset verifies the address too, since its link reached the mailbox, which is how a member an administrator created, who has no password, gets both. A reset of an address that was not verified also removes the member's external logins, in the same change, since they date from before anyone showed whose address it is. A reset and a change of password end every session of the member, access tokens included, and a wrong current password on `change-password` counts towards the lockout. `MemberMail` sends through `IEmailSender`. A link goes to the front end when `Links:VerifyEmail` or `Links:ResetPassword` is set, an absolute `http` or `https` URL with `{email}` and `{token}` after its host (`FCMS3160` otherwise), and to the member page on the public origin (4.15) when it is not; a link is never built from a request, so with neither no mail goes out, the log has event 9071 with `FCMS3173`, and the answer is as before. An address gets one mail a minute of each of two slots (`MemberMailLimiter`): the mail its owner asks for, a reset or the verification mail again, and the link an administrator sends a new member, take one; what a registration or a sign-in through a provider sends, the verification mail of a new member, the note that the address has an account and the link to choose a password, takes the other, since anyone who knows the address can cause it. So a visitor who keeps registering the owner's address never holds back the reset the owner asks for, and the address gets at most two mails a minute. A Requested mail needs only the address too, so a visitor can hold back the owner's next request for a minute, but what goes out is still a working link to the owner's mailbox. The time of the last mail of a slot is kept in `IDistributedCache` under a SHA-256 hash of the normalized address, never the address, for the minute, and with a shared cache it is read and written under the distributed lock `members.mail_limit`, so of two instances that ask at once one sends. Every instance sees the limit once the host registers a shared cache; the default `MemoryDistributedCache` keeps it per instance, as it keeps everything (4.13), and the instance then decides it alone, without the lock. A mail held back, also when the lock is not free within 10 seconds, is event 9072, and a send that failed, or a limit that could not be read, 9070; neither changes the answer. The recurring job `members.remove_unverified` removes, once a day, the members that have not verified their address `UnverifiedMemberLifetime` (7 days) after they were made, a member an administrator created included; such a member never signed in, and the job raises no event (9073). It counts from when the member was made, not from its last mail, and a link to a member that is not verified ends when the job may remove the member: the mail gives the earlier of the token's lifetime and `created_at` plus `UnverifiedMemberLifetime` as its expiry, and both token providers refuse the token after it. So a link asked for on the last day is good for the hours it says, and once none are left no mail goes (event 9076). A member with a password or a provider login that is registered again then counts as made now and gets a new link; one with neither, such as a member an administrator made, waits until the job removes it, at most a day, and is registered afresh. Keeping the time of the last mail instead would need a column the table does not have (#3002).

*Groups and permissions.* `cms_member_group (id, name, description, role_id, created_at)` and `cms_member_group_member (group_id, member_id)`. A group has a name of 1 to 64 lower-case letters, digits, `-` and `_`, unique in the instance, so that it is one name on every database and one word of the principal's groups, and is bound to one role, of the instance or of a site; a group always has a role, and one that only names its members for a row rule is bound to a role without grants. A member holds the Public role of the site of the request, the site's own or else the seeded one as for a visitor, the built-in Member role, and the roles of its groups. The evaluator reads them with their stamps, and the names of the groups, in one query on every request, so a change holds from the next request; a member that is deactivated or deleted holds nothing, not even what a visitor reads, and nothing is taken from the token, whose roles or groups claim is never read. The principal a row rule sees carries the group names in the claim `fcms_groups`, and `Principal.IsInGroup(name)` (`FluentCMS.Content`, in the SDK) reads it, as in `ctx => perk => perk.Tier == (ctx.Principal.IsInGroup("gold") ? "gold" : "standard")`. Only that principal carries them (4.8). A member's token has no scope that manages, so its grants give it published reads and nothing else, a group bound to a role with `*` included (4.8). A role is not deleted while a group is bound to it (`FCMS3044`, 4.10); the foreign key cascades, so a site that is deleted takes the groups bound to its roles along, as it does the API keys bound to them.

*Clients and tokens.* A client of `cms.Clients.Applications` names its pool with `Pool`, `Admins` (the default) or `Members`. A client of the member pool asks for `openid`, `profile`, `email` and `offline_access` only, names no roles, has no consent step, needs redirect URIs or `AllowDirectLogin`, and is not the admin application's client (`FCMS3021`); it may have a secret, and is never a service account: it holds neither the client credentials grant nor introspection. The authorize endpoint answers such a client from the member cookie and never from the admin's, challenging to `/account/members/login`, with `prompt`, `max_age` and the absolute session lifetime as for an admin user. Its tokens carry the audience `fluentcms:members`, `fcms_kind` `Member`, `sub`, `name` (the display name, or the address) and the scopes asked for among those four, whatever else was asked; they carry no roles and no groups. The token endpoint reads the member again for a code or a refresh token and refuses one that is deactivated, deleted, locked out or whose security stamp changed (`invalid_grant`). A code or refresh token says whose it is by its kind and is redeemed only through a client of the pool it was issued in, so a client the configuration moves to the other pool redeems nothing. The bearer scheme accepts both audiences, and a member's token is kept out of the Management API, of drafts and of `/mcp` by its scopes: it never holds `management`, `draft` or `mcp`. `/connect/userinfo` answers a member's token from the member. `/connect/logout` ends the sign-in of the pool of the client the request names, by its `client_id` or the audience of its `id_token_hint`, and the admin's when it names none; the two logout pages share an antiforgery token, so the member page marks its form (`fcms_pool`), neither page passes the mark on from a link, and the endpoint takes a confirmation only from the page of the pool it signs out. Deactivating or deleting a member, a reset and a change of password revoke its authorizations and every token of its subject (`MemberSessions`), so an access token stops on the next request, one of a direct login without a session included. `UserSessions` lists and ends a member's sessions for the Management API, since a member's id is the subject of its tokens.

*Direct login* (Q4). `grant_type=member_password` with `username`, `password` and `client_id` at `/connect/token`, a custom grant of OpenIddict, for a client of the member pool with `AllowDirectLogin` while `cms.Members.AllowDirectLogin` is on; both are off by default. Every refusal is `invalid_grant` with one description: the option off, a client without the flag, an unknown address, a wrong password, an address that is not verified, a member that is deactivated or locked out. The reason is in the log (event 9075, with the client and the client address, never the address typed or the password), and an address or a password that was refused is a `member.login_failed` activity row. A wrong password counts towards the lockout, and the hash runs for an unknown address and for a member that may not sign in, as on the login page. Every client of the member pool holds the grant's permission, so that the endpoint answers; a client of the admin pool does not, and OpenIddict refuses it (`unauthorized_client`). The discovery document names the grant only while the option is on, and the startup report lists `member-direct-login` in Production as the less secure way in (4.15).

*Member pages.* Razor Pages of the `FluentCms` area at `/account/members/login`, `/logout`, `/register`, `/verify-email`, `/forgot-password` and `/reset-password`, and `/external-login` and `/logins` for the providers (member providers as built), with the layout, branding, headers, content security policy and `auth` rate limit of `/account` (hosted pages as built), and public models (`MemberLoginModel` and the others in `FluentCMS.Core.Api.Account`) for a host that overrides a page. They sign in with the cookie scheme `FluentCms.Members` (cookie `.FluentCms.Members`), apart from Identity's application cookie, so an admin session and a member session in one browser neither sign each other in nor out; `MemberSignInManager` signs out that cookie alone. The login page takes the address and the password with lockout, gives every refusal one answer, an address that is not verified included, and returns to a local `ReturnUrl` only. The register page is there only while registration is on. Opening the verification link changes nothing: the page has a button, so a mail scanner that follows links does not spend the token, and without a link it sends the mail again. The reset page is also where a member an administrator created chooses its first password. Every form carries an antiforgery token.

*Member providers as built* ([#2993](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2993)). `cms.Members.Providers`, bound from `FluentCms:Members:Providers:{name}`, lists the external providers members sign in with, apart from `cms.Sso`. A provider has `Type` (`Google`, `Microsoft`, `GitHub`, `Apple` or `Oidc`, in any case), `ClientId`, `ClientSecret`, `Scopes` and `DisplayName`; `Oidc` takes `Authority` (https, unless `RequireHttpsMetadata` is off) and may have no secret, and `Apple` takes `TeamId`, `KeyId` and `PrivateKey`, the PEM text of the key, and no `ClientSecret`. A name is 1 to 64 lower-case letters, digits or `-`: the options find a provider whatever the case, but its logins are kept under the name as it is written, so a name has one way to be written and a later change of case cannot part the members from their logins. It is none of the sign-in methods of the pool (`password`, `direct_login`), since a sign-in event names the provider as its method. A provider that is not valid stops the host with `FCMS3180` and its key: a name that is not one, an unknown or missing type, a missing client id, a missing secret for Google, Microsoft or GitHub, an Apple key that does not import as a private key, a scope that is not one word, a display name of more than 128 characters, `LinkVerifiedEmail` on Microsoft, and a name whose scheme is the name of another authentication scheme; for the last, the authentication options are built when the host starts. Secrets come from configuration only, and no message or log line repeats one. *Handlers.* The name is the login provider in `cms_member_login` and the callback path, `/account/members/sso/{name}`; the scheme is `member-{name}`, added to the authentication options at start as the providers of `cms.Sso` are, after them, so that a member provider whose scheme is the name of an admin provider is the one refused. A scheme of a member provider has no display name, which is what keeps it off the admin login page, which lists the schemes that have one; the member pages list `cms.Members.Providers` and challenge nothing else, so a provider of the same name in both sections is two schemes that never meet. Google and Microsoft are the in-box handlers (`Microsoft.AspNetCore.Authentication.Google` and `.MicrosoftAccount` 10.0.15), GitHub and Apple those of aspnet-contrib (`AspNet.Security.OAuth.GitHub` and `.Apple` 10.1.0, Q10), and `Oidc` the OpenID Connect handler with the code flow, PKCE and the token's own claim names. An OAuth handler builds the `redirect_uri` from the request, when it challenges and again when it redeems the code, with no event between the two, so each is subclassed (`MemberGoogleHandler` and the others) to build both from the public origin when one is set (4.15); the OpenID Connect provider does it in `OnRedirectToIdentityProvider`. The Apple handler signs its client secret with the private key (ES256, in the name of the team) and validates the id token against Apple's keys. A host tunes a provider with `services.Configure<GoogleOptions>("member-{name}", ...)` and the like. Neither the remote sign-out path nor the signed-out callback path of an `Oidc` provider is served (404): a provider's front-channel logout ends no member session, and no logout at the provider is started here. The Apple package writes the client secret it signs and the tokens of a sign-in to the log at Trace; a handler's log is named after its type, so `MemberAppleHandler` keeps the name of the package's handler, and all of it is under the category `AspNet.Security.OAuth.Apple`, and none under a category of FluentCMS ([#3006](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3006)). `AppleLogLevel`, a post-configuration of `LoggerFilterOptions`, holds that category at `Information` or above unless the configuration names it or a category under it: it adds a rule of the category for every provider and one for each logging provider that has rules of its own, since a provider's own rule outranks a rule of every provider whatever its category, at the level named for every provider when there is one. A rule only raises the level the category had and keeps the filter of the rule it replaces, so a provider held above `Information`, as the event log is on Windows, stays there. A default of `Trace`, for every provider or for one, does not reach the category; naming it lower does, and the startup report lists `member-apple-logging` while a logger of the package's categories writes below `Information` in Production with an Apple provider configured, which also catches a logger factory of the host's own. The request log of ASP.NET Core writes the URL of each request at `Information`, query included. The callback of a Google, Microsoft or GitHub provider is a GET, so its line holds the one-time `code` and the `state`; Apple and `Oidc` providers, and the providers of `cms.Sso`, post their answer (`form_post`), so theirs holds the path only. The query is left in the log: a code is redeemed once, by the handler as the callback arrives, and only with the client secret, and with the PKCE verifier of the browser's correlation cookie for Google and Microsoft. *The external cookie.* The handlers sign into `FluentCms.Members.External` (cookie `.FluentCms.Members.External`, five minutes), apart from Identity's external cookie, which is the admin pool's, so a sign-in at a provider of one pool is never read by the pages of the other. The ticket names the provider that issued it, written when the provider answers, and `MemberSignInManager` is never asked for Identity's external sign-in, which would end the admin's cookie. `/account/members/external-login` reads the cookie, ends it whatever follows, and asks `MemberExternalSignIn`. *Who signs in.* A member linked to the login signs in, with the provider as its authentication method and no `amr`, unless it is deactivated, locked out or not verified. Anyone else gets a member only while `AllowRegistration` is on, named by the provider's address and display name, with no password, the member and its login in one change; with registration off the sign-in is refused (`FCMS3187` in the log). *Verified addresses.* The address of a new member counts as verified only when the provider says so: `email_verified` of Google's user information, of Apple's id token and of an `Oidc` id token. The GitHub handler would take the primary address whether or not GitHub verified it, and the public address of the profile before that, so `MemberGitHubHandler` takes the primary address only when it is verified and the profile's address is not mapped: a GitHub sign-in carries a verified address or none, `user:email` is always asked for, and an account without a verified primary address makes no member (`FCMS3181`). Microsoft gives no such flag, so its address never counts as verified. A member made with an address that is not verified gets the verification mail of the pool and is not signed in; the mail names the provider, so that the owner of the mailbox knows which account its link lets in, the page says that a mail is on its way, and a later sign-in through the provider, or `resend-verification`, sends it again until the address is verified. *An address that is a member's.* It is never taken over: the sign-in is refused (`FCMS3182`), with no member named in the event, and the member links the provider after signing in. `LinkVerifiedEmail` on a provider, off by default and refused for `Microsoft`, links such a member at its first sign-in through the provider when the provider says the address is verified, the member may sign in now, which needs its own address verified, and it has no login of that provider yet. A sign-in with an address the provider did not verify is answered alike whether or not the address is a member's, with the page of the mail, and the member gets the mail that says it has an account. *An address that is not verified yet.* A sign-in through a provider with the address of a member whose address is not verified, by a login that is not that member's, is a second claim of the address, and `MemberAccountService.ContestAsync` treats it as it treats a second registration (registration and mail), whatever the provider says of the address: the password or the logins the first claim left are removed with one new security stamp, which ends the links sent so far, the member leaves its groups, and the mail carries the link that chooses a password. Nothing is linked and nobody is signed in, also with `LinkVerifiedEmail` and an address the provider verified, since the member is not shown to be its owner's yet; the answer is the page of the mail, or the refusal while registration is off, which is what a visitor without a member gets there. Without this, whoever claimed the address first, with a password or through a provider that does not verify addresses, would keep that way in once the owner, who was told that a mail is on its way, used a verification link from its inbox. A member with neither a password nor a login is left as it is, so a repeat changes no stamp, and an address still gets one mail a minute. A login that a provider brought without verifying the address does not outlive the owner of the mailbox: it is removed when the address is registered again and when a reset link sets the password, before the address was verified (registration and mail), so whoever made the member first cannot sign in as its owner later. Verifying with the verification link keeps the login, as it keeps a password the registration set. *Linking.* `/account/members/logins` shows the signed-in member its logins. Linking starts there with a form post, goes to the provider and comes back to the page, which names the account at the provider and links it only on a second form post; the sign-in at the provider carries the id of the member that started it and is not taken by another member or by the external login page. A login belongs to one member and a member has one login of a provider (`FCMS3186`). `MemberLogins` lists `{ "provider", "displayName" }`, without the provider's key, and removes the member's logins of a provider (`FCMS3185` when it has none) in one save with a new security stamp, and then ends the member's sessions, access tokens included, as a new password does; the page that removed it keeps its own cookie. The last way to sign in is not removed (`FCMS3184`, 409): a login counts while its provider is in `cms.Members.Providers`, and a password while the member has one. `GET /me/logins` and `DELETE /me/logins/{provider}` are the member's routes and `GET` and `DELETE /members/{id}/logins` and `/logins/{provider}` the administrator's, with `members.manage` (4.10). *Failures.* An error at the provider, or a callback that belongs to no sign-in started in that browser, goes back to the member login page as a failed sign-in (`invalid_credentials`, event 9091) with the return URL it started with; one that was started to link goes back to the logins page and is no sign-in. The callbacks are answered by the authentication middleware, before the endpoints and the rate limiter of their policies, as those of admin SSO are; the check before authentication counts them with the `auth` limit by client address, as it counts the pages, and each failed callback is a `member.login_failed` row. Every form of these pages carries an antiforgery token, and the return URL is a local one. The `member_password` grant takes no external login: a member a provider made has no password. Codes `FCMS3180` to `FCMS3187`; event ids 9090 to 9094.

*Events.* `MemberRegisteredEvent`, `MemberVerifiedEvent`, `MemberUpdatedEvent` (with the names of what changed, `MemberChanges`), `MemberDeactivatedEvent`, `MemberDeletedEvent`, `MemberGroupChangedEvent`, `MemberLoginLinkedEvent` and `MemberLoginUnlinkedEvent` (with the provider) from the store and the services with the change, `MemberGroupCreatedEvent`, `MemberGroupUpdatedEvent` and `MemberGroupDeletedEvent` for a group, and `MemberLoginSucceededEvent` and `MemberLoginFailedEvent` from `MemberSignInManager` with the way in as their method (`MemberLoginMethods`: `password` on the page, `direct_login` at the token endpoint, and the name of a provider for a sign-in through it). They are the activity rows `member.registered`, `member.verified`, `member.updated`, `member.deactivated`, `member.deleted`, `member.group_changed`, `member.login_linked`, `member.login_unlinked`, `member_group.created`, `member_group.updated`, `member_group.deleted`, `member.login_succeeded` and `member.login_failed`, of no site. What a member does to its own account names the member as the actor, what an administrator does the administrator, and a failed sign-in the anonymous principal, without the address that was typed. Codes `FCMS3160` to `FCMS3174`, and `FCMS3180` to `FCMS3187` for the providers; event ids 9070 to 9076 and 9090 to 9094.

**From the command line (B-91).** `fluentcms user create` makes an active administrator, with a password read at a prompt or from standard input and never from an argument, in the host only; `user invite`, `user add-role`, and `apikey create`, `list` and `revoke` call the services of the endpoints, inside the host as the system or with `--url` as a management key (6.1).

**Security defaults.** Closed by default: no anonymous Delivery access until the Public role grants it; CORS allow-list with no wildcard; secure cookie flags; strict rate limit on `/connect/*` and `/api/members/v1/*`; secrets only from configuration providers; Production refuses to start with a generated signing key (`FCMS1102`), without a public origin (`FCMS1105`) or with the Data Protection ring unencrypted (`FCMS1106`), unless `Security:AllowGeneratedSigningKey`, `Security:AllowRequestOrigin` or `Security:AllowUnencryptedKeyRing` is set for it. The last two were report lines until the code review of 7 Nov 2026, which made them refusals like the first (#2847, decision 1; #2848, #2849): an unset origin lets a forged `Host` receive a real user's reset link, and an unencrypted ring lets a reader of the database forge the sign-in cookie. A setting accepted by its opt-in stays in the startup report (4.15), and other environments start as before.

### 4.8 Authorization

Rows 4.3 to 4.8, 4.12, 4.17, 8.14; decisions 31, 32.

**Permission catalogue.** Core permissions (`users.manage`, `roles.manage`, `apikeys.manage`, `sites.manage`, `settings.{area}.manage`, `jobs.manage`, `activity.read`, `schema.read`, `plugins.manage`, `transfer.manage`, `system.read`, `members.manage`), generated per type (`content.{type}.create|read|update|delete|publish|unpublish|restore|purge`), and plugin-declared ones through `IPermissionProvider`. All stored in `cms_role_permission (role_id, permission, constraints json)`.

**Constraints.** Additive only, no deny rules. A grant may be constrained to sites, locales, a field allow-list (`fields: ["title", "slug"]`, null means all), a row rule name and a plugin's resources (`resources`, such as media folders, [ADR 0004](../decisions/0004-folder-limited-media-grants.md)). A grant with `resources` counts only in `IPermissionChecker.ResourcesAsync`, which answers null when a grant with no resource limit holds the permission and otherwise the union of the resources of the grants that hold it; every other check, core's and content's included, leaves it out, so `*` limited to resources gives no core or content permission. A principal's effective permission set is the union of its roles' grants. Field lists and row rules are tied together where the union would otherwise give access no grant did: a write is decided per grant against the entry, and until responses can project per row, the rows of a read stay the union while a field is readable only if a grant with no row rule allows it or every applying grant does.

**Global types.** A write to a type with `ContentScope.Global` (create, update, patch, delete, restore) needs a grant with no site limit, from a role with no `site_id`, because a global entry is shared by every site. Reads are unchanged. The seeded instance-wide roles are unaffected.

**Shared fields.** A non-localizable field of a localized type is shared by all locales, so writing it needs a grant with no locale limit, or one that covers every locale of the entry, including a locale being added. Two grants that cover the locales only together do not count. Otherwise the field is unwritable and keeps its value.

**Roles.** `cms_role` has an optional `site_id` (null is instance-wide). Built-ins seeded by the first migration: Super Admin (everything), Editor, Author (own entries row rule), Public (the anonymous principal per site, which is how public access configuration is stored), Member (default for the member pool).

**Last Super Admin.** The instance always keeps one active user who holds `*` with no constraints through an instance-wide role (#2847, decision 5; #2850). Super Admin's own grants do not change (`FCMS3043`), and an edit of another instance-wide role that drops that grant is refused with `FCMS3052` when no other active user would hold it. Deactivating a user and taking roles from it follow the same rule (admin users as built, 4.7).

**Row rules.** Registered in code with a name and used by roles as data:

```csharp
cms.Authorization.AddRowRule<Article>("own-entries",
    ctx => article => article.CreatedBy == ctx.Principal.Id);
cms.Authorization.AddRowRule<Article>("region",
    ctx => article => article.Region == ctx.Principal.Claim("region"));
```

The evaluator compiles the predicate into the list query and checks it on single-entry operations. JSON-authored rules are a Should (decision 32).

**Evaluator.** `IAuthorizationEvaluator` caches the effective permission set per principal for the request; a `permissions_version` stamp on roles invalidates it across instances. Scopes narrow but never widen: a token with `draft` may read drafts if its roles allow; `management` tokens are bounded by roles. The set keeps its answers about content (the grants behind an operation, the readable fields and the row rule) per type, operation, site and locale for the request, so filtering the fields of a list goes through the grants once per type, not once per entry and field. A grant's pattern is split once, when its role's grants are loaded into the cache across requests. `RoleService.DeleteAsync` takes a deleted role out of that cache on its instance; another instance keeps the entry, which no one asks for again, until it restarts, bounded by the number of roles (#2854). Without an operation nothing is answered (2.3).

**Member groups on the principal as built** ([#2992](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2992)). The evaluator reads a member's groups with its role stamps (4.7) and puts their names on the principal it hands to row rules, so `Principal.IsInGroup` is for row rules. The principal of the operation context itself, which a hook, a job or a plugin's endpoint reads, comes from the token and carries no groups, so `IsInGroup` answers false there for every name. Code outside a row rule must therefore neither refuse something because it is false nor allow something because its negation is true. Putting the groups on the operation's principal would cost a query on every request of a member, also on those that check no permission, so it is left out. Taking a member out of a group and deleting a group need `members.manage` alone (4.10), which widens what a member reads when a row rule negates a group, as `!IsInGroup("trial")` does; a host that writes such a rule gives `members.manage` with that in mind.

**Role stamps on every request as built.** The stamps of the caller's roles are read on every request, in one query, and are what carries a role change to every instance: the grants are kept per role and stamp, and the role an anonymous caller holds, its site's own Public role or else the instance-wide one, is kept per site with the stamp it had when it was found. A warm anonymous request reads that one stamp; a stamp that moved, or a role that is gone, finds the Public role again in the query that reads the stamps. An API key's role comes from the row the authentication handler read and checked for the request (it is kept in `HttpContext.Items` until the request ends, and its expiry is checked again), so the key is read once per request; a job, which has no request, reads the key again, so a key revoked since the job was scheduled holds no role (#2857).

**Client roles as built** ([#2978](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2978)). An MCP client that registered itself has a role (4.7, "Dynamic client registration as built"), and a call through it is allowed what both the user's roles and that role allow. The authorize endpoint puts the role's id on the code as the claim `fcms_client_role`, and the token endpoint reads the client again for a code or a refresh token and writes the claim anew, so the access token carries the role the client has now; a token of any other client has no such claim. The evaluator reads the claim from the principal, loads the grants of that role with the principal's own, in the one query that reads the role stamps, and gives `EffectivePermissions` a second set, which is the role's grants worked out as for a principal of its own with the same scopes and bindings. Every answer is then what both sets give: a permission and a content operation need a grant in each, the readable and writable fields, the readable locales and a plugin's resources are those both allow, the row rules of both apply together, and handing out a grant (`Covers`) needs both to hold it. Since it is the evaluator, it holds on every surface that takes the token, Delivery included, and for a plugin's `IPermissionChecker`. A claim that names no role, or a role that was deleted since, is a set with no grant, which leaves the token nothing rather than the user's own reach; a role is therefore not kept from deletion by a client that has it. The role only removes: a client with Super Admin gives a user nothing the user's roles do not, so approving a client with a role needs no grant of that role, unlike giving a role to a user or a key. `IEffectiveAccess`, which the MCP tool list and the input schemas of its tools are built from (5.4, "Tools as built"), describes the same: the types and their readable fields, the operations of a type, the fields a create or an update takes and the permissions held are those both sets give, so a tool is listed through a client only when a call through it would be allowed.

**Introspection and MCP.** Both call the evaluator to drop types and fields the principal cannot read, so generated admin forms and agent tools match permissions (4.17). As built, the MCP plugin asks through `IEffectiveAccess`, which core answers from the evaluator (5.4).

### 4.9 Activity log

Row 4.14; the backend half of 3.26 and the data for 4.20 (Should).

`cms_activity (id v7, site_id, occurred_at, actor_type, actor_id, actor_name, origin, client_id, action, target_type, target_id, target_label, locale, details json, correlation_id, ip)`. Written by event subscribers, so plugins get logged for free when they raise events. Retention job, default 30 days, configurable per site. `GET /api/management/v1/activity` with filters on actor, origin, action, target, date range; permission `activity.read`.

**As built.** Every event of the catalogue that exists has an outbox handler named `activity.{action}`, registered with `cms.Events.AddActivity<TEvent>(name, describe)`, which a plugin calls for its own events. The row says what the event says: actor, origin, client id, correlation id, site and time come from its `EventSource`, not from the job that delivers it, and the id of the row is the delivery key, so a repeated delivery writes nothing. A row names its target by type and id and never by a field of content, and text is passed through `SecretScrubber` and cut to its column; `IActivityLog` is the direct writer for what no event covers: it writes on a context of its own, refuses inside a transaction, and stores actions in lower case; `ip` is filled only by a writer that knows it. `IActivityQuery` reads the site of the current operation, newest first, filtered by actor, origin, action (exact, or a prefix ending in `*`), target and date range (`From` inclusive, `To` exclusive), and leaves the permission check to the endpoint. Rows of no site (the failures of jobs, whose text can name internals of the host) are returned only with `IncludeInstance`, which is for instance-wide readers only: the query cannot tell a reader of one site from a reader of the instance, so the Management endpoint must check an instance-level permission before it sets it. Retention is `cms.Activity.Retention` (30 days) with `SiteRetention` by site slug until sites have settings of their own; the instance-wide rows use the default. The job is `activity.retention`, daily at 03:30 UTC, deleting in batches of `PruneBatchSize` (at most 1000), served by an index on `occurred_at`. `cms.Activity.Enabled = false` stops new rows and stores no schedule, leaves a stored one alone (the job returns at once while the log is off), and drops the rows already waiting in the outbox. `role.updated` has the details `added` and `removed`, the grants the update gave and took, each its permission followed by its constraints as JSON when it has any, separated by semicolons and cut to 256 characters like every detail (#2854). A refused API key writes no row, except a valid key sent from an address it is not allowed from (4.7).

### 4.10 REST APIs

Rows 2.1, 2.2, 2.6 to 2.9, 2.12 to 2.20, 10.8, 10.12.

**Delivery API.** Read-only, published by default, under `/api/delivery/v1`:

| Route | Notes |
| --- | --- |
| `GET /{type}` | list with the query grammar; singletons return the single entry |
| `GET /{type}/{id}` | by id |
| `GET /{type}/by/{field}/{value}` | by a unique field such as slug |
| `GET /media/{id}` | asset metadata and URLs (media plugin; as built in 5.1, B-66) |
| `GET /openapi/v1.json`, `GET /scalar` | document and UI (host-level, not versioned) |

`status=draft` requires a preview key, a bearer token with the `draft` scope, or a preview token for the entry (4.7). Responses carry `ETag` (hash of version, locale and query), `Last-Modified` and a configurable `Cache-Control` (default `public, max-age=60, stale-while-revalidate=300`). Output caching (decision 56) tags responses with `site:{id}`, `type:{site}:{type}` and `entry:{id}`; publish, unpublish and media events evict by tag and raise `CacheInvalidatedEvent` for webhook-driven CDN purges.

**As built.** A request bridge in `FluentCMS.Core.Api.Common`, shared with the Management API, sets the `OperationContext`. It reads the principal from the `FluentCms` scheme that `AddFluentCms()` registers, which goes to the API key handler when a key is sent and otherwise to the configured bearer scheme, never to the host's default. A host without that scheme uses the request's user. A request without one is the anonymous principal. An authenticated principal without a usable `fcms_kind` or `sub`, or one whose credentials the scheme refuses, is 401 (`FCMS3102`), never anonymous. The site follows 4.6. A key bound to one site reads it. A key bound to several reads the one named, or the site of the request's host name when it is among them, which is the default site unless a host resolver names another (4.6), and is otherwise refused (`FCMS3122`, 400). A named site outside the binding is 403 (`FCMS3121`) whether it exists or not, and an `X-Cms-Site` header and a `site` parameter that name different sites are refused (`FCMS2201`). Delivery reads drafts only with the `preview` or `draft` scope, even for a principal whose `management` scope lets the engine read them. A preview token (4.7) is taken by `GET /{type}/{id}` with `status=draft` only: the list, `by/`, another type or entry, a read without `status=draft` and, for a token bound to a locale, another locale or `locale=all` are refused with `FCMS3028` (403), whose `errors[].path` is `id`, `type`, `status` or `locale`; another site is `FCMS3121` as for a bound key. A draft read is `no-store`, so no cache keeps what a token read. An unknown type and one the principal cannot read are the same 404 (`FCMS3104` on `type`). A problem's `errors[].path` names the parameter the client sent (`filter[publishedOn][gt]`, `limit`, or `field` and `value` on `by/`), not the field the engine reports. `by/{field}` needs a unique field (`FCMS3123`), which is checked before the query runs, so a field that is not unique costs no query; a field the principal may not read is refused as the unknown field the query would report (`FCMS2202`), so that no field is shown to exist by its error. Under `locale=all` an entry is `{ "id", "locales": { code: entry } }`, and a type that is not localized has one entry, keyed by the empty string, as its `locale` field is.

**Caching as built.** The `ETag` is a hash of the body, which holds the version and locale of every entry, populated ones included, and follows from the query, so it also changes when a populated target or what the caller may read changes. There is no `Last-Modified`, and only `If-None-Match` decides a 304: no date in the body moves when an entry leaves a list, a populated target is unpublished or a role changes what the caller may read, so a date would let a client or a CDN keep a stale copy. `If-Modified-Since` is ignored, as RFC 9110 (13.1.3) asks of a resource with no modification date, and the Delivery policy takes it off the request before the output cache looks, which would otherwise answer it by the date it stored the copy; such a request is answered in full, from the cache when it holds a copy, rather than running the endpoint each time. `FluentCms:Delivery:Cache:CacheControl` is sent as written to a published read without credentials, in its `private` form to one with a key or a token (a CDN keys by URL, not by credentials), `no-store` on a draft read, and an error keeps the exception handler's `no-cache,no-store`; responses vary on `Authorization`, `X-Api-Key`, `X-Cms-Site` and `X-Cms-Locale`. The three routes answer `HEAD` as `GET` without the body, `Content-Length` included, and the output cache keeps a `HEAD` apart from its `GET`. `UseFluentCms()` adds the output cache last, after the rate limiter, authentication and authorization, so a policy the host puts on `MapFluentCms()` holds for a cached answer too, and the Delivery group carries its policy: one copy for every request without credentials and one per API key, keyed by a hash of its claims (role, sites, scope), for `OutputCacheDuration` (60 seconds; `OutputCache`, `cms.DeliveryCache` in code, turns it off, and with it the evictions and their event unless `CacheTagHeader` is set). The key also holds the schema hash, as the OpenAPI document's does, so a store that outlives the process or that instances on another schema share serves none of them a copy made for another schema. Beside the host name, which the framework puts in the key, it holds the site that host name resolves to (4.6, B-80), so the copies of two sites are apart whatever their host names, and a host name that moves to another site reads none of the copies it left, on the instance that moved it at once and before any eviction has run. The schema is built once per process, so a schema change is a new process: the in-memory store starts empty, and the hash keeps a shared one right during a rolling deployment, where evicting at start would not, since an instance still on the old schema would write old copies again. A bearer token, a draft read, refused credentials and anything but a 200 are not cached, and the other FluentCMS groups say so, so a host's base policy leaves them alone. Response compression goes before `UseFluentCms()`: the cache then keeps the body the endpoint wrote and compression encodes each answer for its request, so the key need not vary on `Accept-Encoding`. Compression inside the cache would keep a body encoded for one client, so a response that has a `Content-Encoding` while no compression middleware wraps the cache is not kept, by the Delivery and the OpenAPI policies alike. A cached read is tagged `site:{id}`, `type:{site}:{type}` (`type:global:{type}` for a global type, whose entries every site serves) and `entry:{id}` for every entry in the body, populated ones and references included. An outbox handler evicts `entry:{id}` and the type's tag on `EntryPublishedEvent`, `EntryUnpublishedEvent`, `EntryTrashedEvent` and `EntryRestoredEvent`, and `site:{id}` on `SiteUpdatedEvent`, which a change to the site or to one of its locales raises (4.6), since a site's locales decide its fallbacks, and raises `CacheInvalidatedEvent` with those tags. A change to a role or its grants evicts `site:{id}` the same way, because the Public role decides what the shared copy holds and a key's role what its own holds: `RoleVersionInterceptor`, which raises the role's permissions version in the same `SaveChanges`, stages an internal `RolePermissionsChangedEvent` in the outbox there, with the role's site, or every site for a role of the instance, so that `RoleService` and anything else that writes roles through `CmsDbContext` evict with the commit, and a crash after it does not leave the copies. A newly published relation target shows when the copy expires. The store is the provider slot `IOutputCacheStore` (4.13), the framework's in-memory store by default, which the startup report lists; an eviction reaches only the store of the instance that delivers the event, so with several instances on the in-memory store the others serve their copies until they expire, and a shared store, such as Redis, evicts for all. Lookups count in `fluentcms.cache.requests` as cache `delivery`. This answers items 6, 7, 13 and 15 of [#2860](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2860) of the review of 7 Nov 2026.

**Cache tag header as built.** `FluentCms:Delivery:Cache:CacheTagHeader`, unset by default, names a header that carries a published read's tags for a CDN that purges by tag: `Cache-Tag` (Cloudflare), `Surrogate-Key` (Fastly), `Edge-Cache-Tag` (Akamai) or any other HTTP token; a name that is not a token, or one the response writes itself (`Cache-Control`, `ETag`, `Vary`, `Set-Cookie` and the like), stops the host with `FCMS1101`. A published read, anonymous or with a key, from the cache or not, a 304 included, carries the tags the output cache gives it: `site:{id}`, `type:{site}:{type}` or `type:global:{type}`, and `entry:{id}` for every entry in the body; the media route carries `site:{id}`, `media:{id}` and `media-presets:{site}`. A draft read is `no-store` and carries none, and without the option no response carries them.
