The tags are separated by a space for `Surrogate-Key`, as Fastly reads it, and by a comma for any other name. Every `CacheInvalidatedEvent` names tags of that same set, and the evictions and their event run while either the output cache or the header is on, since a CDN holds what the header tagged though the server kept nothing. The purge is the webhooks plugin's (5.2): a webhook on `cache.invalidated` receives `data.tags`, and a receiver checks the signature and calls the CDN's purge by tag, for Cloudflare `POST /client/v4/zones/{zone}/purge_cache` with `{ "tags": [...] }`. The core takes no CDN dependency and sends no purge itself. This answers [#2935](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2935).

**Round trips as built.** Before its content queries a request used to read the default site or the named one, a site's default locale and its locales (once per lookup), the site's Public role by name or an API key's row a second time, and the role stamps. Now the site and its locales come from `SiteCache` (4.6), the Public role and the grants from the cache keyed by the stamps (4.8), and a key's role from the row its authentication read. A warm anonymous Delivery read of a public entry sends the stamp of the Public role and its content queries only (for a type with a relation: the entry with its document, and its references), three queries where it sent nine, and one with an API key adds the key's lookup by the handler, four where it sent ten (`DeliveryRoundTripTests`). The stamp read stays because it is what makes a role change visible on the next request on every instance; a change to an existing site or its locales reaches another instance within 30 seconds (#2857).

**Plugin Delivery groups as built.** A plugin mounts a group under `/api/delivery/v1` with `MapDelivery(prefix)` (4.13), beside `MapManagement`. Its endpoints get what core's Delivery group has, applied by the same code: the Delivery surface, the output cache policy, rate limit and CORS policy (`GET` and `HEAD`), the FluentCMS authentication with an anonymous caller read as its site's Public role, the request bridge's site and locale, and a place in the OpenAPI document. A copy of an answer is tagged with its site only, so it goes when the site is updated, a role changes or the plugin is disabled in the site (an outbox handler evicts `site:{id}` on `PluginDisabledEvent`, since a copy is served before the plugin's per-site gate); an endpoint whose answer changes on anything else opts out with `CacheOutput(policy => policy.NoCache())`, and sets its own `Cache-Control`. A prefix whose first segment is a route parameter or the name of a content type would take core's `/{type}` routes and stops the host (`FCMS1524`), and `MapGroup` still refuses a raw prefix under a group of core. The media plugin's route of the table above is `GET /api/delivery/v1/media/{id}`, mapped with `MapDelivery("/media")`; it sets the `ETag`, `Cache-Control` and `Vary` of content itself and tags its copies `media:{id}` and `media-presets:{site}` beside the site's, which its own outbox handlers evict (5.1, B-66). This answers [#2888](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2888) of the review of 7 Nov 2026.

**Management API.** Under `/api/management/v1`, bearer or management key:

| Group | Routes |
| --- | --- |
| content | `/content/{type}` list and create; `/content/{type}/{id}` get, patch, delete; `/publish`, `/unpublish`, `/discard`, `/schedule`; `/versions`, `/versions/{n}/restore`, `/versions/{a}/diff/{b}`; `/content/{type}/bulk`; `/content/{type}/trash` list, `/trash/{id}/restore`, `/trash/{id}` purge |
| schema | `/schema`, `/schema/types/{type}`, `/schema/snapshot` |
| identity | `/users`, `/users/{id}/sessions`, `/users/{id}/two-factor/reset`, `/invitations`, `/roles`, `/permissions` (catalogue), `/api-keys`, `/members`, `/member-groups` |
| sites | `/sites`, `/sites/{id}/locales`; `/sites/{id}/hosts` (multi-site plugin, 5.6) |
| settings | `/settings/{area}` get and put, with the JSON schema of the settings type |
| jobs | `/jobs`, `/jobs/{id}`, `/jobs/{id}/retry`, `/jobs/{id}/cancel`, `/jobs/dead-letter` |
| events | `/events/dead-letter` list and purge by handler, `/events/dead-letter/{id}` remove, `/events/dead-letter/{id}/retry` |
| activity | `/activity` |
| plugins | `/plugins`, `/plugins/{name}/sites/{site}` enable and disable |
| transfer | `/transfer/export` (job, then download), `/transfer/import` (upload, dry run, apply) |
| system | `/system/diagnostics`, `/system/health` (detailed; `system.read`) |
| me | `/me`, `/me/sites`, `/me/sessions`, `/me/two-factor` |

**Content group as built.** The routes need a bearer token or a key with the `management` scope: a request that is not signed in is 401 and one without the scope, such as a Delivery key, is 403; what the principal may then do is the content service's to decide, through the request bridge as on Delivery. `GET /content/{type}` and `GET /content/{type}/{id}` take the Delivery query grammar and read the draft unless `status=published` is asked. `POST /content/{type}` creates and answers 201 with `Location`, which keeps the `site` parameter the request named; `PUT /content/{type}/{id}` replaces the draft (`UpdateAsync`) and `PATCH` applies a JSON merge patch, both in the request's locale, where `If-Match: "0"` adds a locale the entry does not have. `DELETE` moves the entry to the trash. `POST .../publish` answers `{ "entry", "warnings" }`, `.../unpublish` and `.../discard` the entry; `POST .../schedule` takes `{ "action": "publish" | "unpublish", "at", "timeZone" }`, where `at` has an offset without a zone (`2027-03-01T09:00:00Z`, an instant) and is the wall-clock time there with one (`2027-03-01T09:00:00` with `Europe/Berlin`), and the other form is refused (`FCMS2108` on `at`), and `DELETE .../schedule/{action}` cancels. `GET .../versions` lists `{ "items" }` newest first, `GET .../versions/{n}` reads one, `POST .../versions/{n}/restore` restores it and `GET .../versions/{a}/diff/{b}` answers `{ "from", "to", "changes" }`, all in one locale. `POST /content/{type}/bulk` takes the list's `filter[...]` parameters in the query string and `{ "operation", "entries", "items", "cap", "patch" }` in the body, and answers `{ "items", "cancelled" }` with an entry's status, id, version, warnings and, for one that failed, the problem the single call would have answered. `POST .../preview-token` mints a preview token for the entry (4.7). `GET /content/{type}/trash` lists the trash with the query grammar, `POST /trash/{id}/restore` restores and `DELETE /trash/{id}` purges. A save needs `If-Match` with one tag, `"7"` (`FCMS3130`, 428, when it is missing; `FCMS3131`, 400, for `*`, a list or anything else); publish, discard and version restore take it optionally, with `*` as none. The tag is strong, but a weak one, `W/"7"`, is read by its opaque value: a proxy that compresses responses, such as nginx with `gzip on`, weakens the `ETag` it passes on, and a client that echoes it would be refused on every save. This departs from RFC 9110, whose `If-Match` compares strongly and so never matches a weak tag; the tag names a version rather than the bytes of a representation, so a weakened one still names it. A response that holds a draft gives its version as `ETag`, unless a value came through the fallback chain, which can carry another locale's version, and a conflict is 412 (`FCMS3105`) with the current draft in `current`, in the response shape. A body is a JSON object that names a key once, read before the engine sees it (400 when it cannot be, 415 and `FCMS3107` for another media type), and a key a schedule or bulk body does not use is refused (`FCMS2108`). An unknown type is 404 on every route, and one the principal cannot read is the same 404 on a read; a write leaves it to the content service, since a principal may write without reading. A write and a read of versions take only `site` and `locale` from the query string, bulk its filter too, and `locale=all` is refused where one locale is needed. `IfMatch` in `FluentCMS.Core.Api.Common` is for the other groups that take a version.

**Idempotency keys as built** ([#2933](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2933)). `POST /content/{type}` and `POST /content/{type}/bulk` take an optional `Idempotency-Key` header: one value of 1 to 255 visible ASCII characters (`!` to `~`), compared exactly, or 400 (`FCMS3140`). A key belongs to the principal (kind and id) and the site of the request, so two callers never share one. It is kept in `cms_idempotency_key` as its SHA-256, with a SHA-256 of the request (method, path, query string, asked locale, content type, principal, site and body) and the response. The first request claims the key by inserting its row, so of two that send it at once one runs and the unique index refuses the other, on every database; a key already in use is answered from its row without a write. A repeat of the same request answers the stored status, `Content-Type`, `Location`, `ETag` and body with `Idempotent-Replayed: true`, and runs nothing. The same key with another request is 422 (`FCMS3141`), and a repeat while the first still runs is 409 (`FCMS3142`). Only a response below 500 is kept: a request that is refused with a problem, or fails, releases the key, so a corrected or later retry runs (a request the client abandoned keeps it until the lease ends, since its work may have committed), while a bulk request answers 200 when items fail, and that answer is kept. The route's own checks (type, parameters, locale) come first, so a malformed request claims nothing. `FluentCms:Idempotency:Window` (24 hours, at most 30 days) is how long a key is kept from its first request; a key past it is claimed afresh, and the recurring job `idempotency.retention` (hourly at minute 41) removes expired rows. A request whose instance stops before it answers holds the key until `InProgressTimeout` (5 minutes, at most the window) runs out; a repeat then takes it over and runs (event 9140). The work and the stored response are not one transaction, so a response that cannot be stored (event 9141) leaves the key held until the lease ends, and a repeat then runs again. The Management CORS policy allows the header and exposes `Idempotent-Replayed`, and the OpenAPI document lists both on the two routes.

**Identity, sites, settings, jobs, events, activity, plugins and me as built.** These groups, and the system group (4.15), need the `management` scope as the content group does. The routes read the request, and the in-process services check the permission through `ManagementAuthority` (`FluentCMS.Core.Authorization`), so a caller without it is 403 (`FCMS3103`), or 401 when not signed in. A permission for the instance is one that no site limits: a role of a site, a grant limited to sites and a key bound to sites do not hold it. Paged lists take `page` and `pageSize` (50, at most 100) and answer `{ "items", "total", "page", "pageSize" }`, other lists `{ "items" }`. A query parameter a route does not take is refused (`FCMS2201`), and so is a body key (`FCMS2108`). No caller hands out more than it holds: a role, a user's role or a key's role must give nothing beyond the caller's own grants, compared grant by grant with the pattern, sites, locales, fields and row rule (a grant that reaches several sites may be held through a different grant in each).

- Users, with `users.manage` for the instance, since a user belongs to no site: `GET /users` (`search` on user name and email), `GET` and `PATCH /users/{id}` (`userName`, `email`, `mustChangePassword`), `POST /users/{id}/deactivate` and `/reactivate`, `PUT` and `DELETE /users/{id}/roles/{roleId}`, `GET /users/{id}/sessions` and `DELETE /users/{id}/sessions/{sessionId}` through `UserSessions`, `GET /users/{id}/logins` and `DELETE /users/{id}/logins/{provider}` (4.7), and `POST /users/{id}/two-factor/reset`, which answers the user and needs a caller signed in with a second factor (4.7). Changing, deactivating or reactivating a user, ending its session, removing its login or resetting its two-factor needs every grant of the user's roles too, and giving or taking a role every grant of that role. A user does not deactivate itself or reset its own two-factor. Deactivating the last active user who holds `*` with no constraints, or taking from it the role that grants it, is refused (`FCMS3052`, 4.7).
- Invitations: `GET /invitations` lists the pending ones, `POST /invitations` `{ "email", "roleIds" }` sends one linked to `/account/accept-invitation` on the request's origin, and `DELETE /invitations/{id}` revokes; `InvitationService` needs `users.manage` for the instance and every grant of each role named.
- Roles, with `roles.manage` where the role applies (the instance for an instance-wide role, its site otherwise): `GET /roles` and `GET /roles/{id}` (also for `users.manage`, `apikeys.manage` and `members.manage`), `POST /roles` `{ "name", "description", "siteId", "requireTwoFactor", "grants": [{ "permission", "constraints" }] }`, `PUT /roles/{id}`, which replaces the name, the description and the grants, and `requireTwoFactor` when the body gives it, and `DELETE /roles/{id}`. A grant names a pattern the catalogue matches, sites (only its own for a role of a site), locales, fields and a registered row rule, and an empty list is refused. What a change adds and what it replaces or deletes must both be within the caller's reach. Built-in roles keep their names and are not deleted, and of Super Admin only `requireTwoFactor` changes (`FCMS3043`). An edit that drops `*` with no constraints from an instance-wide role is refused when it would leave no active user who holds it (`FCMS3052`, 4.8). `RoleService` writes through `CmsDbContext`, whose interceptor raises the permissions version, and deletes a role in one statement only while no user holds it, no key that is not revoked uses it and no member group is bound to it (`FCMS3044`), so the cascade drops no holder without its `UserRoleChangedEvent`, no live key without `api_key.revoked` and no group without its event. `role.created`, `role.updated` and `role.deleted` are activity rows of the role's site. `GET /permissions` lists the catalogue.
- API keys: `GET /api-keys`, `GET /api-keys/{id}`, `POST /api-keys` `{ "name", "class", "roleId", "siteIds", "typeNames", "expiresAt", "rateLimit" }`, which answers 201 with the key and its `secret` once, `DELETE /api-keys/{id}`, which revokes and keeps the row, `POST /api-keys/{id}/expire` `{ "at" }`, `POST /api-keys/{id}/rotate` `{ "overlapSeconds" }` (optional), which answers the key and its new `secret` once, and `PUT /api-keys/{id}/allowed-addresses` `{ "allowedAddresses" }` (4.7). `POST /api-keys` also takes `allowedAddresses`. `ApiKeyService` needs `apikeys.manage` for the instance for a key bound to no site and in each of its sites otherwise; a key out of reach is 404 and left out of the list, and a new key's role, as its binding narrows it, holds only what the caller holds, so a key bound to sites mints no key that reaches further.
- Members, with `members.manage` for the instance, which is apart from `users.manage`: `GET /members` (`search` on the address, `group`, `verified`, `active`, with paging), `GET /members/{id}`, `POST /members` `{ "email", "displayName" }`, which makes a member without a password whose address is not verified and mails it a link to choose one (409 `FCMS3173` while the link has nowhere to go, 4.7), `PATCH /members/{id}` `{ "displayName" }`, `POST /members/{id}/deactivate` and `/reactivate`, `DELETE /members/{id}`, `GET /members/{id}/sessions` and `DELETE /members/{id}/sessions/{sessionId}`, `GET /members/{id}/logins`, which lists the member's external logins as `{ "provider", "displayName" }`, and `DELETE /members/{id}/logins/{provider}`, which removes one and ends the member's sessions (404 `FCMS3185` when it has none, 409 `FCMS3184` for its last way to sign in, 4.7), and `PUT` and `DELETE /members/{id}/groups/{groupId}`. Deactivating and deleting end the member's sessions. A member is `{ "id", "email", "emailVerified", "displayName", "createdAt", "lastLoginAt", "deactivatedAt", "lockoutEnd", "groups" }`. `GET`, `POST`, `PUT` and `DELETE` on `/member-groups` and `/member-groups/{id}` take `{ "name", "description", "roleId" }`. A group hands the grants of its role to its members, so creating a group, changing one (with the role it has, since a new name changes what a row rule gives its members) and putting a member in one need every grant of that role, as handing a role to a user does (403); deleting a group and taking a member out of one only take away and need `members.manage` alone. Each change raises its event and is an activity row of no site (4.7).
- Sites, with `sites.manage` for the site: `GET /sites`, `GET` and `PATCH /sites/{id}` (`name`, and `defaultLocale`, an enabled locale of the site; `SiteUpdatedEvent`), and `GET`, `POST` `{ "code", "position", "enabled", "fallbacks" }`, `PATCH` and `DELETE` on `/sites/{id}/locales/{code}` (`SiteUpdatedEvent`, 4.6), where a fallback is another locale of the site and `fallbacks: null` is the implicit chain. The default locale stays enabled and is not removed, and a locale with entries, trashed ones included, is disabled rather than removed (`FCMS3136`). `site.locale-added`, `site.locale-updated` and `site.locale-removed` are activity rows of the site. Creating and deleting sites, and their host names, are the multi-site plugin's routes, with `sites.manage` for the instance (5.6).
- Settings: `GET` and `PUT /settings/{area}` for the site of the request and `/settings/{area}/defaults` for the instance defaults, with `settings.{area}.manage` there for both, since the read is what the admin edits. A read answers `{ "area", "siteId", "source", "version", "value", "secretsSet", "schema" }` from the redacted resolve, with the version as `ETag`; a write takes the whole document and needs `If-Match` with the version read (`"0"` for no row), and a row that has moved is `FCMS2808` (412).
- Jobs, with `jobs.manage` for the instance: `GET /jobs` (`status`, `type`, `queue`), `GET /jobs/{id}` with its attempts as `history` and never its payload, `GET /jobs/dead-letter`, `POST /jobs/{id}/retry` for a dead-lettered job (`FCMS3133` otherwise) and `POST /jobs/{id}/cancel` for a pending one (`FCMS3134` otherwise; a running job is left to finish). A cancelled job is dead-lettered with its `JobDeadLetteredEvent` in one transaction, so a schedule it carried closes as for any dead letter, and it can be retried.
- Events, with `jobs.manage` for the instance, as for jobs, since an error can name internals of the host whatever site its event came from: `GET /events/dead-letter` lists the failed outbox deliveries, newest first, as `{ "id", "eventId", "eventType", "handler", "attempts", "lastError", "occurredAt" }` and never the event. `POST /events/dead-letter/{id}/retry` queues one again with fresh attempts and `DELETE /events/dead-letter/{id}` removes it, both answering 204; a delivery that is not failed is `FCMS3139` (409) and an unknown one `FCMS3138` (404). `DELETE /events/dead-letter?handler={name}` removes every failed delivery to that handler, in batches, and answers `{ "handler", "removed" }`; without `handler` it is `FCMS2101`. `EventAdministration` checks the permission and writes the activity rows, of no site: `event_delivery.retried` and `event_delivery.removed` with the target type `event_delivery`, the delivery's id and the handler as label, and `event_delivery.purged` with the target type `event_handler` and the count, when it removed any.
- Activity: `GET /activity` with `actorId`, `actorKind`, `origin`, `action` (a trailing `*` for a prefix), `targetType`, `targetId`, and `from` and `to` with an offset, needs `activity.read` in the site of the request, and adds the rows of no site only for `activity.read` for the instance.
- Plugins: `GET /plugins` lists every plugin in plugin order with `name`, `version`, `sdkRange`, `dependsOn`, `enabled` for the site of the request, `contributions` (`fieldTypes`, the `permissions` of its permission providers, the `endpoints` of its groups as `{ "method", "route" }`, `jobs`, and `tools`, the names of the MCP tools it offers the caller in that site, 5.4) and `sites`, its enablement `{ "siteId", "slug", "enabled" }` in each site where the caller holds `plugins.manage`. Any caller with the `management` scope reads it, because the admin hides a disabled plugin's UI for every user. `PUT /plugins/{name}/sites/{site}` `{ "enabled" }` needs `plugins.manage` for that site and answers `{ "siteId", "slug", "enabled" }`; an unknown plugin is `FCMS1551` and an unknown site `FCMS2301`, both 404 (4.13).
- Me: `GET /me` is the caller, and for a user also its user name, email, `mustChangePassword`, `twoFactorEnabled` and roles; `GET /me/sites` lists the sites in which the caller holds a grant (4.6); `GET /me/sessions` and `DELETE /me/sessions/{id}` are a user's own sessions. `GET /me/two-factor` is `{ "enabled", "required", "pending", "recoveryCodesLeft" }`, `POST /me/two-factor/enrol` answers `{ "sharedKey", "authenticatorUri" }` (an `otpauth` URI named after the brand of the hosted pages), `POST /me/two-factor/verify` `{ "code" }` turns two-factor on and `POST /me/two-factor/recovery-codes` `{ "code" }` replaces the codes, both answering `{ "recoveryCodes" }`, and `POST /me/two-factor/disable` `{ "code" }` turns it off (4.7); `GET /me/logins` and `DELETE /me/logins/{provider}` are its external logins (4.7); a key or a service account is 403.

Codes `FCMS3040` to `FCMS3052` and `FCMS3132` to `FCMS3139`; the member routes have `FCMS3160` to `FCMS3174` and `FCMS3184` to `FCMS3186` (4.7).

**Members API as built** ([#2992](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2992)). `/api/members/v1` is a group of its own (`Members`), with the `auth` rate limit by client address, which the check before authentication counts as for `/connect` and `/account`, the CORS policy of `FluentCms:Security:Cors:Members:AllowedOrigins` (empty by default; `GET`, `HEAD`, `POST`, `PATCH` and `DELETE`, which removes an external login, with `Authorization` and `Content-Type`, and no credentials, so a browser sends no cookie), no output cache, and the surface `members`; it is not in the OpenAPI document. `/connect` and `/.well-known` answer the Members origins beside the Management ones. The routes read the request and `MemberAccountService` decides (4.7). `POST /register` `{ "email", "password", "displayName" }`, `POST /resend-verification` `{ "email" }` and `POST /forgot-password` `{ "email" }` answer 202 with no body whatever the address; `register` answers 404 (`FCMS3104`) while registration is off. `POST /verify-email` `{ "email", "token" }` and `POST /reset-password` `{ "email", "token", "password" }` answer 204, or 422 with `FCMS3166` for a token that is wrong, used or expired and for an unknown address alike. With a member's token, `GET /me` answers `{ "id", "email", "emailVerified", "displayName", "createdAt", "lastLoginAt", "groups" }` with `Cache-Control: no-store`, `PATCH /me` `{ "displayName" }` changes the display name (left out it stays, and an empty text clears it), and `POST /me/change-password` `{ "currentPassword", "newPassword" }` answers 204 and ends the member's sessions. `GET /me/logins` lists the member's external logins as `{ "items": [ { "provider", "displayName" } ] }` with `Cache-Control: no-store`, and `DELETE /me/logins/{provider}` removes one and ends the member's sessions, the token of the call included (404 `FCMS3185`, and 409 `FCMS3184` for the last way to sign in; #2993). Another caller is refused there: an admin user's token or a key with 403 (`FCMS3103`), no credentials with 401. A body is JSON (415 otherwise), a key the route does not take is refused, and a request that is wrong in itself is a 422: `FCMS3164` for an address that is none, `FCMS3165` for a password the policy refuses, `FCMS3167` for a wrong current password. A member's token on the Delivery API reads published content as far as its roles allow and is never cached: the output cache keeps a copy for a request without credentials and for an API key only, so a read that a row rule narrowed for one member is not served to another caller.

**Schema group as built.** `GET /schema`, `GET /schema/types/{type}` and `GET /schema/snapshot` need the `management` scope and `schema.read` in the site of the request, checked before any type is looked up (403, `FCMS3103`). `SchemaIntrospection` (`FluentCMS.Core.Schema`) then asks the evaluator what the caller may read in the site and locale of the request, which is what `TypeAccess` asks on a content route. A type it may not read is left out of `/schema`, and `/schema/types/{type}` answers it with the same 404 as an unknown type (`FCMS3104` on `type`). A field outside its allow-lists is left out, not blanked, and so is a relation to a type it may not read and the reverse side of a field that went, so that no name of such a type shows. A component or block that only what went used goes too, one that nothing uses stays, and a readable rich text field keeps every block, since an inline block may be of any. A condition or a slug source that reads a field that is left out goes too, so no name of such a field shows either. A condition goes whole, even when only one of its clauses reads such a field, because what is left of it would state a rule other than the one declared, and publishing still enforces a `requiredWhen` as declared. `/schema` answers `{ "types", "components", "blocks" }` and `/schema/types/{type}` one type, as the `SchemaDescription` records of the SDK, which a client can read back; `ISchemaIntrospection` there is the seam for the MCP plugin's schema resources. A type has `kind` and `localized`, and a collection or singleton also `scope`, `unpublishedReferences`, `maxVersions` and `trashRetentionDays`. A field has its flags, `formerNames`, `display`, `defaultValue`, `validation` (`required`, `unique`, the lengths, the range and `pattern`), `visibleWhen` and `requiredWhen` as `{ "operator", "field", "values", "operands" }`, `relation`, and the field type's `settings`, where a blocks field names its union in `blocks` and a select its `options`. Members that are null are left out. `/schema/snapshot` is the canonical `schema.json` text of what the caller sees, with its SHA-256 as `ETag`, so for a caller that reads everything it is the instance's snapshot and schema hash.

**Error model.** RFC 9457 `application/problem+json` on every error: `type` (`urn:fluentcms:error:{code}` until a docs domain exists), `title`, `status`, `detail`, `code` (`FCMS...`), `errors[]` of `{ path, code, message }` for validation, `current` on 412, `traceId`. **As built.** `FluentCMS.Core.Api.Common` maps the engine's exceptions to a status, a code and this body in one place (`ProblemMapper`) and writes it through an `IExceptionHandler` and `IProblemDetailsService` that `AddFluentCms()` registers; the host adds `app.UseExceptionHandler()`. `ErrorCodeRegistry` lists every `FCMS` code once with its status and title, and a test fails when a code in `src` is missing from it. A validation exception is 422 with `errors[]`, or the status its codes share in the registry (400 for query errors, 404, 409 or 412 for the few codes that say so) and `FCMS2100` when its errors have different codes. Access denied is 401 for a request without a signed-in principal and 403 for one with it. A hook's own code is kept, 422 unless the registry says otherwise. A code raised only while the host starts or a job runs is 500, and so is any other exception, with a generic `detail` and `FCMS3199`. The HTTP layer's own codes are `FCMS2100`, `FCMS3101` to `FCMS3105`, `FCMS3107` for a body of another media type (415), `FCMS3110` and `FCMS3111` for the rate limit and the body cap, `FCMS3121` to `FCMS3123` for the request bridge and the Delivery API, `FCMS3130` and `FCMS3131` for `If-Match`, and `FCMS3140` to `FCMS3142` for `Idempotency-Key`.

**OpenAPI 3.1.** `Microsoft.AspNetCore.OpenApi` with document transformers that add one schema per content type and per block, block unions as `oneOf` with discriminator, the filter grammar as documented parameters, security schemes (`oauth2` authorization code, `apiKey`), examples from the sample pack. Served per deployment at `/openapi/v1.json`; Scalar UI at `/scalar` (dependency approved in Q9).

**OpenAPI as built.** `AddFluentCms()` registers the document `v1` of `Microsoft.AspNetCore.OpenApi` with the Delivery and Management routes only, and the host maps it at `/openapi/v1.json` and Scalar at `/scalar` with `app.MapFluentCmsOpenApi()`, which returns a builder whose conventions, such as an authorization policy, apply to both (Appendix B leaves their authentication to the host). Both are FluentCMS endpoints of the `docs` surface: they carry the security headers, the coarse limit counts them before authentication and the `delivery` policy by client address, and the FluentCMS scheme does not run on them, so a key sent there is never looked up; they are still not held to the path base. The document is built once and kept by the output cache for a day: one copy per scheme, host and path base, which its server follows, or one in all with a public origin, keyed also by the schema hash, so that a store which outlives the process serves no copy made for another schema. The schema is built once per process, so there is no reload to evict on. `UseFluentCms()` runs authorization before the output cache, so a route that needs authorization, by its own requirement or the fallback policy, keeps its copy too and hands it only to a caller the policy lets through. Scalar.AspNetCore serves its bundle and helper beside the page from its embedded assets, so the page's `Content-Security-Policy` allows scripts from its own origin and, by a nonce made per request, the inline script that starts them, and styles from its own origin and inline, which the bundle injects (`default-src 'none'; script-src 'self' 'nonce-…'; style-src 'self' 'unsafe-inline'; img-src 'self' data:; font-src 'self' data:; connect-src 'self'`, the public origin added when it is set, `base-uri 'none'; form-action 'none'; frame-ancestors 'none'`). Scalar's CDN fonts, telemetry and agent chat would leave the origin, so they are off. This answers [#2856](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2856) of the review of 7 Nov 2026. They are mapped in Development, and elsewhere only with `FluentCms:OpenApi:Enabled = true`, because the document names every type and field of the schema to whoever can reach it; this spec did not settle it, so the default is the safer one. The routes come from endpoint metadata. A route under `{type}` carries a `ContentOperation` that says what it reads and answers, and a document transformer writes it once per content type in place of its `{type}` form, so a new content route needs only that metadata; any other route gets a tag from its group, an operation id from its method and path (a parameter without its constraint, so `{id:guid}` is `ById`, and a Delivery route prefixed `delivery`, as content's `deliveryListArticle` is, so a plugin's `GET /media/{id}` in both groups gives `getMediaById` and `deliveryGetMediaById`), its security and the problem response. `entry-{type}` holds the system fields as read-only and every field that is not hidden, each from its field type's fragment and nullable unless its storage has an empty value (4.3), a relation also as its populated entry and rich text also as a string. `input-{type}` is what a write sends: the same fields without the system ones, a relation as ids and rich text as a document. No field is required, since a draft may leave any out and `fields` selects. A block is `block-{name}` with `type` as a `const` and an optional `id`, and a block list is the `oneOf` with the `type` discriminator that `BlocksFieldType` writes. The query grammar of 4.4 is shared parameters, and each type has `filter-{type}`, a `deepObject` with the operators of each field it can filter, the fields of its single components as `component.field` and the system fields, and `sort-{type}`, which lists its sort keys. `oauth2` has the authorization code flow, with `x-usePkce` for PKCE, and client credentials, at the document's server, which is `FluentCms:PublicOrigin` when it is set, as the issuer is, and otherwise the request's origin and path base; `apiKey` is the `X-Api-Key` header; a Delivery route also accepts no credential. Examples come from `cms.OpenApi.AddExample<T>()`, which `AddBlogSample()` calls. The schema group answers the SDK's `SchemaDescription` records. The identity, site, settings, job, activity and me groups declare their bodies on each route, `Produces<T>` for the response and `Takes<T>` for a request body, which the route reads key by key, so its record only describes it, with `additionalProperties: false` since the route refuses a key it does not take; `Takes<T>` names no media type, because with one, as `Accepts<T>` gives, routing would refuse another with a bare 415 before the route answers its problem. A record is the service's (`UserInfo`, `SiteInfo`, `ApiKeyInfo` and the like) or, where a route wrote an anonymous object or a JSON tree, one of the same JSON in `AdminBodies`: `ItemList<T>` and `ItemPage<T>` for the lists, and `RoleResponse`, `CreatedApiKeyResponse`, `SettingResponse`, `JobResponse`, `MeResponse` and `RecoveryCodesResponse`. The settings read is `SettingResponse`, whose `value` follows the area's JSON schema under `schema`, and a write takes the document as an object. Enums are camel case names and numbers are numbers whatever the host's `JsonOptions` say, since FluentCMS writes them so and reads a body's numbers only as numbers. The system group answers `HealthReportResponse` at `/system/health`, its checks in order of name with the names of `HealthStatus` as they are, and the SDK's `StartupReport` at `/system/diagnostics`. A route of these groups declares the query parameters it reads with `Query(...)`, with their types, defaults and limits (`page` from 1, `pageSize` from 1 to 100 and 50 by default), and `ManagementQuery` accepts and reads only those beside the bridge's `site` and `locale`, so a parameter read without being declared fails every request to the route. Every route beside content also lists the bridge's `site` and, as `bridge-locale`, its `locale`, which such a route checks against the site's locales and reads nothing by.

**Rate limiting.** ASP.NET Core rate limiting with policies `delivery` (partition by key or IP), `management` (by principal), `auth` (strict, by IP). `RateLimit-*` headers and `Retry-After`. Per-key overrides from `cms_api_key.rate_limit`.

**CORS and headers.** Separate allow-lists for Delivery and Management from configuration and settings; no wildcard by default; security headers middleware (`X-Content-Type-Options`, `Referrer-Policy`, `Content-Security-Policy` with `frame-ancestors` for the hosted pages).

**Request limits.** Body size, page size cap (default 100), populate depth cap, filter node cap, upload caps (media plugin).

**HTTP protections as built.** `app.UseFluentCms()` adds the exception handler, routing, security headers, CORS, a coarse limit per client address, the request limits, authentication, the rate limiter of the endpoints, authorization, the step that drops the body of a `HEAD` and the output cache in that order, and `MapFluentCms()` refuses to run without it (`FCMS1104`). A host does not call `UseAuthorization()` itself; one that still does runs its policies a second time, which changes nothing but the cost, and `AddFluentCms()` registers the authorization services the step needs. The policies are token buckets per caller with limits from `FluentCms:RateLimits` (delivery 600, management 300, auth 20) as the rate per minute: a bucket holds the limit and fills again over a minute, so a quiet caller may send the limit at once and then one request per minute divided by the limit, and a burst at the turn of a minute is not let through twice, as it was with fixed one-minute windows. A bucket is kept as the time it is next full (the generic cell rate algorithm), so one number is its state and no timer runs per caller, and a clock that moves back moves the bucket with it; the coarse limit (`PreAuth`, 6000) runs before authentication on every FluentCMS endpoint, which routing has found by its metadata, so a plugin's routes are covered without registering their prefixes, and on any path under a group of core that no endpoint answers, such as `/connect/token`; `/health` counts in a partition of its own (4.15); a key's `fcms_rate_limit` claim replaces the delivery limit up to `Delivery:MaxKeyLimit`; the caller is read from the `FluentCms` authentication scheme when there is one, and IPv6 addresses are limited by their /64. Responses carry the draft fields `RateLimit-Limit`, `RateLimit-Remaining` (what may be sent now) and `RateLimit-Reset` (seconds until the bucket is full), as the bucket was right after it counted the request: the bucket records them for the request when it grants or refuses it, and they are not looked up again when the response starts, since the partitioned limiter disposes a bucket that has been full for ten seconds and a slow request would lose them ([#2965](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2965)). A refusal is a 429 problem (`FCMS3110`) with `Retry-After`, the seconds until the next request is let through, which the refusing lease carries. The caller is read once per request into an `ApiCaller` when the FluentCMS scheme has run: its kind, `sub`, rate limit claim and, for a key, the hash of its claims that the Delivery output cache keys its copy by, computed on first use; the client address is read once too and shared by the limit before authentication and the endpoint's. A partition is the caller alone, with its limit only when a key's own limit replaces the policy's, so a request builds no partition key or limiter factory of its own. A `/connect` or `/account` request, which the check before authentication has counted under the auth limit, passes the endpoint's limiter in a partition of its own that has no limiter: that limiter keeps one limiter per partition key, so sharing the caller's key would let the pass-through stand in for the caller's bucket on a hosted page, or the bucket count the request a second time ([#2968](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2968)). This answers items 8 and 10 of [#2860](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2860). The allow-lists come from configuration only; the settings half is not built. `/connect` answers the Management origins. Both policies allow the `X-Cms-Site` and `X-Cms-Locale` headers and expose `ETag`, `Location`, `Retry-After` and the `RateLimit-*` fields. A FluentCMS response other than Delivery's that sets no `Cache-Control` gets `no-store`, so a key's secret, recovery codes, `/me` and the diagnostics are kept by no browser or proxy. The body cap is `FluentCms:Limits:MaxBodySize` (2 MiB, `FCMS3111` on 413). It runs before authentication, where the authorization server reads the body of a token, introspection or revocation request, and holds a path under a group of core that no endpoint answers by its prefix, as the coarse limit does. The page size, depth and filter caps stay the engine's. Details are in `docs/host-configuration.md`.

**Rate limit by caller class as built (B-75).** `RequireCallerRateLimit()` (`FluentCMS.Plugins.Abstractions`) puts an endpoint or a group of a plugin under the policy `caller`, in place of the Delivery limit of `MapGroup`: a caller whose principal has the `management` scope is counted per principal at the Management rate, and any other per API key or client address at the Delivery rate, a key's own limit included, with the headers and the 429 problem of those policies. The rate limiter keeps its buckets per policy, so these endpoints count apart from the REST APIs. The GraphQL endpoint uses it (5.3).

### 4.11 Events and hooks

Rows 8.4, 8.16, 6.10.

**Event bus.** `IEventBus.PublishAsync<TEvent>` with `IEventHandler<TEvent>` subscribers, in-process. Events raised during a transaction are buffered and dispatched after commit, in `[HookOrder]` order; a failing handler is logged and does not affect the write. Handlers that must not be lost (webhooks, activity log, cache invalidation) subscribe through the outbox: the event is also written to `cms_outbox` in the same transaction and a runner job drains it, so a crash between commit and dispatch loses nothing.

**In-process handlers, as built.** The handlers of `IEventHandler<T>` stay awaited after the commit, because a caller that reads after a write expects their side effects, such as a cache eviction, to have run; moving them behind the response is declined. Each one is bounded instead. It runs off the caller's thread, so that one that blocks before its first await is cut off too, and the caller waits for it at most `FluentCms:Events:Handlers:Timeout` (default 5 seconds, the default of `OutboxOptions.JobEventTimeout`), with the `WaitAsync(timeout)` that bounds the handlers of a job event. Its token is the host's shutdown, and the dispatch cancels it when the timeout passes, before the next handler runs, so a cooperative handler stops then; one that does not is logged as abandoned (event 9615), left to end on its own, and the next handler runs. The handlers of an event run in a scope the dispatch creates, as the handlers of a job event do, never in the caller's, so a handler left running shares no `DbContext` or other scoped service with the request, which goes on to read its response. The abandoned handler keeps its scope until it ends, and the handlers after it run in a fresh one. Whatever it does after the caller went on, such as using the request's services through `HttpContext.RequestServices` once the request has ended, is logged (9617) and never thrown, so it neither reaches the caller nor surfaces as an unobserved task exception; a scope that fails while it is disposed is logged too (9618). The bound is per handler, so an event with several slow handlers waits up to the bound for each. A handler that ends after `FluentCms:Events:Handlers:SlowThreshold` (default 1 second) is logged as slow (9616), and every run is recorded in `fluentcms.event.handler.duration` (histogram, seconds) by `fluentcms.operation` (the event type), `fluentcms.handler` (the handler's class) and `fluentcms.outcome` (`succeeded`, `failed` or `timed-out`). This answers [#2883](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2883) of the review of 7 Nov 2026.

**Publishing inside a transaction, as built.** Outside a transaction of the content service, `IEventBus.PublishAsync` is refused with `FCMS2602` while another transaction is open in the scope, whether on `CmsDbContext`, on `SystemDbContext` or on a plugin's `PluginDbContext<TPlugin>`, because its end cannot be seen and an event that may yet roll back is never delivered. Core adds a transaction interceptor to every plugin context it registers, which remembers the contexts of the scope that began or joined a transaction, and the transaction probe asks whether one of them still has it open. A plugin publishes after its own transaction commits. An explicit `IEventBus.StageAsync(DbContext, ...)`, which would write the outbox rows into the plugin's transaction, is declined before 1.0 to keep the SDK small. The activity log (`FCMS2704`), settings (`FCMS2807`) and the Super Admin guard read the same probe, so they refuse inside a plugin's transaction too. This answers [#2885](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2885) of the review of 7 Nov 2026.

**Outbox dead letters and retention, as built.** A delivery that runs out of attempts is left as failed and stays in the dead-letter list, which the Management API lists, retries, removes and purges by handler (4.10). A row whose handler is missing on the instance that claims it, as when its plugin was removed, or whose plugin is disabled in the row's site (4.13), is put back without using an attempt for `MissingHandlerDelay` (5 minutes) at a time, but only until `OutboxOptions.OrphanAfter` (7 days) has passed since its event happened. Then it is moved to the dead-letter list with an error that names the handler, and the plugin when it is disabled, and is logged (9619); it is never deleted unseen. A failed row's `available_at` is when it failed. `OutboxOptions.FailedRetention` removes failed rows that long after they failed; it is null by default, so they stay until they are retried or removed, as dead-lettered jobs do, which `jobs.retention` never removes (4.12). Delivered rows go after `Retention` (7 days). Both are removed by the recurring job `events.outbox.prune`, hourly on the default queue with a 10-minute attempt timeout, in batches of `PruneBatchSize` (500, at most 1000) as `jobs.retention` does, and no longer by the drain, which runs every minute. A batch is claimed with one conditional `UPDATE ... WHERE id IN (...)` that checks every condition of the read again, under an owner of that claim alone, and read back by that owner, so two drains of one instance never take each other's rows. A handler that is cut off at `DeliveryTimeout`, or left by a stopping host, may still be running: the drain cancels its token before it settles the delivery, whatever the timer of the handler's own limit has done, and its scope and its token sources are disposed in a continuation of its task, once it ends, and a fault it ends with is logged (9620), as is a scope that fails while it is disposed (9621). This answers [#2884](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2884) and items 1 and 2 of [#2887](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2887) of the review of 7 Nov 2026.

**Events of an import, as built (B-92).** Everything an import writes carries `Origin.Import`, and the events of one run share a correlation id: `import:{run}`, or `import+webhooks:{run}` when the import was started with `webhooks=true`. The webhooks plugin enqueues no delivery for an event of `Origin.Import` whose correlation id does not start with `import+webhooks:`. The correlation id travels with the event through the outbox, so the choice needs no column, no SDK member and nothing kept beyond the request. The other handlers, such as cache invalidation and the activity log, hear the events of an import as they hear any.

**Catalogue.** Content: `EntryDraftSavedEvent`, `EntryPublishedEvent`, `EntryUnpublishedEvent`, `EntryDraftDiscardedEvent`, `EntryVersionRestoredEvent`, `EntryTrashedEvent`, `EntryRestoredEvent`, `EntryPurgedEvent`, `EntryScheduledEvent`, `EntryScheduleCancelledEvent`. Media (plugin): asset uploaded, updated, replaced, moved, deleted. Identity: user created, updated, deactivated, role changed, login linked, login unlinked, key created, key revoked, key expiry changed, key rotated, key allowed addresses changed, key address refused, login succeeded, login failed, member registered. Settings: `SettingsChangedEvent`. Sites: created, updated. Plugins: `PluginEnabledEvent`, `PluginDisabledEvent`. Infrastructure: `CacheInvalidatedEvent`, `JobFailedEvent`, `JobDeadLetteredEvent`.

**Hooks.** `IBeforeValidateHook<T>`, `IBeforeCreateHook<T>`, `IAfterCreateHook<T>`, the same pairs for Update, Delete, Publish, Unpublish, Restore; `T` is a content class or `ContentDocument` for all types. `HookContext` carries the `OperationContext`, the entry, the previous state, the changed field paths, `Locales` and `Cancel(code, message)`. A before hook may change the entry; an after hook gets a copy of what was written, and its changes are discarded (4.1). `Locales` lists the locales the operation acts on: every locale of the entry for a delete or a restore, whose entry holds one of them, and the entry's own for any other operation; a type that is not localized has the one locale `""`, as its events do. A purge runs no hooks (item 5 of [#2869](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2869)). Ordering: `[HookOrder(int)]` with plugin dependency order as the tiebreak (decision 49).

### 4.12 Job runner

Rows 6.6, 8.10, 10.7 (locks), 10.20 (Should); decision 40.

**Tables.** `cms_job (id, type, payload json, queue, site_id, run_at, priority, status, attempts, max_attempts, lease_until, lease_owner, last_error, created_at, created_by, origin, completed_at)`, `cms_job_attempt (job_id, attempt, started_at, finished_at, outcome, error)`, `cms_recurring_job (name, type, cron, payload json, queue, next_run_at, enabled)`. A recurring job has a `type` because a schedule must say which job it fires. Job `status` is `pending`, `running`, `succeeded` or `dead_lettered`. The claim is served by `ix_cms_job_claim` on `(queue, priority DESC, run_at, id)`, filtered to live jobs (`pending`, `running`), so finished jobs are never walked. `payload` is the payload as JSON (`JsonSerializerOptions.Web`); one whose type writes a scalar, such as the `int` of an `IJob<int>`, is stored as `{"value":12}`, because the `ISJSON` check of SQL Server takes only an object or an array, and the payload type tells the reader to unwrap it ([#2711](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2711)).

**API.** `IJob<TPayload>.ExecuteAsync(payload, JobContext)`; `IJobScheduler.EnqueueAsync`, `ScheduleAsync(runAt)`, `AddRecurringAsync<TJob, TPayload>(name, cron, payload, queue)`, which is safe to call at every startup on every instance. `IJobDeadLetters` lists dead-lettered jobs and retries one; it checks no permission, so `jobs.manage` is enforced by the caller. Jobs are registered with `cms.Jobs.AddJob<TJob, TPayload>("stable-name")`, and that name is what `cms_job.type` stores.

**Principal and actor.** A job always runs as `Principal.System` with `Origin.Job`. An actor in the payload (a payload that implements `IJobActorPayload`) does not replace the principal: it goes to `OperationContext.Actor`, which authorization never reads, so a payload cannot borrow a user's roles. The actor is built from kind, id and name only, with no claims. `OperationContext.AuditPrincipal` is `Actor ?? Principal`, and audit columns (`created_by`, `updated_by`, `deleted_by`, `author_id`) use it, so a scheduled publish records who scheduled it. A `System`, `Anonymous` or empty actor fails the attempt with `FCMS2507`.

**Runner.** A hosted service with a configurable worker count per queue. Claiming uses the provider dialect: `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` (PostgreSQL), `UPDLOCK, READPAST` (SQL Server), optimistic `UPDATE` that rechecks every condition of the read (SQLite). Leases with heartbeat; retries with exponential backoff and jitter; dead-letter after `max_attempts`; graceful shutdown; multi-instance safe without extra infrastructure. **Attempt timeout.** Off by default. `JobRunnerOptions.AttemptTimeout` sets a limit for every job, and `AddJob<TJob, TPayload>("name", attemptTimeout)` overrides it for one (`Timeout.InfiniteTimeSpan` turns it off). When an attempt passes its limit the runner stops renewing the lease and cancels `JobContext.CancellationToken`. A job that stops by throwing `OperationCanceledException` fails the attempt with `FCMS2508`, which is retried with backoff or dead-lettered by the usual rules. A job that ignores the token keeps its worker until it returns, but its lease lapses, so another worker retries it, or the sweep dead-letters it on its last attempt; a job that returns normally is a success. `IJobRunner` is an interface so a Hangfire adapter can replace it (**Adapters** below).

**Built-in jobs.** Scheduled publish and unpublish, trash purge, version pruning, activity retention, idempotency key retention, outbox dispatch (its own `outbox` queue), the mail a visitor asks for with an address, the reset of either pool and the verification mail of a member again (`members.send_mail` and `users.send_password_reset`, on their own `mail` queue with two workers an instance, so that the answer never waits for a send), webhook delivery (plugin, its own `webhooks` queue), media variant cleanup (plugin).

**Job retention.** A recurring job (`jobs.retention`, daily) removes succeeded jobs and their attempts once `JobRunnerOptions.SucceededRetention` (7 days) has passed since `completed_at`, in batches of at most `RetentionBatchSize` (500, at most 1000), each batch in one transaction. Pending, running and dead-lettered jobs and recurring definitions are never removed. It sets an attempt timeout, and it is safe to run on several instances and to run again.

**Wake.** A worker that finds nothing to claim waits `PollInterval` (5 seconds), or until its own instance adds a job of its queue that is due now: `IJobScheduler` wakes an idle worker of the job's queue after the insert, and the outbox kick wakes the `outbox` worker when it finds a drain job already pending. Each queue holds at most one wake per worker of the queue on the instance, so a burst wakes every idle worker once and no more. The wake is process-local, and the workers of other instances find the job on their next poll. An event with a webhook on an idle instance is then sent as soon as the drain and the send allow, rather than after two polls of about 5 seconds each. This answers item 5 of [#2887](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2887).

**Queue ages.** Each maintenance turn (`MaintenanceInterval`, 15 seconds) also measures how long the oldest job of each queue that is due and held by no worker has waited, `now - run_at` over the jobs of every instance, and keeps it in memory. The `job-runner` health check carries the ages in its data, in seconds by queue, and reports Degraded when one is above `FluentCms:Health:PendingJobAgeLimit` (15 minutes; `Timeout.InfiniteTimeSpan` for no limit), which leaves the instance in rotation and logs event 9104. The observable gauge `fluentcms.job.oldest_pending_age` (seconds, by `fluentcms.queue`) reports the same, with every queue of the instance's options at zero when nothing of it waits. So workers that all hang on jobs that ignore their tokens, while the maintenance loop keeps beating, show in both. This answers item 8 of [#2887](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2887).

**Errors.** The error an attempt stores and logs has every string, number and property name of the payload taken out, one of four or more characters wherever it appears and a shorter one as a whole word, since an exception message can quote the job's input. The names of the schema's content types, components and blocks and of their fields are kept, because they are not secret and an error such as "Content type 'article' not found" says little without them. This answers item 9 of [#2887](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2887).

**Adapters.** A Hangfire adapter replaces `IJobRunner`, and `IJobScheduler` too when it keeps its jobs elsewhere. Three things of core assume the built-in runner. The outbox kick reads `cms_job` for a pending `events.outbox.drain` job before it enqueues another, so with jobs kept elsewhere every commit that records outbox rows enqueues a drain job; the adapter dedupes them itself, or accepts them, since a drain that finds nothing is cheap. The wake belongs to the built-in runner and its scheduler, so the adapter's own polling sets the latency. The queue ages are measured by the built-in runner's maintenance loop, as its heartbeat is. This answers item 10 of [#2887](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2887).

**Management.** `/jobs` endpoints (4.10), permission `jobs.manage`. Cron parsing uses Cronos (MIT), approved in Q9. `fluentcms jobs list`, `run` and `retry` (6.1, B-91) go through `JobAdministration`; `jobs run <name>` adds a run of a recurring job due now, and with `--inline` runs it in the command's process as a job that process holds from the start (`JobStore.AddClaimedAsync`), with one attempt.

### 4.13 Plugin host, server side

Rows 8.1 to 8.4, 8.6, 8.10 to 8.16; decisions 11, 13, 14, 46 to 49.

**Shape.**

```csharp
[FluentCmsPlugin("media", Version = "1.0.0", SdkRange = "[1.0.0,2.0.0)")]
public sealed class MediaPlugin : IPlugin
{
    public void ConfigureServices(IPluginServiceBuilder plugin)
    {
        plugin.Services.AddScoped<IMediaService, MediaService>();
        plugin.AddFieldType<MediaFieldType>();
        plugin.AddPermissions<MediaPermissions>();
        plugin.AddSettings<MediaSettings>();
        plugin.AddModel<MediaModelContributor>();      // entities with prefix cms_media_
        plugin.AddMigrations(typeof(MediaPlugin).Assembly);
        plugin.AddJob<VariantCleanupJob, VariantCleanupPayload>("media.variant-cleanup");
        plugin.AddEventHandler<EntryPurgedEvent, ReleaseReferencesHandler>();
    }

    public void Configure(IPluginAppBuilder app)
    {
        app.MapGroup("/media").MapMediaEndpoints();   // under the CMS route group, CMS auth by default
        app.MapDelivery("/media").MapMediaDeliveryEndpoints();   // under /api/delivery/v1
        app.MapManagement("/media").MapMediaManagementEndpoints();
    }
}
```

**Registration.** Explicit `AddPlugin<T>()` wins and fixes order; `[assembly: FluentCmsPlugin(typeof(MediaPlugin))]` on referenced assemblies is discovered through the compile-time dependency context. No folder scan, no runtime loading.

**Validation at startup.** SDK range against the running SDK version, dependencies present, duplicate names; failure stops the host with `FCMS15xx` errors (`FCMS14xx` is authorization's).

**Plugin host as built.** The manifest is `[FluentCmsPlugin("name", Version = "1.0.0", SdkRange = "[1.0.0,2.0.0)", DependsOn = ["other"])]` on the plugin class, and the same attribute on an assembly, `[assembly: FluentCmsPlugin(typeof(T))]`, names a plugin class for discovery, as many as the assembly has. A name is lower kebab-case like a content type or a settings area, at most 32 characters, so that `cms_{name}__migrations` and the plugin's tables fit a PostgreSQL identifier. `Version` is a semantic version and, left out, the informational version of the plugin's assembly; `SdkRange` is required. `IPluginServiceBuilder` has the plugin's `Name` and the host's `Services`, and `IPluginAppBuilder` its `Name` and the root `Services`; core implements both and plugins only call them, so the contributions of B-61 are new members that break no plugin. For `IServiceCollection` the SDK references `Microsoft.Extensions.DependencyInjection.Abstractions`, and nothing of EF Core or ASP.NET Core. `cms.AddPlugin<T>(configure)` creates the plugin with its parameterless constructor and hands it to `configure`. Discovery reads the app's deps file (the first of `APP_CONTEXT_DEPS_FILES`, else `{entry}.deps.json`) once per process, loads the runtime assemblies of the libraries that depend on `FluentCMS.Sdk`, directly or through other libraries, and reads their assembly attributes; an app with no deps file on disk, such as a single-file one, discovers nothing and adds its plugins with `AddPlugin`. So does an app that sets the `AppContext` switch `FluentCMS.Plugins.DisableDiscovery` (`<RuntimeHostConfigurationOption Include="FluentCMS.Plugins.DisableDiscovery" Value="true" />` in its project file), which is for an app that references plugins it adds to some of its hosts only, as the data tests do (B-80); such a host says so in the log when it starts (event 9237). A plugin that is added and discovered is registered once, as added and where added; one added twice is a duplicate. **Order.** The plugins are taken as they came, the added ones in the order of their `AddPlugin` calls and then the discovered ones in the order of the deps file, and each is placed after the plugins it depends on, which move ahead of it when they come later; plugins with no dependency between them keep that order unless a dependency pulls one of them forward. `ConfigureServices` runs in this order inside `AddFluentCms`, after the host's callback and before core adds its own services, so a plugin's service replaces one that core adds only when none is there, and `Configure` runs in this order inside `MapFluentCms`, after the endpoint groups of core. The plugin host is the `IHookTiebreaker` (a host that registers its own keeps it): among hooks and event handlers of equal `[HookOrder]`, one of a plugin ranks by the plugin's place, and one of no plugin, the host's or core's, after every plugin's, keeping its registration order. A type belongs to the plugin whose `ConfigureServices` registered it, by implementation type or instance, or else to the plugin whose class is the only plugin class in the type's assembly, which covers a hook registered with a factory; the app's own assembly is left out of that, since its other types are the host's. `Configure` runs once, however many times `MapFluentCms` is called, and a discovered plugin whose constructor throws stops the host with its own exception. **Codes.** `FCMS1501` the type cannot be a plugin (no manifest or several on the class, an assembly attribute with a name instead of a type or one that cannot be read, or a discovered class that is abstract, does not implement `IPlugin` or has no public parameterless constructor), `FCMS1502` the name, `FCMS1503` the version, `FCMS1504` an SDK range that is missing or not interval notation, `FCMS1505` a running SDK outside the range, `FCMS1506` two plugins with one name, `FCMS1507` a missing dependency and `FCMS1508` a dependency cycle, whose message lists the cycle; each message names the plugin and its class. `FCMS1520` to `FCMS1527` are the contributions' (below), `FCMS1540` to `FCMS1544` the provider slots', `FCMS1550` to `FCMS1559` per-site enablement's (below), `FCMS1560` to `FCMS1579` the webhooks plugin's (5.2), `FCMS1580` to `FCMS1599` the media plugin's (5.1), `FCMS1600` to `FCMS1639` the MCP plugin's (5.4), `FCMS1660` to `FCMS1679` the multi-site plugin's (5.6), and `FCMS1680` to `FCMS1699` the AI assist plugin's (5.5). **SDK versions.** The running version is the informational version of `FluentCMS.Sdk` without its build metadata, and the range is parsed in core. A released SDK is held to the range exactly as NuGet holds a version. A pre-release of the SDK counts as the release it leads to when it is held against a bound that is a release, and is compared in full against a bound that is a pre-release: `1.0.0-preview.3` and `1.0.0-dev` satisfy `[1.0.0,2.0.0)`, `2.0.0-preview.1` does not, and `[1.0.0-preview.2,2.0.0)` refuses `1.0.0-preview.1`. NuGet's own precedence puts `1.0.0-preview` below `1.0.0` and `2.0.0-preview` below `2.0.0`, so a range written against released versions would refuse every dev and preview build of the line it names and take the previews of the next major version, which may break the plugin; the rule turns both around and loosens nothing for a release. The startup report and `/system/diagnostics` list the plugins in this order with name and version; the full manifest is the Management plugin list's.

**Plugin contributions as built.** `IPluginServiceBuilder` mirrors the core builders, each method doing what its `cms.*` counterpart does: `AddFieldType<T>()`, `AddPermissions<T>()` (an `IPermissionProvider`), `AddSettings<T>()`, `AddJob<TJob, TPayload>(type, attemptTimeout)`, `AddJobQueue(name, workers)`, `AddEventHandler<TEvent, THandler>()`, `AddOutboxHandler<TEvent, THandler>(name)`, `AddActivity<TEvent>(name, describe)`, `AddErrorCode(code, status, title)` and, for the MCP server, `AddMcpTools<T>()`, `AddMcpResources<T>()` and `AddMcpPrompts<T>()` (5.4), and `Providers` holds the provider slots (4.13, "Provider slots as built"). `AddJobQueue` adds a job queue whose worker count its factory reads from the host's services when the runner's options are built, so that it can come from the plugin's own options; a count the host sets for that queue, in `cms.Jobs.Options.Queues` or under `FluentCms:Jobs:Queues`, wins, and a name that is empty or longer than 64 characters throws. `FluentCMS.Plugins.Abstractions` adds `AddModel<T>()`, `AddMigrations(assembly)`, `MapGroup(prefix)`, `MapManagement(prefix)`, `MapDelivery(prefix)` and `MapProtectedResource(prefix, options)` (4.7, "Protected resources as built") as extension methods ([ADR 0003](../decisions/0003-plugin-contribution-contracts.md)). **Tables.** A model contributor maps the plugin's entities on an EF `ModelBuilder`. Each table and view is named `cms_{plugin}_...`, with the hyphens of the name as underscores, and is neither `cms_{plugin}__migrations` nor a table of core or of another plugin; `AddFluentCms` checks this by mapping the contributor on a model without conventions and stops with `FCMS1523`. A content type whose table a plugin maps stops the host with `FCMS1206`. The contributors join `CmsDbContext` after the system tables, excluded from its migrations, and build `PluginDbContext<TPlugin>`, a scoped context with the provider's conventions that the plugin's code queries. **Streams.** `AddMigrations(assembly)` registers the plugin's stream: the migrations of the assembly that name `PluginDbContext<TPlugin>` in `[DbContext]`, with the history table `cms_{plugin}__migrations`, which `IDataProvider.ConfigurePluginMigrations` points at the database. The streams run after the system stream and before the content stream, in plugin order, and a second assembly for one plugin is `FCMS1525`. A model ahead of its migrations stops the migration with EF's pending model changes error. The assembly holds a set per provider, in a namespace ending in `Sqlite`, `PostgreSql` or `SqlServer`, and the host takes the set of its provider (4.3, "Plugin migration sets per provider as built"). **Endpoints.** The groups are relative to the path base and run their endpoints inside the request bridge's `OperationContext`, with no output cache unless an endpoint adds one. `MapManagement(prefix)` is under `/api/management/v1` with what core's Management groups have: the `management` scope (401 without a principal, 403 without the scope), the Management rate limit and CORS policy, and the OpenAPI document. `MapDelivery(prefix)` is under `/api/delivery/v1` with what core's Delivery group has, its output cache included, and an anonymous caller read as its site's Public role (4.10, "Plugin Delivery groups as built"). `MapGroup(prefix)` is under the CMS group with the Delivery rate limit and CORS policy and the plugin's name as its surface, which keeps it out of the OpenAPI document. "CMS auth by default" means that its endpoints need a caller the `FluentCms` scheme signed in, a bearer token or an API key of any class, and answer any other with a 401 problem (`FCMS3102`); an endpoint that is public on purpose, such as the media plugin's asset bytes at `/media/{id}/{filename}`, opts out with ASP.NET Core's `AllowAnonymous()`, on the endpoint or on the group. It is an endpoint filter after the request bridge's, as the `management` scope is, so no host needs `UseAuthorization` for it, and it runs before the per-site gate, so an anonymous caller learns nothing of where the plugin is disabled. Credentials that are sent and refused are a 401 even where anonymous is allowed, as on Delivery. Being signed in is all the group checks: what the caller may do is the plugin's to check with `IPermissionChecker`. The coarse limit before authentication covers these routes as it covers core's (4.10), so a flood of keys sent to them is turned away before any key is looked up. This answers decision 4 of the review of 7 Nov 2026 ([#2847](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2847), [#2859](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2859)): a plugin's group is not anonymous-capable by default. The startup report does not list the endpoints that allow anonymous, because each is a choice written in the plugin's code rather than a setting an operator could change, and a warning at every start for a public media route would teach operators to ignore the warnings; the Management plugin list names every endpoint. A prefix under a group of core, one whose first segment is a route parameter, and one that does not start with a slash or ends with one are `FCMS1524`. So is a `MapDelivery` prefix whose first segment is a route parameter or names a content type. **Codes.** A plugin's code is upper case letters and four digits, neither core's nor another plugin's, with a status from 400 to 599 and a title (`FCMS1522`). A `PluginException(code, message)`, a `HookRejectedException` and a validation error with the code get its status and title; a code the plugin did not add is 422. **Field types.** A plugin type's name is neither core's nor another plugin's (`FCMS1521`). A setting of kind `FieldSettingKind.FieldReference` names a sibling field of the same type, component or block: schema validation refuses one that names no other field (`FCMS1520`), and schema introspection leaves the setting out for a caller who cannot read that field, as it does a slug's `source`. Such a setting is optional, as `source` is, so that a narrowed snapshot still loads; a plugin type that makes one required stops the host (`FCMS1526`). Core's `IFieldTypeRegistry` in the container has the plugins' types, and a host that builds its own `SchemaModel` builds it with that registry, as the test host does. The sample plugin of the tests (`tests/FluentCMS.Plugins.Sample`) uses every contribution and stays the fixture of the plugin host's tests; a plugin starts from the `fluentcms-plugin` template (6.2, B-94), which is not generated from it and uses the permissions, settings, hooks, model, migrations and Management endpoints of this section.

**Permission checks of plugins as built.** A plugin's service enforces its own permissions as core's services do, with `IPermissionChecker` (`FluentCMS.Authorization`, in the SDK): `AllowsAsync(permission, site)` and `RequireAsync(permission, site)`, which throws `PermissionDeniedException`, answered with 403, or 401 without a principal. A null site is the instance as a whole, which only a grant with no site limit reaches. A call outside an operation is refused with `InvalidOperationException` (`FCMS1408`, 2.3), so host code that acts as the system wraps it in `SystemOperation.Begin`. Core implements it with the checks of its Management services, so the endpoint carries the identity and the service decides. The webhooks plugin checks `webhooks.manage` with it.

**Problem descriptions for plugin surfaces as built** ([#2977](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2977)). A plugin surface that does not answer with problem details, such as MCP, asks `IProblemDescriber` (`FluentCMS.Plugins`, in the SDK) what the REST APIs would answer an exception with: `Describe(exception)` gives a `ProblemDescription` with the stable `Code`, the HTTP `Status` the code stands for, its `Title`, a `Detail` for people and the validation `Errors`. Core implements it with the mapping of its own problem details and the codes of the host, those the plugins added with `AddErrorCode` included, and reads from the current operation whether the caller is signed in, which tells a 401 from a 403. An exception that says nothing of its cause is `FCMS3199` with status 500 and a message that holds nothing of the exception, and the surface that asked logs it, since core's own handler never sees it.

**Per-site enablement.** `cms_plugin_site (plugin, site_id, enabled)` read through `IPluginState`. A disabled plugin's endpoints return 404 for that site, its hooks, events, jobs and MCP contributions are skipped, and the Management plugin list reports it disabled so the admin hides its UI.

**Per-site enablement as built.** `cms_plugin_site` is keyed on plugin and site and goes with its site. A plugin with no row for a site is enabled there, and a row of a plugin that is no longer registered says nothing. `IPluginState.IsEnabledAsync(plugin, site)` (SDK) reads it. Each instance reads the rows whole and keeps them for 30 seconds. A write through the Management API drops them at once, so the instance that wrote answers by the new state from the next request, and another instance within 30 seconds. In a site where a plugin is disabled:

- its `MapGroup`, `MapManagement` and `MapDelivery` endpoints answer 404 (`FCMS1550`), after the signed-in caller or the `management` scope is checked; a copy of a `MapDelivery` answer in the output cache goes with the outbox delivery of `PluginDisabledEvent`, which evicts the site;
- its hooks are skipped for an operation of that site, and its in-process event handlers for an event of that site;
- its outbox handlers get no row for an event of that site, as an in-process handler is not called, so what happens there while it is off is never delivered to it. A row recorded before it was disabled is owed to it: the drain puts it back without using an attempt, as it does a row with no handler (`OutboxOptions.MissingHandlerDelay`), and delivers it once the plugin is enabled there again. A row still waiting `OutboxOptions.OrphanAfter` (7 days) after its event happened is moved to the dead-letter list with an error that names the plugin and the site, so it is not lost, and is retried from there once the plugin is enabled (4.11);
- its jobs of that site complete without running, as succeeded. A job of no site is the instance's and runs. When the state cannot be read, the job is released as a stopping host releases it, without using an attempt, and waits one `PollInterval`, as the drain puts a delivery back (item 3 of [#2887](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2887)).

A hook or handler is the plugin's by the tiebreaker's rule above, and a job type or an outbox handler by the `AddJob` or `AddOutboxHandler` that added it. An activity row described with `AddActivity` is core's log and is always written. An event or job of no site is not gated. Contributions of the whole instance stay: field types, model, migrations, permissions and the settings schema, because every site shares the schema. The MCP plugin reads `IPluginState` and does not ask the MCP providers of a plugin that is disabled for the site of a request (5.4). Enabling or disabling raises `PluginEnabledEvent` or `PluginDisabledEvent` with the site as its source, which the activity log records as `plugin.enabled` or `plugin.disabled` with the target type `plugin` and the plugin's name. A write that changes nothing, such as enabling a plugin with no row, raises nothing. Log event ids 9230 to 9235.

**Provider slots.** Defined in the SDK, defaulted in core, replaceable by plugins or the host: `IFileStorage` (media plugin, local default), `IEmailSender` (core; SMTP through MailKit, plus a log-only sender for Development; Q9), `ISearchIndex` (search plugin), `IDistributedCache` (BCL memory default), `IDistributedLock` (core, `cms_lock` table default), `IImageProcessor` (media plugin), `IChatClient` and `IEmbeddingGenerator` from Microsoft.Extensions.AI (AI assist plugin).

**Provider slots as built.** `IProviderSlots` (`FluentCMS.Providers`, in the SDK) has `Use<TSlot, TImplementation>()`, which fills a slot with a singleton of the implementation, and `cms.Providers` is core's. The slots core fills are `IEmailSender` (SMTP, the log or a refusal, 4.7), `IDistributedLock` (`cms_lock`, 4.15), `IDistributedCache`, whose default is the framework's `MemoryDistributedCache`, and `IOutputCacheStore`, whose default is the framework's in-memory store, the registration `AddOutputCache()` makes, since its type is internal, and `ISearchIndex`, whose default `NoSearchIndex` means no index, so that a query with `q=` is refused until the search plugin fills it (4.4, "Search as built"); `IFileStorage` and `IImageProcessor` come with the media plugin, which declares them with its defaults (B-64, B-65) through `IPluginServiceBuilder.AddProviderSlot<TSlot, TDefault>()`: such a slot is filled and replaced like core's, and the report lists its default with the declaring plugin; a slot declared twice, by two plugins or by a plugin and core, stops the host with `FCMS1544`. The AI assist plugin declares `IAiClientFactory` the same way (B-83, 5.5): a factory that gives the `IChatClient` and the model of a site, where the plan had `IChatClient` itself as the slot, because a single client cannot follow the settings of each site; the interface is in the plugin, since the SDK takes no Microsoft.Extensions.AI dependency. `IEmbeddingGenerator` comes with semantic search (9.16). What fills a slot is settled when `AddFluentCms` ends: the host's `Use`, else a plugin's, else a service registered for the slot directly (by the host before `AddFluentCms` or in its callback, or by a plugin's `ConfigureServices`), else the default. A `Use` takes out every other registration of its slot, so it wins whatever the order of the calls and the registrations, and of two `Use` calls of one caller for a slot the last wins. Two plugins that replace one slot stop the host with `FCMS1540`, which names the plugins and their implementations, unless the host chooses with its own `Use`; a `Use` of a type that is not a slot stops it with `FCMS1541`, one whose implementation is abstract or an interface with `FCMS1542` when it is called, and one made after `AddFluentCms` has settled the slots, which would change nothing, with `FCMS1543`. A registration that the framework makes for a slot on core's behalf (Razor Pages adds the memory cache) is not the host's and gives way to the default. A registration after `AddFluentCms` comes last and wins, as for any service, and the report says so. A plugin calls the same `Use` on `IPluginServiceBuilder.Providers`, which core fills with `ProvidersBuilder.For(name)`, the replacements of that plugin. The startup report and `/system/diagnostics` list the slots in the order core declares them under `providerSlots`, each with `slot`, `implementation` (the type in use; for a factory the type it returns, or null when it throws), `source` (`default`, `host`, `plugin` or `services`) and `plugin`. Codes `FCMS1540` to `FCMS1544`; no event ids.

**Settings.** `[Settings("media")] sealed class MediaSettings` is persisted per site in `cms_setting (site_id, key, json, updated_at, updated_by)` with instance defaults and per-site overrides. `ISettings<T>.GetAsync(site)` reads it; `SettingsChangedEvent` follows writes; the Management API exposes get and put per area with the JSON schema of the type so the admin can render it.

**Settings as built.** `cms.Settings.Add<T>()` registers a class that has `[Settings("area")]` and a parameterless constructor, and a bad one stops the host (`FCMS2801`). Its permission `settings.{area}.manage` is in the catalogue. `cms_setting` also has `id` and `version`, and two unique indexes (one over `key` where `site_id` is null, one over `site_id, key` elsewhere); a site's rows go with the site. A read resolves a whole document: the site's row, else the instance row, else the code default, and a stored row the class can no longer read is skipped with a warning in the log, so one bad row never takes a site down, and a key that a stored document lacks, such as one added since or one marked `[Required]`, takes its code default. Reads are cached per scope for `cms.Settings.CacheDuration` (30 seconds): a write on the instance drops the cache of its area at once, on every scope, and another instance sees it when its own reads expire, which is the most it can lag.
