A write needs `settings.{area}.manage` for the site, or from a grant with no site limit for the instance defaults (`SettingsAccessDeniedException`), goes through a context of its own, so it saves nothing the caller has tracked and drops nothing the caller has tracked, and replaces the whole document with one statement that names the version it read, starting again when another writer got there first, so the last of two writes stands; a caller that names the version it read (0 for no row; the Management endpoint's `If-Match`) gets `FCMS2808` when the row has moved, for a reset too. A document is refused with `FCMS2803` (not a JSON object, too large or not JSON), `FCMS2804` (a key the type does not have, one error per key) or `FCMS2805` (a value of the wrong type or one that breaks a validation attribute, on the class, its nested objects, the items of lists and the values of dictionaries, with the path of the key such as `limits.port` or `many[2].port`), and the error names the key and never the value. After the commit it raises `SettingsChangedEvent`, whose source carries the site (empty for the instance defaults), so the activity row is the site's; the event holds the area and nothing of the values. The row, its event and its activity row are not atomic: the event is raised after the commit, so a failure in between leaves the write without them. A write inside a transaction is refused (`FCMS2807`), and an unknown site is `FCMS2806`. A property of the class itself that is marked `[SettingsSecret]`, or named like a secret, is stored with the rest of the document but appears in no event, activity row, log line, validation message or schema default, and the schema marks it `writeOnly`; `[SettingsSecret]` on a property below the top level stops the host (`FCMS2801`), and so does a nested property named like a secret, because only the top level is kept out of reads, events and schemas (`[SettingsSecret]` is the real control; a name is also taken for a secret as a safety net, by a rule of its own that is narrower than the scrubber of events and logs, which reads free text: the name is read as words, ignoring digits and a version marker at its end, by its C# name as well as its JSON name, and is a secret when its last word is `password`, `passphrase`, `secret`, `token`, `pwd` or `apikey`, or its last two are `api`, `private`, `secret`, `access`, `signing` or `encryption` followed by `key`, `client secret` or `connection string`, plurals included, so `Keywords`, `SortKey` and `AccessKeyId` are not); an error under a secret names the secret's key and nothing below it, since a dictionary key may be as secret as its value; encrypting it at rest is left to the database. A document written without a secret key keeps the stored value, because the admin never sees a secret and must not wipe it by sending its form back, while a key that is sent, even as null, replaces it. The stored value is that of the document the read resolved to, which is what the caller was shown: the row being replaced, or for a site with no row of its own the instance default. A row that the class cannot read was skipped by the read, so it gives nothing, whether it is the row of the site (the site then takes the instance default's secrets) or the instance row. What a site inherits is a copy: rotating the secret on the instance does not reach a site that has saved its own row. A stored secret that the class would no longer accept, written under an older shape, is not kept: it takes its code default and a warning in the log names the key, never the value. The Management endpoint reads through a redacted resolve: the document without its secret keys, its source (site, default or code), the version of the row a write to that site would replace (0 when it has none, even if the document came from the instance default, and the version of the row that is there even if it was skipped because the class cannot read it, so that an unreadable row can be saved over), and the secret keys that hold a value in a stored document. The schema of a type is `System.Text.Json`'s, with the validation attributes of its properties added.

The name rule for secrets applies only to a property whose value can be text or key material, which a secret is ([#3079](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3079)): a Boolean, an enum, a number of up to 64 bits, a date, a time or a duration is never a secret by its name, alone, nullable or as the items of a list, so the AI assist plugin's `maxOutputTokens`, an `int`, is returned by a read, has its default in the schema and is not `writeOnly`. A string, a dictionary of any values (its keys are text), bytes as the items of any list or array (`byte[]`, `List<byte>`, `sbyte[]`), `Memory<byte>`, a `Guid`, an `Int128`, a `UInt128`, a `decimal`, an object and a type that enumerates itself stay under the rule, alone or in a list. The check of a nested property named like a secret follows the same rule, so a nested `int MaxTokens` does not stop the host. `[SettingsSecret]` still marks a property of any type.

**Manifest exposure.** `GET /api/management/v1/plugins` lists name, version, SDK range, dependencies, contributions (field types, permissions, endpoints, jobs, tools) and enablement per site. As built (4.10), `tools` names what the MCP tool providers of each plugin offer the caller (5.4).

### 4.14 Transfer and schema snapshot

Rows 10.9, 10.10, 1.19.

**Export package.** A zip, written and read as a stream: `manifest.json` (format version, FluentCMS version, schema hash, site and its locales, created at, options, the list of sections), `schema.json`, `content/{type}.jsonl` (one line per entry with both states), `settings.json` (without secrets), `roles.json` (optional) and `plugins/{plugin}/` for what a plugin contributes, which for the media plugin is the asset and folder metadata and, optionally, the files. Users, members, API keys and the activity log are never in a package. A package is of one site. `POST /transfer/export` answers the zip as a stream, with `transfer.manage`.

**Import.** `POST /transfer/import` takes the zip as its body and answers a stream of progress lines that ends with the report of creates, updates, skips, conflicts and errors. Schema hash check (equal, or compatible when the instance has every type and field the package uses), upsert by id, per-type selection, import into the site of the request, and a dry run with the same report. The import writes through the content service as the system in an import mode that keeps the ids, times and published state of the package, so validation, hooks, references, versions and the activity log behave as for any write. Each entry is its own transaction, and an import that stopped is finished by running it again. Everything an import writes carries `Origin.Import`, and the webhooks plugin delivers nothing for it unless the import asks for webhooks.

**No jobs, no stored package (10.3, P7).** The first design ran export and import as jobs with a download from `/transfer/export/{jobId}`. Core has nowhere to keep a package that every instance can reach, so both are streamed within the request instead.

**Plugin data (10.3, P11).** `ITransferContributor` in the SDK (`FluentCMS.Transfer`) lets a plugin write sections on export and read them on import, with the dry run. The media plugin implements it in M6; the other first-party plugins can follow without a change to the SDK.

**Left out of the first package.** `references.jsonl`, since references are derived from the fields on import, and versions, which the list of sections in the manifest lets a later release add.

**Transfer as built (B-92).** [#3015](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3015). No migration, no job and no stored package: both endpoints run in the request, and a caller that goes away cancels them.

- `POST /api/management/v1/transfer/export` takes an optional JSON body, `{ "types": [..], "globalTypes": true, "publishedOnly": false, "roles": false, "options": { "mediaFiles": "true" } }`, and answers `application/zip` as it is written. It needs `transfer.manage` in the site of the request and reads as the system, hidden fields too, outside the trash. One export of a site runs at a time across every instance, through `IDistributedLock` (`transfer.export.{site}`), and a second is refused with `FCMS3205` (409). The activity row `transfer.exported` names the site, the types and the count of entries.
- The package, format version 1: `manifest.json` (`formatVersion`, `fluentCmsVersion`, `schemaHash`, `site` with id, slug, name and default locale, `locales`, `createdAt`, `options`, `sections`), `schema.json` (the snapshot of 4.2), `content/{type}.jsonl`, `settings.json`, `roles.json` when asked, and `plugins/{contributor}/`. A line of content is `{ "id", "locales": [{ "locale", "status", "draftVersion", "publishedVersion", "createdAt", "createdBy", "updatedAt", "updatedBy", "publishedAt", "firstPublishedAt", "draft": {..}, "published": {..} }] }`, with each state's fields as a version holds them, shared ones included; `draft` is left out by `publishedOnly`, and `published` for a locale that is not published. `settings.json` is the site's own documents, redacted as the Management API shows them; `roles.json` the site's roles that are not built in, with their grants.
- `POST /api/management/v1/transfer/import` takes the zip as its body (`application/zip` or `application/octet-stream`), up to `FluentCms:Transfer:MaxPackageSize` (1 GiB; `FCMS3204`, 413). The body is kept in a temporary file deleted when the request ends, because a zip is read from its end. Query: `dryRun`, `overwrite`, `missingLocales=refuse|skip`, `webhooks`, `roles`, `types` (comma separated) and `options` (`name=value` pairs for the contributors). It needs `transfer.manage` in the site of the request and imports into that site, and a package that has the file of a selected global type needs it for the instance, whatever size the file declares, as writing a global type does (4.8); a site's administrator imports the other types with `types`. One import of a site runs at a time across every instance (`transfer.import.{site}`), and a second is refused with `FCMS3207` (409). Before any write and before the answer starts it refuses a body that is no package, a file of it larger unpacked than the limit, a file that holds more than its zip entry declares, or a `settings.json` or `roles.json` it cannot read (`FCMS3200`), a format other than 1 (`FCMS3201`), a schema the instance cannot hold (`FCMS3202`, with an error per type or field) and a locale the site does not allow (`FCMS3203`, unless `missingLocales=skip`). Every file of a package is read through one stream, `PackageEntryStream`, which refuses with `FCMS3200` once a file yields more bytes than its entry declares, so the sizes the import checks are the sizes it reads. `System.IO.Compression` reads a stored file to its compressed size whatever size it declares, and stops a deflated one at its declared size, so each file whose compressed size is larger than its declared one is read through before any write, which costs no more than the package. The schema is taken when its hash is the instance's, or when the instance has each selected type with the same kind, scope and localization, and every field of those types and of the components and blocks they use, with the same field type, multiplicity, localization and target; more types, fields or blocks in the instance are fine.
- The answer is `application/x-ndjson`: a line `{ "section", "counts" }` when a section ends, `{ "section", "done" }` every 100 entries, and a last line `{ "report": { "dryRun", "total", "sections", "items" } }`, where each count is `created`, `updated`, `skipped`, `conflicts` and `errors`, and each item is `{ "section", "id", "outcome", "code", "message" }`, in the order the import took them. The sections are the content types by name, `@settings`, `@roles`, `@plugins` (a package folder that no contributor enabled in the site reads, `FCMS3214`) and those the contributors name. The order is settings, roles, contributors, then content, so that an entry can refer to what a contributor brought.
- Import mode: each entry runs in a scope and a transaction of its own, through `ContentService` as `Principal.System` with `Origin.Import`, which the service lets the system principal use only while the import's scoped mode is set; `SystemOperation.Allows` is unchanged. The caller is the operation's `Actor`, so versions and activity name who imported. An entry the instance lacks is created under the package's id; one it has is skipped when every locale has the package's `updatedAt` (to the microsecond, which every provider keeps) and status, is a conflict when a locale is newer in the instance unless `overwrite=true`, and is otherwise updated. An id that belongs to another type or site is `FCMS3210`, and one in the trash `FCMS3211`. The published state of each locale is written as the draft and published, a locale the package does not publish is unpublished, and a draft that differs is then written over it, each through the steps of any write: validation, hooks, references, versions, events and cache eviction. A publish in import mode takes its references' targets in whatever state they are, since the package brings them too. Once written, the rows of `cms_document` take the package's `createdAt`, `createdBy`, `updatedAt`, `updatedBy`, `publishedAt`, `firstPublishedAt` and an `Unpublished` status, and an entry the import created takes the package's draft and published version numbers, its own versions renumbered to them; an updated entry keeps its own numbering. A dry run runs the same steps and rolls each entry's transaction back, which takes its events and outbox rows with it, and counts the assets that the contributors' dry run reports as there, so its report is the import's. An activity row `transfer.imported` carries the counts; a dry run writes none.
- Plugin data: `ITransferContributor` (SDK, `FluentCMS.Transfer`) with `Name`, `ExportAsync(ITransferExport)` and `ImportAsync(ITransferImport)`, added with `IPluginServiceBuilder.AddTransferContributor<T>()`. A contributor runs only in a site where its plugin is enabled, writes and reads only `plugins/{Name}/`, gets the options, the dry-run and overwrite flags and both sites, and reports each item with a `TransferOutcome`. A name that is not lower kebab-case or that two contributors share stops every transfer (`FCMS3230`).
- Spans `transfer export` and `transfer import`, codes `FCMS3200` to `FCMS3230` (the media plugin adds `FCMS3220` and `FCMS3221`), log event ids 9180 to 9187.

**Decisions as built (B-92).** Settled for [#3015](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3015):

- Global types are in a package unless `globalTypes` is false, and their entries are compared by id across sites like any other.
- Users, members, API keys, the activity log, schedules, versions and the trash are in no package. A `createdBy` or `updatedBy` the instance does not know is kept as the id it is.
- Roles go into a package and come out of it only when asked, and a grant limited to the site of the package is limited to the site of the import. The caller needs `roles.manage` in the site and every grant the role had and gets, as an edit of its own would, so an import is no way to raise one's own grants; the write is then the import's.
- The settings of an area the instance does not have are an item error (`FCMS3213`), a document equal to the site's is skipped, and the caller needs `settings.{area}.manage` in the site.
- The export request may write synchronously to the response body, because a zip written to a body that cannot seek ends each file that way.

**The commands as built (B-101).** [#3024](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3024). `fluentcms export --output <file> [--site <slug>] [--types <list>] [--published-only] [--roles] [--media-files]` and `fluentcms import <file> [--dry-run] [--site <slug>] [--types <list>] [--overwrite] [--webhooks] [--missing-locales refuse|skip] [--roles] [--json]` run in project mode, where the host calls `TransferExporter` and `TransferImporter` itself as the system with `Origin.Cli`, or with `--url`, where the tool calls the two endpoints with the key in `Authorization: ApiKey` and the site, by slug or id, in `X-Cms-Site`. Both modes build the same request (`--media-files` is the option `mediaFiles=true`, and the options are an empty object without it), so they write the same package but for the manifest's `createdAt`. A package is written to a temporary file beside the target and moved over it once complete, so a failed export leaves the target as it was. An import prints the progress lines as text on standard error and the report as text on standard output, or with `--json` the endpoint's last line `{"report":{..}}` unchanged, in both modes alike (`ImportOutput` in Core, which the tool uses too). It exits 1 with `FCMS3250` when the report has a conflict or an error, on a dry run too, so a pipeline can stop on it. A refusal keeps its code in both modes: the endpoint's problem code first (`FCMS3200` to `FCMS3207`, `FCMS2301` for a site that does not exist), and inside the host the same codes from the services' `ValidationException`. The tool adds `FCMS3248` (the package cannot be read), `FCMS3251` (any other refusal of the instance; a 401 and a 403 say what the key needs), `FCMS3252` (the instance cannot be reached) and `FCMS3253` (an answer that is not JSON lines or ends before its report). The key is never written out, and of the address only its scheme, host and port, in `FCMS3252`. The HTTP client has no timeout, since a package takes as long as it takes; Ctrl+C stops it. `import --roles` is added to the issue's options, so that a package exported with `--roles` can bring them.

**Schema tools.** `fluentcms schema snapshot` from code or from an instance; `fluentcms schema validate`; `fluentcms schema diff` between the code, a running instance's introspection, a schema file or the applied history, with a non-zero exit on drift so CI can gate deploys. The diff marks destructive changes and reports a field removed beside a field added of the same kind as a possible rename, with the `[FormerNames]` line to add; `migrations add` prints the same report and refuses a destructive change without `--allow-destructive` (10.3, P5). `fluentcms schema apply` brings the content tables of a host whose types are data up to its schema (4.3, 10.3, P4).

**Schema tools as built (B-97, [#3020](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3020)).** `schema snapshot [--output <file>]` writes the canonical snapshot of 4.2, inside the host from its schema and, with `--url`, from `GET /api/management/v1/schema/snapshot` byte for byte, so both give the same bytes for a key that reads every type. `schema validate` builds the host's schema and prints every `FCMS12xx` error on standard output, then `FCMS3320` on standard error, and exits 1. `--schema <file>` checks a file: in the tool's own process with no host, unless `--project` or `--assembly` names one, and then inside the host with its field types. Without a host a field type that core does not register, such as the media plugin's, is accepted unchecked and named in a note. `schema diff` reads from the schema that is deployed to the new one, so `added` is what a deploy adds and a destructive change is what it would lose: from the instance of `--url` (read with `schema.read`), the newest row of `cms_schema_history` (`--applied`) or a schema file (`--schema`) to the host's schema, and from `--url` or `--applied` to a schema file when `--schema` is given beside them. `--url` with a schema file needs no host; `--url` alone asks the host for its snapshot with `schema snapshot --output` into a file of the tool's. It prints the report of 4.2, one line of JSON with `--json` (`{"changes":[{"kind","family","type","field","formerField","properties","destructive","text"}],"possibleRenames":[{"family","type","from","to","attribute"}]}`), and exits 0 when nothing differs and 1 with `FCMS3322` on standard error when something does. An empty history is `FCMS3323` and a history that cannot be read or holds another shape `FCMS3324`. `migrations add` prints the report from the schema on the model snapshot to the host's, from nothing for the first migration, and refuses with `FCMS3327`, writing nothing, when a change is destructive or a field is renamed, unless `--allow-destructive` is given; a model snapshot written before it carried the schema leaves the change unknown, which is refused the same way with `FCMS3328`.

### 4.15 Infrastructure and operations

Rows 10.3, 10.5 to 10.8, 10.11 to 10.15.

**Configuration.** Everything under the `FluentCms` section (`FluentCms:Database:Provider`, `FluentCms:Database:MigrateOnStartup`, `FluentCms:Security:*`, `FluentCms:Delivery:Cache:*`, `FluentCms:Jobs:*`), bound to options with data annotations and `ValidateOnStart`. Environment variables map through the standard `__` separator, which is how the reference host is configured. Secret providers (user secrets, Key Vault, AWS Secrets Manager) are the host's configuration builders; core takes no dependency on them.

**Health.** `/health/live` (process) and `/health/ready` (database, storage provider, job runner heartbeat, plugins) through `Microsoft.Extensions.Diagnostics.HealthChecks`; detailed JSON at `/api/management/v1/system/health` behind a permission.

**Observability.** One `ActivitySource` (`FluentCMS`) with spans for content operations, hooks, jobs, webhooks and MCP calls; one `Meter` with request counts by surface, query duration, job latency, cache hit ratio, webhook outcomes; structured logs with event ids and `FCMS` codes. Exporters are the host's choice. Nothing is sent to the project.

**Health and observability, as built.** `FluentCmsHealthCheckTags` (`FluentCMS.Diagnostics`, in the SDK, so plugins tag their checks) has two tags, `live` and `ready`, and `/health/live` and `/health/ready` run the checks tagged with each through `Microsoft.Extensions.Diagnostics.HealthChecks`, with no authentication and no rate limit policy of the endpoints. They stay reachable without credentials, because the image's `HEALTHCHECK` and an orchestrator's probes call them, so they are kept cheap and bounded instead: the FluentCMS authentication step skips the `health` surface, so a key sent there is never looked up, and the check before authentication counts them per client address in a partition of their own, apart from the coarse limit, with `FluentCms:RateLimits:Health:PermitLimit` (1200 a minute, twenty a second from one address, far above what an orchestrator sends one instance). A path under `/health` that no endpoint answers counts there too. A refusal is the same 429 problem, and the probes carry no `RateLimit-*` headers. Where the network allows, probes should still be restricted to the orchestrator and the load balancer. This answers decision 16 of the review of 7 Nov 2026 ([#2847](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2847), [#2855](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2855)). They answer 200 for Healthy and Degraded and 503 for Unhealthy, with the status as the only body, never a description or an exception. Core registers `process` (live), `database` (ready, one row of a system table, so it also fails when the system migrations did not run) and `job-runner` (ready); `services.AddHealthChecks().AddFluentCmsReadyCheck<T>("name")` adds a ready check with a five second timeout (it is in Core, because the SDK has no reference to the health check abstractions; a plugin that references only the SDK calls `AddCheck` with the tag). The job runner beats in memory at every turn of its maintenance loop (`JobRunnerOptions.MaintenanceInterval`, 15 seconds), and ready is Unhealthy when the last beat is older than `FluentCms:Health:JobRunnerHeartbeatTimeout` (default 2 minutes; `Timeout.InfiniteTimeSpan` leaves it out for an instance that runs no jobs) or when the runner has not started, and Degraded when the oldest pending job of a queue has waited longer than `FluentCms:Health:PendingJobAgeLimit` (4.12). `GET /api/management/v1/system/health` needs `system.read`, an instance-level permission: it is in the catalogue and checked with `InstanceAccess`, so a role of one site or a grant limited to sites does not reach it, nor does a key bound to sites or a Delivery or Preview key. It runs every check and answers 200 with `{ status, totalDurationMs, checks: [{ name, status, durationMs, tags, description, error }] }`, whatever the status; descriptions and exception text pass through `SecretScrubber`. The one `ActivitySource` and the one `Meter` are both named `FluentCMS` (`FluentCmsTelemetry.Name`), and nothing is exported: a host subscribes with `AddSource` and `AddMeter` from its own OpenTelemetry setup, and Core takes no dependency on it. Spans are named `content {operation}` (create, update, patch, delete, restore, purge, publish, unpublish, discard-draft, schedule, cancel-schedule, versions, version, restore-version, diff, get, query, query-trash, count, bulk), `hook {kind}` (one per run of the hooks of a kind, with `fluentcms.hooks` their number) and `job {type}` (with `fluentcms.queue`). The MCP plugin adds `mcp {tool}` for a tool call (5.4). They carry `fluentcms.operation`, `fluentcms.type` (the name the schema resolves, and not on jobs) and `fluentcms.site`, and never a value of content; a job's span has the Error status when the attempt throws. The instruments are `fluentcms.requests` (counter, by `fluentcms.surface` of delivery, management, connect (with `/.well-known`), account, health or docs (the OpenAPI document and Scalar), and `fluentcms.status_class`, counted with the status the client got, including a refusal an endpoint throws), `fluentcms.query.duration` (histogram, seconds, by the `fluentcms.type` the schema resolved, and not recorded for a query refused as invalid), `fluentcms.job.latency` (histogram, seconds, the length of an attempt by `fluentcms.operation` the job type and `fluentcms.outcome`), `fluentcms.job.oldest_pending_age` (observable gauge, seconds, by `fluentcms.queue`, 4.12), `fluentcms.event.handler.duration` (histogram, seconds, the length of a run of an in-process event handler by `fluentcms.operation` the event type, `fluentcms.handler` and `fluentcms.outcome`, 4.11), and `fluentcms.cache.requests` (counter by `fluentcms.cache` and `fluentcms.result`) with `fluentcms.cache.hit_ratio` (observable gauge by `fluentcms.cache`); the Delivery output cache reports its lookups as `delivery`. Every `[LoggerMessage]` in Core has an event id, in a range per area that follows the `FCMS` families: 90xx identity (the authorization server 9001 to 9003 and 9005, the hosted pages 9004, admin SSO 9040 to 9044, the member pool 9070 to 9076, member providers 9090 to 9094), 91xx host (health 9101 to 9104, the problem handler 9110, email 9120, the startup report 9130 to 9132, the command runner 9170 to 9172 of the range 9170 to 9179), 92xx plugins (per-site enablement 9230 to 9234), 93xx storage (migrations 9301, schema history 9311), 94xx content (9401, scheduled jobs 9411, trash purge 9421), 95xx jobs, 96xx events, 97xx activity, 98xx settings and 99xx seeding. The event name is the method name, and a message carries the `FCMS` code where one applies.

**Multi-instance.** Stateless API; Data Protection and signing keys persisted to the database by default; jobs lease through the database; `IDistributedLock` defaults to a `cms_lock` table and gets a Redis provider in `FluentCMS.Caching.Redis`; output cache in memory by default or Redis; media on shared storage.

**Multi-instance as built.** `IDistributedLock` (`FluentCMS.Providers`, in the SDK) has one method, `TryAcquireAsync(name, timeout)`, which returns a handle that releases the lock when disposed, or null when the timeout passed first (`TimeSpan.Zero` tries once, `Timeout.InfiniteTimeSpan` waits until the lock is free); the handle's `Lost` token is canceled when the lock is lost while held. Core's default, added with `TryAdd` and with no options, keeps one row per name in `cms_lock (name, owner, acquired_at, lease_until)`. A holder takes a 30 second lease and renews it every 10 seconds while it holds the lock, so a holder that stopped without releasing it blocks the others until its lease runs out, and the next holder takes it over with a warning (event id 9160). Taking the lock is an update conditional on the row being free or its lease over, so of two that try at once one wins, and a waiter only reads the row while the lock is held, polling from every 50 milliseconds up to once a second. A free lock keeps its row with no owner, so only the first use of a name inserts, and lock names are a fixed set, one per task rather than one per item. A renewal that finds another holder, or that fails until the lease has run out, cancels `Lost` (9161); a renewal or a release that fails is logged (9162, 9163), and a release changes nothing once another holder has the lock. Leases are read against each instance's clock, so instances keep their clocks in step, as the job leases already need. The Data Protection ring is in `cms_data_protection_key` (4.7), so instances on one database read each other's cookies and protected payloads, and the keys survive a restart. Data Protection makes a key when its ring has none it can use, and instances that started together on an empty ring each made one; one that had read only its own kept it for up to a day, refusing the others' cookies once the two minutes in which Data Protection reloads the ring for an unknown key were over. The key manager now makes a key under the lock `fluentcms:data-protection-key`, after reading the ring again, and returns a key that another instance made in the last five minutes (the clock skew Data Protection allows) instead of making a second; when the lock is not free within 10 seconds it makes the key without it (9164). The wait blocks the thread Data Protection asks on, since `IKeyManager` is synchronous, so it covers a live holder, which makes its key within a second, and not one that stopped without releasing the lock, whose 30 second lease it used to wait out. A synchronous `TryAcquire` on `IDistributedLock` is declined: the default lock is a table read through EF Core's asynchronous calls, so it would only move the blocking, and every provider of the slot would have to implement it (item 6 of [#2887](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2887)). The changes that can take Super Admin from its last holder run under `fluentcms:super-admins` (4.7), and fail rather than go on without it. The Delivery output cache is the `IOutputCacheStore` slot, in memory by default, which evicts only the copies of its own instance; a host that registers a shared store, such as `Microsoft.AspNetCore.OutputCaching.StackExchangeRedis`, evicts for every instance, and the key's schema hash keeps instances on different schemas apart (4.10). Each instance keeps its own copy of the sites and their locales, of plugin enablement and of settings, and sees a change another instance made within 30 seconds (4.6, 4.13); role changes are seen on the next request everywhere, since every request reads the role stamps (4.8). The Redis provider in `FluentCMS.Caching.Redis` is still to come.

**Reverse proxy.** Forwarded headers, `PathBase` option for sub-path hosting, HTTPS redirection and HSTS options; documented for Nginx, Caddy, YARP, Azure Front Door and Cloudflare.

**Reverse proxy as built.** The options are `FluentCms:ReverseProxy` (`ReverseProxyOptions`, `cms.ReverseProxy` in code) and `UseFluentCms()` applies them first, in this order: forwarded headers, the path base, HSTS, the HTTPS redirect, then the pipeline of 4.10, so the pre-auth limiter and everything else that reads the client address or scheme sees what the proxy forwarded. A host no longer calls `UsePathBase` or `UseForwardedHeaders`. Forwarded headers are off by default; when `ForwardedHeaders:Enabled` is set, `X-Forwarded-For` and `-Proto` are read and `-Host` only with `ForwardHost` and for a host of `AllowedHosts`, from the `KnownProxies` and `KnownNetworks` listed (loopback only when both are empty), up to `ForwardLimit` hops (1). `PathBase` is a path such as `/cms`; every FluentCMS endpoint group (the ones `MapFluentCms` maps, so also one added later) is served only under it, and the same path without it is a 404 problem, written after the security headers and CORS. The gate holds a request to the path base when routing found a FluentCMS endpoint or its path is under a prefix of a group, which covers the authorization server's own paths. Routes the host maps itself (its own endpoints, `/openapi/v1.json`, `/scalar`, `/media`) are its own concern and are not held to it. The HTTPS redirect is off by default, because behind a proxy that ends TLS it loops unless the scheme is forwarded, and takes `HttpsRedirection:Port` (left out, the framework finds the port from `HTTPS_PORT` or the server's address, and redirects nowhere if it finds none); proxy addresses and networks must be written in full, so `1` is refused rather than read as `0.0.0.1`; HSTS is on outside Development and off in Development, with `Hsts:MaxAge` (30 days), `IncludeSubDomains` and `Preload`. A bad value stops the host with `FCMS1101` and its key. Setup for each proxy is in `docs/reverse-proxy.md`.

**Public origin as built.** `FluentCms:PublicOrigin` (`cms.PublicOrigin` in code; the configuration wins) is the address users reach the instance at, such as `https://cms.example.com`. It is an absolute URL with no user, query or fragment, `https` outside Development, whose path is empty or `FluentCms:ReverseProxy:PathBase`, which is added when it is left out; anything else, a path without a path base included, stops the host with `FCMS1101` and the key. When it is set, the password reset and invitation links, the issuer, the endpoint URLs of the discovery document, and the OpenAPI document's server and with it its `oauth2` URLs are built from it and the path base, so neither `Host` nor `X-Forwarded-Host` reaches them, and tokens carry that issuer, so setting or changing the origin refuses the tokens issued before; the endpoints still answer under any host that host filtering lets through. Without it they follow the request's scheme, host and path base, so Production refuses to start (`FCMS1105`, `PublicOriginCheck`) unless `FluentCms:Security:AllowRequestOrigin` is true, which is for an instance whose `AllowedHosts` or proxy admits only its own names; the startup report then lists `public-origin-unset`, and other environments start and list nothing for it. Host filtering runs before the forwarded headers and sees the proxy's `Host`, so with `ForwardHost` on, `X-Forwarded-Host` is applied only for a host of `AllowedHosts` (copied into `ForwardedHeadersOptions.AllowedHosts`), and for any host when `AllowedHosts` is `*` or unset. Code `FCMS1105`.

**Startup diagnostics.** A `StartupReport` (providers, plugins and versions, pending migrations, schema drift, insecure defaults, environment) logged once at start and served at `/api/management/v1/system/diagnostics`.

**Startup diagnostics as built.** The report is made once per start, after the migrations, by a hosted service that logs it at Information with event id 9130 (the report as JSON in the message, and its counts as properties) and one warning with event id 9131 per insecure default; a report that cannot be made is logged (9132, the exception type and its scrubbed message) and replaced by a reduced one that carries the type of the failure, so the endpoint still answers; before any report exists it is a 503 (`FCMS3106`). The report types are public records in the SDK (`FluentCMS.Diagnostics`). It holds the environment, the FluentCMS version, the data provider with its version and the resolved `MigrateOnStartup` and `SchemaSync`, the FluentCMS assemblies in the process, the plugins (an empty list until the plugin host exists), the pending migrations of every stream, the schema hash in code against the newest row of the history (applied hash, time and version, and what differs), and the insecure defaults. Those come from the production startup checks, which are asked in every environment so the report and the refusal cannot disagree (the public origin and key ring checks refuse in Production only, so that other environments keep their own lines), and from nine more: a generated signing key that `AllowGeneratedSigningKey` accepted, the key ring unencrypted where it is not refused (`data-protection-keys-unencrypted`: in every environment but Production, and in Production with `AllowUnencryptedKeyRing`), the Development email sender, the HTTPS redirect off in Production, `AllowedHosts` of `*` or unset in Production (`allowed-hosts-any`, read from the host filtering options, so any `Host` is answered), `FluentCms:PublicOrigin` unset in Production and accepted by `AllowRequestOrigin` (`public-origin-unset`, so links in emails and the issuer follow the request's host), schema sync outside Development (`schema-sync-outside-development`, 4.3), forwarded headers trusted from every network, and the direct login of members turned on in Production (`member-direct-login`, 4.7). A plugin adds its own with an `IInsecureDefaultSource` (`FluentCMS.Diagnostics`, in the SDK), which the report asks in every environment, so it decides itself where a setting is insecure; the webhooks plugin reports `webhooks-private-networks` and the MCP plugin `mcp-open-client-registration` (5.4). Free text goes through `SecretScrubber`, and the report holds no connection string, key or password. `GET /api/management/v1/system/diagnostics` serves that same report, so a change after the start does not show until the next one. It needs `system.read`, an instance-level permission: a role of one site, a grant limited to sites and a key bound to sites are refused with 403, and so is a key without the management scope. There is no switch to turn CORS or the rate limits off, so neither can be reported as off.

**Doctor as built (B-97, [#3020](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3020)).** `fluentcms doctor` makes the same report inside the host before any hosted service starts, so nothing listens, migrates or writes; it reads the database only for the pending migrations and the schema history. It prints the report as text, or with `--json` as one line in the shape of `/system/diagnostics`, and on standard error why the host would not start (`FCMS3325`: a schema with errors, a production check that refuses in Production, a migration set of another provider among the streams a start checks, or a report that could not be made), the pending migrations (`FCMS3303`), and a warning when the FluentCMS assemblies have different versions (`FCMS3326`). It exits 1 for a refusal or a pending migration, which makes it the pre-upgrade check of row 11.12; obsolete usage stays the compiler's warning until the first member is obsolete. A report that cannot be made is logged with event id 9172. With `--url` it reads `GET /system/diagnostics` with `system.read`, prints it as text or, with `--json`, as the instance sent it, and judges the pending migrations and the versions the same way.

**Backups.** Documented per database and storage provider in `docs/backups.md`. For a host on SQLite, `fluentcms backup` writes an online snapshot of the database and the files of the local media storage into one archive, and `fluentcms restore` puts them back. On PostgreSQL and SQL Server both commands refuse and name the guide, since the database's own tools do it better. A backup is not built on export packages, which leave out users, keys and the activity log (10.3, P6).

**Backups as built (B-101).** [#3024](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3024). No migration and no package. `fluentcms backup --output <file>` and `fluentcms restore <file> [--force]` are project commands that run inside the host. The snapshot is the SQLite provider's: Core declares the internal slot `IDatabaseFile` (`FilePath`, `Snapshot`, `IsInUse`, `Restore`) and sees it through `InternalsVisibleTo` to `FluentCMS.Data.Sqlite`, whose `SqliteDataProvider` fills it; PostgreSQL and SQL Server do not, so both commands refuse there with `FCMS3240`, which names `docs/backups.md`. `Snapshot` runs SQLite's online backup (`SqliteConnection.BackupDatabase`) with a busy timeout and up to ten tries, since a reader of a database in WAL mode is turned away for a moment while the running host checkpoints, and leaves the copy, written beside the archive rather than in the temporary folder, in the rollback journal, one file. The archive is a zip with `backup.json` (format 1, the FluentCMS version, the provider, the schema hash in code, the applied migrations of every stream, the time, the storage of new uploads and the number of media files), `database/fluentcms.db` and `media/`, with the files under the local storage's root as they lie there, taken after the database, so every asset of the snapshot has its file: a deleted asset keeps its file for the retention of 5.1. Files ending in `.tmp`, an upload the local storage is still writing, are left out. The media plugin registers the `DirectoryInfo` of that root under the service key `FluentCMS.Media.LocalRoot`, which Core reads, since neither references the other; a host without the plugin backs up its database alone. When the slot's storage is not the local one, the files still on the local disk are in the archive and the command says that the others are not. `restore` asks everything before it writes: an archive that is no backup of the format 1 of a host of the same provider (`FCMS3242`), a newer FluentCMS than the host's by SemVer precedence (`FCMS3243`), media files with no local storage to take them (`FCMS3247`) or a name that leaves the root (`FCMS3242`), a database file that exists without `--force` (`FCMS3244`), one that another connection has open (`FCMS3245`: with the exclusive locking mode, the first read is refused while any connection of a host in WAL mode is open; a host that keeps the rollback journal holds no lock while idle and is not seen, which the guide says), and media files over files the root has without `--force` (`FCMS3246`). It unpacks the database beside its file, moves the database it replaces aside (and back when a later step fails), deletes the `-wal`, `-shm` and `-journal` files, moves the snapshot into place and puts it back in WAL mode when the provider uses it, then writes the media files over the root's, leaving the others (a file that cannot be written ends it with `FCMS3307` and the count written, to run again with `--force`), and lists the migrations then pending of every stream, to apply with `fluentcms migrate` or a start. A file that cannot be read is `FCMS3248`, and an output that cannot be written `FCMS3307`. Codes `FCMS3240` to `FCMS3253` of the range `FCMS3240` to `FCMS3259`; no log event id.

**Supported platforms.** Linux and Windows containers, Azure App Service, AWS ECS, Kubernetes, bare `dotnet run`. .NET 10 LTS baseline.

### 4.16 Reference host

Rows 10.2, 11.5 (sample data); decision 3.

`src/FluentCMS.Host`: an ASP.NET Core app referencing `FluentCMS.Core`, all three data providers (chosen by configuration), every first-party plugin and, later, the admin at `/admin`. Content types come from a schema file loaded through the fluent builder (`FLUENTCMS_SCHEMA=/config/schema.json`) or from the built-in sample pack (`FLUENTCMS_SAMPLE=blog`) for evaluation; see Q2. SQLite with zero configuration by default. Multi-stage Dockerfile on the chiseled `aspnet:10.0` image, non-root, multi-arch; a compose file with PostgreSQL and MinIO; health probes; migrations on start for SQLite and an init container pattern (`fluentcms migrate`, with the CLI in M6) documented for the others.

**Reference host as built.** `src/FluentCMS.Host` references Core, the three data providers, the Blog sample and the plugins that have code, and adds each with `cms.AddPlugin<T>()`: the media plugin (5.1) with the S3 storage package, the webhooks plugin (5.2), the MCP plugin (5.4), the multi-site plugin (5.6), the GraphQL plugin (5.3) and the AI assist plugin (5.5), which is off until a site names a provider and opts fields in. `ReferenceHost.Build(WebApplicationBuilder)` sets it up from configuration alone, so environment variables are enough, and maps `UseFluentCms`, which includes `UseAuthorization`, `MapFluentCms` and `MapFluentCmsOpenApi`, whose document and Scalar UI stay off outside Development unless `FluentCms:OpenApi:Enabled` is true. `FluentCms:Database:Provider` is `Sqlite` (the default), `PostgreSql` or `SqlServer` (`FCMS3150` otherwise), and `ConnectionStrings:FluentCms` is the connection string: `Data Source={content root}/fluentcms.db` by default on SQLite, and required on the others (`FCMS3151`). `FLUENTCMS_SAMPLE=blog` adds the Blog types and `cms.AddBlogSample()` (`FCMS3152` for another value), and `FLUENTCMS_SCHEMA` loads a schema file through `SchemaBuilder.LoadSchemaFile` (`FCMS3153` when the path does not exist; a file with errors stops the host with the `FCMS12xx` codes of the builder); both may be set. On SQLite either one calls `cms.AllowSchemaSyncOutsideDevelopment()` (4.3), so their tables are created and extended at start in every environment. On PostgreSQL and SQL Server neither calls it, so nothing is applied at start and `FCMS3154` no longer stops the host; `fluentcms schema apply` brings the content tables up to the schema (4.3, B-98), and until it has, startup validation stops the host with `FCMS1301`, which names that command. Any key of `FluentCms:Media:S3` calls `media.UseS3` with that section, so new uploads go to that bucket; a section without a valid bucket stops the host at start with the options' message, and without the section media stays on the local disk. The Dockerfile, built from the repository root, compiles on the build platform for `TARGETARCH`, so `buildx` builds amd64 and arm64 without emulation, and runs on `aspnet:10.0-noble-chiseled-extra` (the extra variant for the ICU and time zone data that localized content and schedules in a time zone need) as the non-root `app` user. It sets `ConnectionStrings__FluentCms=Data Source=/data/fluentcms.db` on a `/data` volume and lifts no Production refusal, so `docker run` with nothing set stops before it touches the database with `FCMS1102`, `FCMS1105` and `FCMS1106`, each naming its setting and its opt-in; `docs/docker.md` shows an evaluation run that sets the public origin and the two other opt-ins, and the production setup. `FluentCms:DataProtection:Certificate:Path` and `Password` name a PKCS#12 file with an RSA key, which the host passes to `ProtectKeysWithCertificate` before `AddFluentCms`, since the image cannot call it itself (`FCMS3155` when the file cannot be read or holds no RSA private key); Azure Key Vault reaches the image as a certificate the platform mounts, and `ProtectKeysWithAzureKeyVault` needs a host of its own. Its `HEALTHCHECK` runs the host with `--probe`, which asks `/health/ready` on the container's own port and path base, since the image has no shell or `curl`. A new instance gets its first administrator from `fluentcms user create` (6.1), which the image runs too, or, for an instance that is set up from its environment alone, from a confidential client with Super Admin registered from the environment, which invites one. A deployment sets `FluentCms__PublicOrigin`, which links in emails and the issuer are built from (`FCMS1105` otherwise), and `AllowedHosts` (`allowed-hosts-any` otherwise), and email goes through SMTP when `FluentCms:Email:Smtp:Host` is set (4.7). `docs/docker.md` is the guide. `src/FluentCMS.Host/compose.yaml` (B-99) runs the image on PostgreSQL 15 with its media in MinIO and `FLUENTCMS_SAMPLE=blog`, as six services: `postgres` and `minio`, each with a volume and a health check; `minio-init`, which makes the bucket `fluentcms-media` and a MinIO user whose policy allows that bucket only, and which the host signs with; `migrate` (`fluentcms migrate`) and then `schema-apply` (`fluentcms schema apply`) with the host's image and database settings, two services because the image runs one command per container and has no shell, both idempotent and so run on every `up`; and `cms`, which starts when both steps and `minio-init` have ended with 0 and `postgres` and `minio` are healthy, on `127.0.0.1:8080`. The public origin is `https://localhost` and `AllowedHosts` `localhost`, both overridable, and the passwords and keys come from `.env`, which is git-ignored: `.env.example` leaves them empty and sets the two opt-ins of an evaluation, named as such, and each value is `${VAR:?message}`, so compose refuses to start without it. MinIO publishes no images any more, so the file uses `pgsty/minio`, pinned by the digest the media tests use, and its own `mc` for the init step. The `Compose` workflow, which runs on pushes to `feature/*` branches and which CI calls on pushes to `main` and manual runs but not on pull requests, builds the image, checks the refusal without `.env`, starts the file with throwaway values and an override that stands in for a TLS proxy and registers a public client, waits for `/health/ready` with a bound and dumps the logs on failure, checks that both steps ended before the host started, makes an administrator and a management key with the CLI in the container, signs the administrator in through the hosted pages with PKCE, publishes an article through the Management API and reads it through the Delivery API, uploads an image that it finds in the bucket and reads back, checks that the wait fails with MinIO stopped, and checks that a second `up` on the same volumes applies nothing and serves the article. The Dockerfile copies every project the host references before the restore. The admin at `/admin` waits for its milestone. Codes `FCMS3150` to `FCMS3155`.

**Commands in the reference host (B-90).** `Program.cs` calls `app.RunFluentCmsCommandAsync(args)` after `ReferenceHost.Build`, so `docker run <image> fluentcms migrate` applies the migrations of every stream and ends the container with the command's exit code, which is what an init container runs; `--probe` is unchanged. A command serves nothing, so it needs none of the Production settings that a start refuses without (`FCMS1102`, `FCMS1105`, `FCMS1106`), only the database. A configuration error of `ReferenceHost.Build` still ends the process with its code and exit code 1. `docs/docker.md` shows the init container, and since B-98 the second one that runs `fluentcms schema apply` after `migrate` for a schema file or the sample. The data tests run the image's host on each provider with a schema file and with the sample: `migrate`, a start that stops with `FCMS1301`, `schema apply`, a second `schema apply` that finds nothing, and a start whose report shows no drift.

**GraphQL in the reference host (B-75).** The host references `FluentCMS.Plugins.GraphQL` and adds `GraphQLPlugin` after the other plugins, so `/graphql` serves the types of `FLUENTCMS_SAMPLE` or `FLUENTCMS_SCHEMA`, and a host with neither answers a schema with no content field. Its options are `FluentCms:GraphQL` (5.3), and in Production introspection needs `schema.read`.

**Seeding.** `ISeeder`, `SeedContext` and `ISeedRunner` are in the SDK (`FluentCMS.Seeding`). A seeder writes through the public services, so permissions, versions, events, activity, trash and scheduling behave as in real use. It runs as the system principal with the `Seed` origin and the full service scope, so trust comes from registering it: only seeders added with `cms.Seeding.Add<T>()` run, at boot and through `ISeedRunner` alike, and a seeder registered on the container in any other way is ignored. `cms.Seeding.RequireLocale("fr")` makes the default site allow a locale before the first seeder runs (sites cannot be managed through the API before M2) and writes a `site.locale-added` activity row when it adds one; without a seeder added the host refuses to start (`FCMS2901`). `cms.Seeding.RunOnStart` (default true) and `ClaimTimeout` (default 15 minutes, `FCMS2902` if not positive) are the other options. With no seeder added nothing is registered and boot does no extra work.

**Seeding runs once per version.** The seeders run in `StartAsync` after the migrations, on the default site and in its default locale, in registration order. What was applied is kept in the instance-wide settings area `seeding` (no system table): per seeder `Name` (default the type name) the highest `Version` that ran to the end. A seeder applied at its current version is skipped at boot; raising `Version` runs it again. This keeps purged content, renamed authors or tags and changed settings from coming back or being overwritten.

**One claim per run.** A run that has seeders to apply takes a single claim for all of them (a `ClaimId` and a time in the same document), with a write that names the version of the settings row it read, and renews the time every `ClaimTimeout / 3` while it runs. Only a claim older than `ClaimTimeout` (default 15 minutes) is taken over, whatever the version of the seeders, so a rolling deploy never runs two versions at once. An instance that loses the race (`FCMS2808`) or finds a fresh claim boots without seeding and logs it at Information; the seed is retried at a later boot or with `ISeedRunner`. A run finishes or releases the claim only while the stored `ClaimId` is still its own: a run whose claim was taken over stops, logs a warning and leaves the row alone. Any exception after the claim is taken (a locale that is refused, a failed mark, a seeder that throws) releases the claim if it is still the run's own, and is rethrown. A seeder is marked applied only after it succeeds. A seeder that throws releases the claim, is not marked, and fails host start (as does any exception in a seeder at boot).

**Seeders converge.** A seeder finds each entry by a natural key that a person does not change (a slug, never a name) and brings it to the state it wants step by step: create if missing, publish if still a draft, add the translation if missing, trash if not trashed, schedule if not scheduled. It does not return early because the entry exists, so a run that stopped halfway is finished by the next. A trashed entry counts as found, and a unique conflict (`FCMS2111`) or a version conflict while creating means another run got there first, so the entry is read again. `SeedContext.AppliedVersion` is the version the seeder last ran to the end at, 0 if never. State changes (publish, trash, schedule) are made only to entries the run created, or when `AppliedVersion` is 0, a first run that may have stopped halfway. After a version bump existing entries stay as a person left them (a restored article is not trashed again, a schedule is not moved) and only missing ones are created. If a version-bump run crashes after creating an entry and before applying its state, the rerun leaves that entry as it is, because it can no longer tell the entry from one a person changed. A state step that finds its entry gone or in the trash (an overlapping run or a person got there first) counts as done.

**Blog sample.** `samples/Blog` (references the SDK, Core and the media plugin, which a host that references the sample discovers) holds `Article`, `Author`, `Tag`, `Seo`, `HeroBlock`, `QuoteBlock`, `GalleryBlock`, the typed settings class `BlogSettings` (area `blog`; it replaces the `SiteSettings` singleton of 4.2, per 8.7), `BlogSeeder` and `cms.AddBlogSample()`. The seed set is two authors, three tags, five articles (one published in `en` and `fr`, one published, one draft, one trashed and one scheduled for 30 days after the host clock) and the site's `blog` settings. Authors, tags and articles have a slug, which is the key. The media fields are back with the media field type (B-67): `Article.Hero`, `Author.Avatar`, `HeroBlock.Image` and `GalleryBlock.Images`. `Logo` is `BlogSettings.Logo`, the id of the logo's asset, because the `SiteSettings` singleton it was on became a settings area, and settings have no field types; it is neither populated nor counted in `cms_reference`. The seed uploads six small PNGs through `IMediaService`, tagged `blog-sample` and found again by file name, with titles and alternative text (the welcome banner's in `fr` too), and the articles, authors, blocks and settings refer to them. Acceptance on PostgreSQL and SQL Server follows #2711, because SQLite is the only provider run for real until then.

## 5. First-party plugins, server halves

### 5.1 Media plugin

Rows 5.1 to 5.12; 5.13 to 5.18 as Should; decisions 34 to 37.

**Tables.** `cms_media_asset (id, site_id, folder_id, file_name, storage_provider, storage_key, mime_type, size, checksum, width, height, duration, title, alt json per locale, caption json per locale, credit, tags json, focal_x, focal_y, is_private, created_*, updated_*, deleted_at)`, `cms_media_folder (id, site_id, parent_id, name, path)`, `cms_media_variant (asset_id, params_hash, storage_key, size, created_at)`, `cms_media_preset (site_id, name, params json)`.

**Storage.** `IFileStorage` (stream open, write, delete, exists, metadata) with per-site provider selection; providers: local disk (allowed in production with a documented warning), S3-compatible, Azure Blob.

**Upload.** `POST /api/management/v1/media` multipart, multi-file; size limits; MIME allow-list with magic-byte sniffing; executables rejected; SVG sanitised or rejected per setting. Upload from URL is a Should.

**Transforms.** `GET /media/{id}/{filename}?w=&h=&fit=cover|contain|fill&q=&f=auto|webp|avif|jpeg|png&fx=&fy=&preset=hero&sig=` where the filename is ignored on lookup (decision 36). `f=auto` negotiates by `Accept` with `Vary: Accept`. Results are cached as variants in the same storage. Limits on dimensions and operation count. Signatures are HMAC over the canonical parameter string with a key kept protected by Data Protection (as built below, B-65); `Media:RequireSignedUrls=false` allows unsigned URLs for trusted setups. `IImageProcessor` has a SkiaSharp implementation by default, and the slot stays replaceable (Q8; as built, B-65).

**Media Delivery API.** `GET /api/delivery/v1/media/{id}` returns metadata and URL templates with the same key classes and cache rules as content.

**Usage and integrity.** `cms_reference` rows with `target_type = media` answer `GET /media/{id}/usage`; delete is refused while referenced unless `force=true`, which nulls the references; replace keeps the id and invalidates variants.

**Private assets.** `is_private` assets are never served from `/media/{id}`; `POST /media/{id}/download-url` returns a short-lived signed URL after a folder permission check (the folder reach of `media.read`, ADR 0004; as built, B-66).

**Field type.** Registers `media` with `MediaRef` and `MediaRefList`, OpenAPI schema, filters by id, and an `IMediaResolver` used by population so `populate=hero` returns metadata and URLs. Rich text and block media references use the same resolver.

**Media as built (B-64).** `MediaPlugin` (`media`) in `FluentCMS.Plugins.Media` references the SDK and `FluentCMS.Plugins.Abstractions`, and the reference host adds it. Its stream on `PluginDbContext<MediaPlugin>` has the SQLite set (`InitialMedia`): `cms_media_asset` with the columns above (`duration` stays null until audio and video metadata, 5.14) and `cms_media_folder (id, site_id, parent_id, name, path)`, where `path` is the ids from the root down to the folder, `/{id}/{id}/`. The variant, preset and signing key tables come with transforms (B-65, below). The PostgreSQL and SQL Server sets are [#2711](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2711)'s, as for webhooks.

- **Contracts.** The SDK has `IFileStorage` (`OpenReadAsync`, `WriteAsync`, `DeleteAsync`, `ExistsAsync`, `GetMetadataAsync`, and the `Name` an asset records) and `IMediaService`, which the endpoints call and the field type, the MCP plugin and other plugins will. The service checks every permission with `IPermissionChecker`.
- **Storage.** `IFileStorage` is a provider slot the plugin declares, whose default `LocalFileStorage` (`local`) keeps files under `FluentCms:Media:Local:Root` (`App_Data/media` under the content root). The root is the instance's, in configuration, not a site setting: the storage is one for every site, and a root a site administrator could change would write anywhere the process can. A key is segments of letters, digits, dots, hyphens and underscores, checked before it becomes a path, and a write goes to a temporary file that is moved over the target. An asset's key is `{site}/{asset}/original{.ext}`, so variants can sit beside it. The local disk is allowed in Production, and the startup report lists `media-local-storage` there with the warning that every instance needs the directory on one shared volume and that backups must include it. Each asset records `storage_provider` and `storage_key`, so a storage plugin ([#2836](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2836), as built in B-68 below) that replaces the slot for new uploads leaves older assets where they are, to be read through the storage they name.
- **Upload.** `POST /api/management/v1/media` reads a multipart body of up to `FluentCms:Media:MaxRequestBodySize` (256 MB): every file part is a file, and the field `folderId` names the folder. The rules are the site's, in the settings area `media` (`settings.media.manage`): `MaxFileSize` (50 MB), `MaxRequestSize` (200 MB, cut to the instance limit), `MaxFilesPerRequest` (20), `AllowedTypes` and `Svg`. A file is read once to a temporary file, counted and hashed with SHA-256 on the way, so one over a limit is refused (`FCMS1582`, 413) without reading it to the end. Its type comes from its first bytes, from a built-in table: JPEG, PNG, GIF, WebP, AVIF, PDF, MP4, WebM, MP3 and WAV, which is the default allow-list; a type off the site's list or no known type is `FCMS1581` (415). Executables are always refused, whatever the name or `Content-Type` say (`FCMS1583`, 415): by signature (PE `MZ`, ELF, Mach-O in both byte orders and universal binaries, and a shebang) and by an executable or script extension. Width and height are read from the headers of JPEG, PNG, GIF and WebP with built-in code. A request stores all its files or none: they are written to the storage only once every file has passed, and a file written for a request that then fails is deleted again (event 9260 when that fails).
- **SVG.** `Svg` is `Refuse` (the default), which refuses every SVG (`FCMS1584`, 415), or `Sanitize`. An SVG is XML read with DTD processing prohibited, no resolver, no processing instructions and no comments, whose root is `<svg>`; anything else is no SVG. Sanitising is a conservative pass with `System.Xml`: it removes `script`, `foreignObject`, `iframe`, `embed`, `object`, `handler` and `listener`, elements of other vocabularies such as XHTML, attributes of other namespaces, `on*` attributes, animations whose `attributeName` is a link or a handler, every attribute whose value holds a `javascript:` or `vbscript:` URL or a `data:` URL other than a PNG, JPEG, GIF or WebP image, and CSS with `@import`, `expression(` or such a URL. Size and checksum are those of the sanitised file. Serving SVGs with a restrictive `Content-Security-Policy` is the Delivery route's ([#2834](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2834)).
- **Metadata.** `GET` and `PATCH /media/{id}` read and change `title`, `alt` and `caption` in the locale of the request (`?locale=` or `X-Cms-Locale`, as for content), `credit`, `tags` (at most 50, each up to 64 characters without quotes, backslashes or control characters), `focalPoint` (`x` and `y` from 0 to 1) and `isPrivate`; a member left out stays, and an empty text clears it. `POST /media/{id}/move` moves an asset to a folder or, with a null `folderId`, the root. `DELETE /media/{id}` sets `deleted_at`, after which the asset is not found; its bytes stay until purge and the usage checks of [#2834](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2834). `GET /media` lists the assets newest first, by `folder` (an id, or `root`), `subfolders`, `type` (a MIME type or a family such as `image/*`), `tag` and `search` (a text in the title, the file name or the tags, whatever the case; B-72), with `page` and `pageSize` (up to 100).
- **Folders.** `/media/folders` lists, creates (`name`, `parentId`), renames (`PATCH`), moves (`POST /{id}/move` with `parentId`) and deletes folders. Names are unique within their parent (`FCMS1587`, 409), folders nest up to 20 deep, a folder cannot move into itself or a subfolder (`FCMS1589`, 409), and only an empty folder is deleted (`FCMS1588`, 409). A folder moves with its subfolders, whose paths are rewritten, and its assets, which keep their folder.
- **Permissions.** `media.read`, `media.upload`, `media.update` (metadata and moves, in both folders), `media.delete` and `media.folders.manage`. A grant may be limited to folders with the `resources` constraint (4.8, [ADR 0004](../decisions/0004-folder-limited-media-grants.md)): it reaches those folders and every folder below them, wherever they move, and only a grant with no folder limit reaches the root. An asset or folder outside the reach is not found (`FCMS1585`, `FCMS1586`), and a caller without the permission gets 403. This replaces `media.folder.{id}.read` above, which a catalogue fixed at startup cannot hold.
- **Events.** `MediaAssetUploadedEvent`, `MediaAssetUpdatedEvent`, `MediaAssetMovedEvent` and `MediaAssetDeletedEvent` (SDK, `FluentCMS.Events`; `MediaAssetReplacedEvent` came with B-66) carry the asset's id, folder and file name, and write the activity rows `media.uploaded` (with `mimeType` and `size`), `media.updated`, `media.moved` (with `from` and `to`) and `media.deleted`, with the target type `media`. Webhooks deliver them (5.2).
- **Codes.** `FCMS1580` an invalid field, `FCMS1581` a type that is not allowed, `FCMS1582` an upload over a limit, `FCMS1583` an executable, `FCMS1584` an SVG the settings refuse, `FCMS1585` no such asset, `FCMS1586` no such folder, `FCMS1587` a folder name that is taken, `FCMS1588` a folder that is not empty and `FCMS1589` a folder moved into itself. Event ids 9260 to 9279.

**Decisions as built (B-64).** Settled for [#2832](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2832):

- **Migrations.** The media stream ships a set per provider since [#2711](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2711) (4.3, "Plugin migration sets per provider as built"), so a host on PostgreSQL or SQL Server passes the foreign-set check of [#2907](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2907).
- **Folder-limited grants.** A `resources` constraint on the grant, naming folder ids, which `IPermissionChecker.ResourcesAsync` answers and the media service extends to subfolders, keeps the catalogue fixed at startup ([ADR 0004](../decisions/0004-folder-limited-media-grants.md)).
- **File types.** A small built-in table of magic bytes, with executables refused by signature as well as by name. No package.
- **Image size.** Read from the headers with built-in code; the image processor comes with transforms ([#2833](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2833)).
- **SVG.** `Refuse` by default, or `Sanitize` with the `System.Xml` pass above, DTD processing off.
- **Local storage.** Its root is in the plugin's configuration (`FluentCms:Media:Local:Root`), since the provider is one per instance; the rules of uploads are the site's media settings. Allowed in Production with the startup report's warning.

**Transforms as built (B-65).** The stream's second SQLite migration, `MediaTransforms`, adds `cms_media_variant (asset_id, params_hash, storage_key, size, created_at)` keyed by asset and hash, `cms_media_preset (site_id, name, params)` keyed by site and name, with `params` a JSON object of the query's names and values, and `cms_media_signing_key (id, key, created_at)`.

- **Contracts.** The SDK has `IImageProcessor` in `FluentCMS.Media`, beside `IFileStorage`: `CanDecode`, `CanEncode`, `IdentifyAsync`, which reads the width, height and frame count from the header without decoding, and `ProcessAsync`, which applies the EXIF orientation, resizes, leaves out EXIF, IPTC and XMP, and encodes an `ImageTransform` (`Width`, `Height`, `Fit`, `Quality`, `MimeType`, `FocalPoint`). It is a provider slot the plugin declares, whose default `SkiaImageProcessor` reads the header with `SKCodec`, reads JPEG, PNG, GIF (its first frame) and WebP into sRGB, and writes JPEG, PNG and WebP; cover, contain and fill are its own. It encodes neither AVIF nor GIF, so `f=auto` never picks AVIF, and a GIF without `f` becomes a PNG. An image that does not decode whole is `FCMS1592`. `IMediaService` adds `GetImageUrlAsync(asset, MediaImageOptions)`, which returns `/media/{id}/{fileName}?…&sig=` relative to the CMS base and checks no permission, and `ListPresetsAsync`, `SetPresetAsync` and `DeletePresetAsync`. `OpenImageAsync(id, MediaImageOptions)` (B-83) opens a copy of an image for code in the host that needs its pixels, such as the AI assist plugin's `altText` (5.5): it needs `media.read` in the asset's folder, reads a private asset too, takes no preset, is held to the same limits of the site and stores its variant as a URL's transform does. Under the shared library it reads the library's row and asks the permission, and takes the limits, in the site of the request, as the other calls do ("Shared library as built").
- **Route.** `GET /media/{id}/{fileName}` is a group of the plugin's own (`MapGroup`), outside `/api`, open to anyone with `AllowAnonymous`, under the Delivery rate limit and CORS; the site is the request's, by host or `site`. The file name is ignored. The parameters are `w` and `h` in pixels (with one, the other follows the aspect ratio), `fit` (`contain` by default, `cover` or `fill`), `q` (1 to 100, 80 by default), `f` (`auto`, `webp`, `avif`, `jpeg` or `png`; the asset's type when left out), `fx` and `fy` (0 to 1, together) and `preset`. A name given twice, or one that is none of these, `sig` or `site`, is `FCMS1580`. `cover` crops around `fx` and `fy`, else the asset's focal point, else the centre. `f=auto` takes AVIF, then WebP, where `Accept` lists the type with a quality above zero and the processor encodes it, and the asset's type otherwise, and answers with `Vary: Accept`. An answer has the `Cache-Control` of `FluentCms:Media:CacheControl` (`public, max-age=86400`) and an `ETag` of the variant, and answers `If-None-Match` with 304. A URL without parameters re-encodes the image in its own type, or as a PNG where the processor does not encode that type.
- **Order of checks.** The signature first, before the asset is looked up (`FCMS1590`, 403); the parameters; the asset, which must be public and not deleted, whatever the signature (`FCMS1585`); the preset (`FCMS1593`); the site's limits on width, height and operations (`FCMS1591`, 422); the type, which the processor must read (`FCMS1592`, 415); and the size recorded at upload against `MaxSourcePixels`. Only then is a stored variant looked up. A new one is identified from its header first: its width times height times frames must be within `MaxSourcePixels`, and a side that follows the aspect ratio within the width and height limits, before it is decoded, against decompression bombs and outsized results.
- **Limits.** The site's media settings have `MaxTransformWidth` and `MaxTransformHeight` (4096 each), `MaxTransformOperations` (6; `w`, `h`, `fit`, `q` and `f` count one each, and so does the focal point, a preset's included) and `MaxSourcePixels` (50,000,000).
- **Variants.** The hash of a variant is the SHA-256 of the transform as resolved: the asset's checksum, the size, the fit, the quality, the type chosen and, for a cover, the focal point. The same request under any file name, a preset that resolves alike, and `f=auto` with the type it chose share one variant, and a new focal point or a new file makes a new one. It is written to the asset's storage beside the original, `{site}/{asset}/{hash}{.ext}`, then recorded; two requests that make it at once write the same key and meet at the primary key, and a row whose file is gone is made again. The recurring job `media.variants.cleanup` (daily at 04:45) removes the variants of assets that are deleted or gone, the file first, in batches of 500; a file it cannot delete keeps its row for the next run (event 9264).
- **Presets.** `GET /api/management/v1/media/presets` lists them (`media.read`); `PUT /presets/{name}` creates or replaces one and `DELETE` removes it (`media.presets.manage`, which a grant limited to folders does not give). A name is up to 64 lower-case letters, digits and hyphens. The body is `MediaImageOptions` in JSON (`width`, `height`, `fit`, `quality`, `format`, `focalPoint`), checked as a URL's parameters are and against the site's limits; a preset names no other preset. A URL's parameters win over its preset's.
- **Signing.** `sig` is the base64url HMAC-SHA256 of `{id}?{name=value&…}`: the transform parameters as the URL gives them, sorted by name and escaped, without `sig`, `site` and the file name. The key is `FluentCms:Media:SigningKey` (base64, at least 32 bytes) when it is set. Otherwise it is 32 random bytes generated once, at the first start under the lock `fluentcms:media-signing-key`, and stored in `cms_media_signing_key` protected by Data Protection, so that instances that start together share it. An instance reads it once. `FluentCms:Media:RequireSignedUrls` false serves unsigned URLs, and the startup report lists `media-unsigned-urls` while it is off in Production.
- **Codes.** `FCMS1590` a missing or wrong signature (403), `FCMS1591` a transform over a limit (422), `FCMS1592` an asset the processor cannot read (415) and `FCMS1593` no such preset (404). Events 9261 to 9267.

**Decisions as built (B-65).** Settled for [#2833](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2833):

- **Dependency.** `SkiaSharp` 3.119.4 and `SkiaSharp.NativeAssets.Linux.NoDependencies` 3.119.4 (MIT), pinned in `Directory.Packages.props`, instead of the ImageSharp the issue named: ImageSharp 4.x and 3.2 fail a Release build without a Six Labors license key, and 3.1.12, the last version without that check, has six open advisories. The Linux assets without dependencies run on the CI runners and in containers without fontconfig; Windows and macOS assets come with `SkiaSharp`. A host that wants ImageSharp, with its own Six Labors license, puts it in the slot.
- **Replaceable processor.** `IImageProcessor` is an SDK provider slot with the SkiaSharp default, and nothing outside `SkiaImageProcessor` knows Skia, so a host or a plugin can use another library, ImageSharp included, or a CDN.
- **Route.** `MapGroup("/media")`, outside `/api`; the file name is ignored on lookup. AVIF only where the processor encodes it, which Skia cannot; WebP from `Accept`, else the asset's type, with `Vary: Accept`.
- **Fail closed.** The route never serves a private or soft-deleted asset, with any signature. Signed downloads of private files are [#2834](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2834)'s, and so are originals and files that are not images.
- **Limits.** Width, height and operations are the site's media settings and are checked before the image is read; the source's pixels, and a side that follows the aspect ratio, are checked from its header (`SKCodec`) before it is decoded.
- **Variants.** In the asset's own storage, recorded in `cms_media_variant`, removed for deleted assets by a recurring job.
- **Presets.** `cms_media_preset` per site, managed under `/api/management/v1/media/presets`, with the permission checks in the service and a new permission, `media.presets.manage`.
- **Signing key.** A key generated once and protected by Data Protection, not a key derived from the ring, so that signed URLs saved in content and CDNs outlive its rotation (90 days by default). The configured key wins. Replacing the key, by configuration or by deleting the row and restarting every instance, invalidates every URL signed before.
- **Query.** An unknown or repeated parameter is refused rather than ignored, so that a typing error does not serve the original size, and `site` is allowed for the host's site resolution.
- **Migrations.** Only the SQLite set of the media stream at first, as for B-64; [#2711](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2711) added the PostgreSQL and SQL Server sets.

**Delivery, usage and private assets as built (B-66).** No migration: references are core's `cms_reference`, download URLs are signed and stored nowhere, and purging needs no column.

- **Contracts.** The SDK has `IContentReferences` in `FluentCMS.Content`, which core implements over `cms_reference` for targets that are not content types: `FindAsync` lists the references to a target from entries outside the trash, in both states, without the entries, locales and fields the principal may not read (the evaluator asks once per type, site, state and locale; a non-localized value is asked in the locale of the operation); `CountAsync` counts the entries outside the trash, whatever the principal reads; `RemoveAsync` deletes every reference to the target, trash included, without a version, and evicts the `entry:{id}` tags of those entries with a `CacheInvalidatedEvent`, as a publish does, while the output cache or the cache tag header is on. `IMediaService` adds `DeleteAsync(id, force)`, `ReplaceAsync`, `GetUsageAsync`, `GetDownloadUrlAsync` (`MediaDownloadUrl`) and `DeliverAsync` (`MediaDelivery`, `MediaUrls`), and `FluentCMS.Events` has `MediaAssetReplacedEvent` with the new file's type and size.
- **Delivery.** `GET /api/delivery/v1/media/{id}`, mapped with `MapDelivery("/media")`, answers `id`, `fileName`, `mimeType`, `size`, `width`, `height`, `title`, `locale`, `alt` and `caption` in the request's locale, `credit`, `tags`, `focalPoint`, `isPrivate`, `createdAt`, `updatedAt` and `urls`; no site, folder, checksum or ids of people. A public asset is read by any caller. `urls` has `original`, the signed URL of the original file; `presets`, a signed transform URL per preset of the site for an image the processor reads; and `template`, only while `RequireSignedUrls` is false, a transform URL with `{w}`, `{h}`, `{fit}`, `{q}` and `{f}` placeholders. The answer has the cache rules of content: an `ETag` that hashes the body, no `Last-Modified`, `FluentCms:Delivery:Cache:CacheControl` as written to an anonymous read of a public asset and in its private form otherwise, the `Vary` of content, and a copy in the output cache tagged `media:{id}` and `media-presets:{site}` beside core's `site:{id}`. Outbox handlers evict `media:{id}` on the updated, moved, replaced and deleted events, and a preset's change evicts `media-presets:{site}` at once; each eviction raises `CacheInvalidatedEvent` with its tags, and none happens while `FluentCms:Delivery:Cache:OutputCache` is off and `CacheTagHeader` unset, as for core. With `CacheTagHeader` set the answer carries `site:{id}`, `media:{id}` and `media-presets:{site}` in that header.
- **Private assets.** A private asset is answered, with `urls` null, only to a caller whose `media.read` reaches its folder (ADR 0004), and is otherwise the same `FCMS1585` as an unknown id.
