A Delivery principal holds no permission but reading content (4.8), so the anonymous caller and Delivery keys never read one; a bearer token or a management key does. `POST /api/management/v1/media/{id}/download-url` needs `media.read` in the asset's folder and answers `{ "url", "expiresAt" }`, `/media/{id}/original/{fileName}?exp=&sig=`, where `exp` is in Unix seconds and the lifetime is the site's media setting `DownloadUrlSeconds` (300, from 10 to 86,400).
- **Original files.** `GET /media/{id}/original/{fileName}`, in the plugin's `/media` group beside transforms, open to anyone. With `exp` it is a download URL: the signature is always checked, whatever `RequireSignedUrls` says, a private asset is served, the answer is an attachment with `Cache-Control: private, no-store`, and from `exp` on it is `FCMS1595` (403). Without `exp` it serves a public asset only, signed unless `RequireSignedUrls` is false, inline with `FluentCms:Media:CacheControl`. The `ETag` is the checksum, `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff` is always sent, and an SVG gets `Content-Security-Policy: default-src 'none'; img-src data:; style-src 'unsafe-inline'; sandbox`. A soft-deleted asset is never served. The canonical string is `{id}/original` or `{id}/original?exp={exp}`, which a transform's `{id}?…` never equals. Only `exp`, `sig` and `site` are taken (`FCMS1580`).
- **Usage.** `GET /api/management/v1/media/{id}/usage` needs `media.read` on the asset and answers `{ "items": [{ "type", "entryId", "status", "locale", "field" }] }` from `IContentReferences.FindAsync`, where `status` is `draft` or `published`, `locale` is null for a non-localized value and `field` is the path as queries name it.
- **Delete.** `DELETE /media/{id}` is refused while entries outside the trash refer to the asset (`FCMS1594`, 409), counted across sites since an id is unique; the message gives no count, which would take in entries the caller may not read. `?force=true` removes the references first and then deletes the asset, so that a delete that fails between the two is tried again rather than leaving references to an asset that is gone. A reference from the trash does not hold a delete back; a restored entry then refers to an asset that is not found. Rich text keeps its own copy of a reference, which reads as an asset not found.
- **Purge.** The daily `media.variants.cleanup` also purges, after the variants, every asset deleted longer ago than `FluentCms:Media:DeletedRetention` (30 days) whose variants are gone: the original file, then the row, in batches of 500. A file it cannot delete keeps its row for the next run (event 9269; 9270 counts them).
- **Replace.** `POST /media/{id}/replace` takes a `multipart/form-data` body of exactly one file and needs `media.update` in the asset's folder. The file passes the checks of an upload against the site's media settings. It is written under a key of its own, `{site}/{asset}/original-{v7}{.ext}`, so the old file serves until the row names the new one; then the name, storage, key, type, size, checksum and image size change in one save with the removal of the variant rows, and the old original and the variant files are deleted (event 9268 when one cannot be). A request that read the old row just before the save may find the old file gone and answer `FCMS1585`. The id, the metadata and the references stay, and `MediaAssetReplacedEvent` is raised, with the activity row `media.replaced` (`mimeType`, `size`) and the webhook event of the same name.
- **Codes.** `FCMS1594` an asset in use (409) and `FCMS1595` a download URL that has expired (403). Events 9268 to 9270.

**Decisions as built (B-66).** Settled for [#2834](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2834):

- **Delivery.** Metadata in the request locale with signed URL templates: the original, one per preset, and a placeholder template only where unsigned URLs are served, since a client cannot sign one. The content `ETag`, `Cache-Control` and output cache, with tags that media events and preset changes evict.
- **Usage.** `cms_reference` rows with `target_type = media`, read through `IContentReferences`, which core owns since plugins do not reference it; entries the caller cannot read are left out.
- **Delete.** Refused with `FCMS1594` while referenced; `force=true` deletes and removes the references, as above. Soft-deleted assets are purged after a retention, by the daily cleanup.
- **Replace.** Keeps the id and metadata, runs the upload checks, drops the variants and raises the replaced event, which webhooks deliver as `media.replaced`.
- **Private downloads.** A short-lived signed URL after the folder check, with its lifetime in the media settings, served until it expires and refused after.
- **SVG.** Served from the original-file route with a restrictive `Content-Security-Policy` and `nosniff`.
- **Migrations.** None.

**Field type as built (B-67).** The plugin adds the `media` field type (4.2) and two scoped services. `IMediaResolver` (SDK, `FluentCMS.Media`) resolves asset ids in the site of the operation to the `MediaDelivery` of B-66: the `MediaAsset` with its alternative text and caption in the locale asked for (that of the operation by default) and the `MediaUrls` the Delivery API gives (`original`, a URL per preset of the site for an image the processor reads, and `template` while unsigned URLs are served), with the preset names read once per call. It needs `media.read` and keeps to the folders a grant limited to folders reaches (ADR 0004), so an asset the caller cannot read, one of another site and one that is deleted are left out, and a caller with no grant of it gets none. A private asset has no URLs. `MediaReferenceResolver` is the plugin's `IReferenceResolver` of `media` (4.4), built on it: a populated asset is `{ "id", "type": "media", "fileName", "mimeType", "size", "width", "height", "title", "alt", "caption", "credit", "focalPoint", "urls" }`, without its folder, tags or who uploaded it, and rich text gets the original's URL and `alt`. `FindMissingAsync` names the ids that are no asset of the site or are deleted, whatever the caller's folders, so a write refuses them with `FCMS2113`. Core stores every media reference, in a field, a list, a block, a repeated component or a rich text node, as a `cms_reference` row with `target_type = media`, `target_id` the asset id, `field` the top-level path of the field that holds it and `locale` `''` unless the field is localized, which is what `IContentReferences` reads for usage and for a delete (B-66). No migration: `cms_reference` exists. `MediaFocalPoint` reads and writes its numbers strictly, so OpenAPI describes `x` and `y` as numbers only.

**Decisions as built (B-67).** Settled for [#2835](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2835):

- **Field type.** `media` is the plugin's, through `AddFieldType`, with `MediaRef` and `MediaRefList`, stored in `cms_reference` with `target_type = media`, an OpenAPI fragment and `eq` and `in` on the asset id. Without the plugin `[Media]` stops the host with `FCMS1203`.
- **Writes.** A write refuses an asset it adds that does not exist in the site or is deleted, with the core code `FCMS2113`, since the check is core's for any target with a resolver; an asset the entry already had is not checked again.
- **`IMediaResolver`.** In the SDK: asset ids to `MediaDelivery`, metadata and the signed URLs of the Delivery API in the request locale, checked against `media.read` and the caller's folders. An asset the caller cannot read resolves to null, and a private one populates without a public URL.
- **Population.** Core populates through `IReferenceResolver`, an SDK hook for targets that are no content type, so it references no plugin and knows no media. `populate=hero` returns the asset; without populate or beyond the depth the value is `{ "id", "type": "media" }`. A populate path naming a block list or repeated component resolves the media inside it.
- **Rich text and blocks.** Media nodes and media fields in blocks and components use the same resolver and count in `cms_reference`. The HTML and Markdown renderers write the URL and the alternative text, the node's own or else the asset's, escaped.
- **Blog sample.** `Hero`, `Avatar` and the images of the hero and gallery blocks are media fields again, and `Logo` is a setting of the `blog` area holding the asset's id, since settings have no field types (4.16).
- **Migrations.** None.

**Storage providers as built (B-68).** `FluentCMS.Plugins.Media.S3` and `FluentCMS.Plugins.Media.AzureBlob` reference the media plugin (2.4) and fill its `IFileStorage` slot for new uploads, through `MediaPlugin.UseStorage`, the seam their extension methods call: `cms.AddPlugin<MediaPlugin>(media => media.UseS3(section))` or `media.UseAzureBlob(section)`. The last call wins, and the host's own `Providers.Use` wins over it. The options are bound from the section and checked at start, with a message that names the keys and quotes no value. No migration.

- **S3.** `S3FileStorage` (`s3`) on `AWSSDK.S3`: `Bucket`, `Endpoint` for an S3-compatible service (with it, the request and response checksums the SDK adds by default are sent only where required, since many such services do not know them), `Region`, `KeyPrefix`, `ForcePathStyle`, and `AccessKeyId`, `SecretAccessKey` and `SessionToken`, or none of them for the AWS SDK's default chain. A write is one `PUT` (a stream that cannot seek is copied to a temporary file first, as the SDK needs the length), and a delete looks the object up first, since S3 answers a `DELETE` alike either way. The credentials need `s3:GetObject`, `s3:PutObject`, `s3:DeleteObject` and `s3:ListBucket`.
- **Azure Blob.** `AzureBlobFileStorage` (`azure-blob`) on `Azure.Storage.Blobs`: `ConnectionString` with `Container`, or `ContainerUrl` with a SAS in its query (read, write, delete and list), and `KeyPrefix`. A 404 of a missing container is an error, not a missing file.
- **Keys.** `FileStorageKeys` (public, in the media plugin) checks a key for every storage as the local disk did: segments of letters, digits, dots, hyphens and underscores. A cloud storage puts its prefix before it.
- **Private objects.** Objects are written without an ACL or public access, and bytes leave through `/media` and the signed URLs only. Variants are written beside their asset, in its bucket or container.
- **Older assets.** `MediaStorage` resolves an asset's storage by the name it recorded: the one in the slot, else a keyed `IFileStorage` of that name. The plugin always registers the local disk as the keyed `local`, so an asset written there is still transformed, served from the original-file route and download URLs, and replaced, and its variants and file are still written, cleaned up and purged there, after the slot moves to S3 or Azure Blob. Transforms, the original-file route, replace (which writes the new file to the slot and deletes the old one and its variants from the asset's storage), and the cleanup and purge read through it; a storage it cannot find leaves the file and its row for the next run.
- **Health.** The plugin adds the ready check `media-storage` (five seconds), which the storage in the slot answers as an `IHealthCheck`: the local disk creates and deletes a file in its root, S3 sends a `HEAD` of the bucket, and Azure Blob lists one blob under the prefix, which a container SAS allows. A failure is Unhealthy with a description that names the bucket or container and the status and error code, or the type of the exception, and carries no exception, since the health service logs one whole. A storage that is no `IHealthCheck` is reported Healthy as unchecked.
- **Tests.** One contract (write, open, exists, metadata, replace, delete, a missing key, keys refused, a stream that cannot seek, ready) runs on the local disk, S3 on MinIO, and Azure Blob on Azurite with a connection string and with a SAS, through Testcontainers on the Linux CI jobs. Hosts on each cloud storage upload, transform through a signed URL and find the variant in the bucket or container, serve an asset of the local disk after the move, report `/health/ready` Unhealthy once the bucket or container is gone, and log, report and answer no credential.

**Decisions as built (B-68).** Settled for [#2836](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2836):

- **Dependencies.** `AWSSDK.S3` 4.0.104.11 (Apache-2.0), the latest 4.0 patch, and `Azure.Storage.Blobs` 12.30.1 (MIT), each referenced by its provider only; `Testcontainers.Minio` 4.15.0 and `Testcontainers.Azurite` 4.16.0 (MIT) for the tests. All are pinned in `Directory.Packages.props`.
- **Configuration.** `media.UseS3(section)` and `media.UseAzureBlob(section)`. Managed identity would need `Azure.Identity` and is left out.
- **Private objects.** Bytes leave through `/media` and signed URLs, never through public bucket URLs.
- **Health.** One ready check, `media-storage`, answered by the storage in the slot, local disk included; descriptions hold no credential and results no exception.
- **Older assets.** Read by the recorded provider name, the local disk always among them; the change to transforms, the original-file route, replace, and the cleanup and purge job is the resolver alone.
- **Tests without Docker.** The tests that need MinIO or Azurite are skipped with the reason where Docker is missing, and on Windows, whose runners run Windows containers only; elsewhere an image that does not start fails them. `CONTRIBUTING.md` says so, and no GitHub Action was added. MinIO no longer publishes images (`minio/minio` is gone from Docker Hub and `quay.io/minio/minio` asks for a login), so the tests use the Pigsty project's continuation of the MinIO community build, `pgsty/minio`, pinned by the digest of its `RELEASE.2026-08-04T00-00-00Z` index (`sha256:b6bfe723…`), since a community tag can be moved; it keeps MinIO's entrypoint and ports, and Azurite `3.35.0` with `--skipApiVersionCheck`.
- **Migrations.** None.

**Transfer as built (B-92).** The plugin's `ITransferContributor`, `media`, writes `plugins/media/folders.jsonl` (parents first) and `plugins/media/assets.jsonl` with every column of an asset that is not deleted but its storage provider and key, which are kept as a hint, and with `mediaFiles=true` each original under `plugins/media/files/{id}/original{ext}`. An import keeps the ids, times and authors, and writes the rows of the library of the site of the import directly, without the media permissions, which `transfer.manage` stands for. A folder moved in the package takes its subtree with it. An asset whose file is in the package is checked against its checksum and written to the storage in the slot; one without is taken only when the storage the package names has the key it names and that key is of the library of the site of the import, as a storage shared between instances has (an asset deleted there then deletes the shared file), and is otherwise reported with `FCMS3220` and not imported, so no entry refers to an asset that cannot be served. An id of another library or of a deleted asset is `FCMS3221`. An asset is skipped when its `updatedAt` and checksum are the instance's, a conflict when the instance's is newer unless `overwrite=true`, and its events are the upload, replace or update events, with the import's origin. A file is never deleted: one an update replaces stays in the storage. Assets are reported under the section `media`, the reference target, so that a dry run counts them as there for the entries that refer to them, and folders under `media/folders`.

**Shared library as built (B-80).** `FluentCms:Media:Library` is `Shared` (the default) or `PerSite` (row 7.10, and the first half of 7.15). With `Shared`, every site reads and writes the library of the default site: its assets, folders and presets, whose `site_id` is the default site's, and whose files are under that site's storage keys. Only the rows follow the library. The permission is checked in the site of the request, with the folder limits of ADR 0004, so each site's roles decide who uploads and who reads, and the media settings (upload rules, transform limits) are those of the site of the request. Content of any site refers to the shared assets: population, the check of the assets a write names, the transform and original routes and the Delivery route all read the library. The usage of an asset lists the entries of every site, and a delete is refused while any refers to it. A Delivery copy is tagged with the site of its request, the asset and `media-presets:{library}`, so a preset change evicts the copies of every site. With `PerSite`, each site keeps its own library, as built in M3, and an asset of one site is unknown in another. An instance with one site behaves the same in both. An instance that already has several sites with assets of their own sets `PerSite`, since `Shared` reads the default site's library only. The option adds no migration and moves no row.

**Deleted sites as built ([#3001](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3001)).** The plugin hears `SiteDeletedEvent` through the outbox (`media.cleanup.site.deleted`, `DeletedSiteMedia`) and removes the library of the site. It marks every asset of the site deleted, removes the site's presets and folders, and then removes each asset, 500 at a time: its variants, each file from the storage the asset recorded and then the row, and then the original file and the asset's row. A file that cannot be deleted, whether the storage fails, times out on its own or is no longer one the instance has, is logged (event 9271) and leaves its asset as a deleted row, which the daily cleanup purges with its file after `DeletedRetention`; the site stays deleted. A delivery cut short by the outbox's `DeliveryTimeout`, or delivered again, goes on with what is left, and event 9272 counts what was removed. A library too large for the outbox's attempts leaves the event failed, and since every asset was marked deleted first, the cleanup purges the rest. With `Shared` the library is the default site's, which cannot be deleted, so only the rows a site kept while the library was `PerSite` are found. No `MediaAssetDeletedEvent` is raised for the removed assets: the Delivery copies go with the eviction of the site (4.6), and the site's webhooks go with the site (5.2).

### 5.2 Webhooks plugin

Webhooks appear in rows 2.2, 2.20, 6.10, 8.16 and 10.8, and decision 47 names them a first-party plugin.

**Tables.** `cms_webhooks_subscription (id, site_id, name, url, secret (Data Protection encrypted), previous_secret (encrypted the same way), previous_secret_expires_at, events json, types json, headers (json, Data Protection encrypted), enabled, created_*, updated_*)`, `cms_webhooks_delivery (id, delivery_id, webhook_id, event_id, event, attempt, state, status_code, duration_ms, request_body, response_excerpt, error, next_retry_at, manual, created_at)`. A plugin's tables start with `cms_{plugin}_` (4.13).

**Delivery.** Subscribes through the outbox; each delivery is a job. Payload `{ version, id, event, occurredAt, site, origin, actor, data: { type, documentId, locale, status, version }, links }`. Headers `X-FluentCMS-Event`, `X-FluentCMS-Delivery`, `X-FluentCMS-Signature: t=<unix>,v1=<hex HMAC-SHA256(secret, t + "." + body)>`, with a `v1` for each secret that signs. Retries at 1m, 5m, 30m, 2h, 24h by default, then dead; manual redeliver; test ping; log retention 30 days.

**Endpoints.** `/api/management/v1/webhooks` CRUD, `/{id}/deliveries`, `/{id}/deliveries/{d}/redeliver`, `/{id}/ping`.

**Webhooks as built.** `WebhooksPlugin` (`webhooks`) in `FluentCMS.Plugins.Webhooks` references the SDK and `FluentCMS.Plugins.Abstractions`, and the reference host adds it. Its stream on `PluginDbContext<WebhooksPlugin>` has the SQLite set (`InitialWebhooks`, `AddDeliveryClaim` with the claim's `manual` column and in-flight index, and `AddPreviousSecret` with the `previous_secret` columns); the PostgreSQL and SQL Server sets are [#2711](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2711)'s ([ADR 0003](../decisions/0003-plugin-contribution-contracts.md)), and [plugin migrations](../plugin-migrations.md) has the steps.

**Subscriptions.** A webhook belongs to the site of the operation that made it. Every endpoint needs `webhooks.manage` in that site, which `WebhookService` checks with `IPermissionChecker`, and a webhook of another site is not found (`FCMS1564`). A webhook names:

- one or more events, by the names of their activity rows: `content.draft_saved`, `content.published`, `content.unpublished`, `content.draft_discarded`, `content.version_restored`, `content.trashed`, `content.restored`, `content.purged`, `content.scheduled`, `content.schedule_cancelled`, `cache.invalidated`, `media.uploaded`, `media.updated`, `media.replaced`, `media.moved` and `media.deleted` (another is `FCMS1563`);
- content types of the schema, where none is every type; a cache or media event has no type and matches by name alone;
- up to 20 headers, none of them one that a delivery sets, whose values are secrets (below).

Names are unique per site (`FCMS1566`). The secret is the caller's, of 16 to 256 characters, or generated (`whsec_` and 64 hex digits) and returned by that create call only. It is stored with Data Protection and is in no other response, event, activity row or log line. `PUT /{id}` replaces the webhook, and leaving the secret out keeps it. A new secret keeps the one it replaces signing beside it until `FluentCms:Webhooks:SecretOverlap` (24 hours; `00:00:00` for none) has passed, below. `webhook.created`, `webhook.updated` (with `secretChanged`) and `webhook.deleted` are activity rows of the plugin's events, which carry the id and the name only. A delete removes the webhook and its delivery log in one transaction on the plugin's context, answers `FCMS1564` when a delete at the same time removed it first, and publishes `WebhookDeletedEvent` after the commit, since a publish inside the plugin's transaction is refused (`FCMS2602`, 4.11); the activity row comes from that event through the outbox. The media events are the SDK's (`FluentCMS.Events`), so a webhook subscribes to them whether or not the media plugin is in the host.

**Headers are secrets.** A receiver's credentials, such as `Authorization` or `X-Api-Key`, are what the headers are for, so their values are treated as the secret is ([#2881](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2881)):

- The `headers` column holds the JSON map protected with Data Protection, under the purpose `FluentCMS.Plugins.Webhooks.Headers` beside the secret's `FluentCMS.Plugins.Webhooks.Secret`, so a protected value of one cannot be read as the other.
- No answer holds a value. List, get, create and update give each header's name with the value `***`. The create answer does not echo them either, because the headers are the caller's, like a secret the caller gives, which that answer leaves out too; only a generated secret is shown once.
- A `PUT` that sends `***` for a header keeps the value stored under that name, compared without case, so a webhook read and sent back keeps its headers. `***` for a header that has no stored value is `FCMS1560`.
- Deliveries send the real values, and the delivery log, the job payload, the events, the activity rows and the log lines hold none.
- A row written before the headers were protected holds plain JSON, which a protected value, in base64url, never starts with. It is read as it is, so it is still delivered, and protected by the next write of the webhook. Once the host has started, after the migrations, the plugin also protects every such row; the update names the plain value it replaces, so a write in between, or another instance doing the same, wins. Event 9248 counts the rows it protected, and 9249 reports a failure, after which it tries again every 30 seconds.
- When the key ring can no longer read the headers, an answer lists none and an attempt fails with "The headers of the webhook could not be read; set them again."

**Secret rotation.** A `PUT` with a new secret moves the one it replaces to `previous_secret`, still protected, with `previous_secret_expires_at` set `SecretOverlap` ahead ([#2886](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2886)). Until then a delivery is signed with both, `t=<unix>,v1=<new>,v1=<previous>`, as Stripe and Svix do, so a receiver that still has the previous secret keeps verifying while it is changed over. Then only the new secret signs. Nothing has to clear the previous one: it is ignored once it has expired, and the next write of the webhook drops it. A second rotation within the overlap replaces the previous secret with the one that rotation replaces, so at most two secrets sign. While the previous secret signs, the answers carry `previousSecretExpiresAt`.

**Target URLs.** A URL is absolute `https`, or `http` in Development, with no user information (`FCMS1561`). Delivery refuses addresses that are not public: loopback, link-local (with `169.254.169.254`), private (RFC 1918, `fc00::/7`), carrier-grade NAT, multicast, unspecified and reserved, and the IPv4-mapped, IPv4-compatible and NAT64 forms of these. The check runs on the resolved addresses at connect time in `SocketsHttpHandler.ConnectCallback`, so DNS rebinding cannot get past a check made only at save. Saving checks the host too, for an early error (`FCMS1562`); a host that does not resolve yet is accepted. Redirects are not followed, and a 3xx is a failed attempt. Deliveries do not go through a system proxy. `FluentCms:Webhooks:AllowPrivateNetworks` (default `false`) turns the address check off, and the startup report lists `webhooks-private-networks` while it is on in Production.

**Delivery.** An outbox handler per event (`webhooks.{event}`) matches the event against the enabled webhooks of its site and enqueues the job `webhooks.deliver` for each. The delivery id is derived from the outbox delivery and the webhook, so a handler that runs again changes nothing. The body is `{ version, id, event, occurredAt, site, origin, actor: { kind, id, name }, data, links }`:

- `version` is `1`. It changes only with a change of the body that a receiver of the current version could misread; a new field is not one. A redelivery sends the body as it was first sent;
- `id` is the event's, the same for a redelivery;
- `data` is `{ type, documentId, locale, status, version }` for content, where `status` is what the event leaves the version as (`draft`, `published`, `unpublished`, `trashed`, `purged`, `scheduled` or `unscheduled`), `{ tags }` for `cache.invalidated`, and `{ assetId, folderId, fileName }` for a media event;
- `links` holds the `management` and `delivery` paths of the entry, or the `management` path of a media asset, relative to the root of the instance behind its path base.

The headers are `X-FluentCMS-Event`, `X-FluentCMS-Delivery` (the same for every attempt of a delivery) and `X-FluentCMS-Signature: t=<unix>,v1=<hex HMAC-SHA256(secret, t + "." + body)>`, with a `v1` for each secret that signs, the current one first. A receiver recomputes the HMAC over `t + "." + body`, compares it with each `v1` in constant time and accepts the body when any matches, refuses a `t` more than five minutes from its own clock, and ignores a delivery id it has already handled. A non-2xx answer, no answer within `FluentCms:Webhooks:Timeout` (10 seconds) or a network error fails the attempt. The job records each attempt and schedules the next one itself, 1m, 5m, 30m, 2h and 24h after a failure on the host's `TimeProvider`, so a delivery has six attempts and the last failure is `dead`. The job itself fails only when the database does. A disabled or deleted webhook gets no further attempts.

**Dead deliveries.** When the last attempt of a delivery fails, or is taken over as abandoned, the job raises `WebhookDeadEvent` with the webhook's id and name, the delivery id, the event and the number of attempts; its activity row is `webhook.delivery_dead` with `deliveryId`, `event` and `attempts`. A ping or a redelivery, tried once, raises none, and neither does a delivery cut short because its webhook was disabled. The webhook stays enabled by default, because turning a receiver off that nobody asked to turn off would surprise an operator. `FluentCms:Webhooks:DisableAfter` (unset by default, and at most `Retention`) opts in: when a delivery dies and the first attempt that failed after both the webhook's last success and its last change was claimed at least that long ago, so that a webhook turned back on or pointed elsewhere starts afresh, the job disables the webhook, logs event 9250 and raises `WebhookDisabledEvent`, the activity row `webhook.disabled` with `failingSince`. The delivery log is the record of the attempts, so no column is added, and the check runs only when a delivery dies. GitHub disables a hook after 30 days of failures and Stripe after 3 days with an email. This answers item 7 of [#2887](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2887).

**Queue and claims.** Every attempt runs on the job queue `webhooks`: the first attempt of a delivery, its retries, a redelivery and a ping. The plugin adds the queue with `AddJobQueue`, with `FluentCms:Webhooks:Workers` workers on each instance (default 2, and 0 for none), so a receiver that hangs holds the workers of that queue for its timeout and no others: scheduled publishing, trash purge and the retention jobs keep the `default` queue's. `FluentCms:Jobs:Queues:webhooks`, or the host's own `Queues` in code, wins over `Workers`. A job claims its attempt before it sends it, by inserting the attempt's row as `pending`. `ux_cms_webhooks_delivery_attempt` makes that insert the claim, and `ux_cms_webhooks_delivery_in_flight`, unique over `webhook_id` where the state is `pending`, lets a webhook have one attempt in flight at a time across every instance. Then the job sends, schedules the next attempt when one follows, and writes the result to the row. The next attempt is scheduled first because a second job for it finds it claimed and sends nothing, while a result written without its next attempt would end the delivery. A job that finds:

- its attempt recorded stops, so a repeat of the job, or a second job for one delivery when the outbox runs a handler again, sends nothing;
- its attempt pending schedules itself again for the moment the claim would be abandoned;
- another attempt of its webhook pending schedules itself again for when that attempt's send must have ended, its claim plus `Timeout`, and at least 2 seconds later, with up to 2 seconds of jitter. It holds no worker meanwhile, and a burst to a receiver that hangs wakes its waiting jobs once per send rather than every few seconds.

A pending row claimed more than `Timeout` plus one minute ago is abandoned: its worker stopped, or could not write the result, before recording it. The minute covers the writes around the send and the clocks of the other instances. The next job to find it, of the same attempt or of another delivery to the webhook, takes it over: the row becomes `failed` (`dead` for the last attempt, a ping or a redelivery) with the error "The worker stopped before it recorded the result of this attempt.", and the next attempt is scheduled on the usual schedule. The attempt is not sent again, so a failed write after a send never sends that attempt twice. Its result is not known, so the delivery goes on, and the receiver may get it once more as the next attempt, which `X-FluentCMS-Delivery` lets it ignore. A row's `created_at` is when it was claimed, and `manual` marks a ping or a redelivery, so that a job that takes over its claim knows it is tried once. This answers decision 17 of the review of 7 Nov 2026 ([#2847](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2847)): a `webhooks` queue, with one attempt in flight per webhook ([#2880](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2880), [#2882](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2882)).

**Body and header contract.** This answers decision 12 of the review of 7 Nov 2026 ([#2847](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2847)). Header values are secrets, as above. Before 1.0 the body keeps its own shape with `version` 1, and the headers keep `X-FluentCMS-*` with the `t=<unix>,v1=<hex>` signature. Neither CloudEvents 1.0 (`specversion`, `type`, `source`, `id`, `time`, `data`) nor the Standard Webhooks headers (`webhook-id`, `webhook-timestamp`, `webhook-signature`) are adopted now. Either can come later as a format a webhook opts into, with the current shape as its default, so version 1 does not break.

**Thin payloads.** The body names the entry and what the event left it as, and carries none of its fields. That stays the default: it holds nothing a receiver's own key could not read, it cannot go stale between attempts, and it stays small. A receiver that needs the entry fetches it on receipt: it takes `data.type`, `data.documentId` and `data.locale` and reads the entry at `links.delivery` with a Delivery key and `locale`, or at `links.management` with a Management key for a draft, so it gets the entry as its key sees it and as it is now. A retry that arrives hours later then still leads to the current entry, and `data.version` tells the receiver whether what it read is older or newer than the event. An opt-in that puts the entry in the body, as the subscriber's key would see it, is left for later; it would be a new field of version 1. This answers item 11 of [#2887](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2887).

**Log.** `cms_webhooks_delivery` holds a row per attempt: the state (`pending` while it is sent, `succeeded`, `failed` with `next_retry_at`, or `dead`), the status code, the duration, the body sent, the first 1024 characters of the answer and the error. `GET /{id}/deliveries` pages them newest first (`page`, and `pageSize` up to 100). `POST /{id}/deliveries/{deliveryId}/redeliver` sends the body of a delivery again, and `POST /{id}/ping` sends a `ping` event. Each is a new delivery that is tried once, even when the webhook is disabled, and answers 202 with its `deliveryId`. The recurring job `webhooks.retention` (daily at 04:15 UTC, stored once the host has started) removes attempts older than `FluentCms:Webhooks:Retention` (30 days) in batches of 500, with a 10 minute attempt timeout, so that a large prune does not hold a worker for good; the batches before it are committed, so the attempt the runner retries goes on where it stopped.

**Per-site enablement.** Core gates the plugin by site (4.13) and the plugin checks nothing itself. In a site where it is disabled, an event records no `webhooks.{event}` row, its endpoints answer 404 and a `webhooks.deliver` job of that site completes without sending. `webhooks.retention` has no site and runs. A retry that was already scheduled when the plugin was disabled completes without sending, so the attempt before it keeps the state `failed` with its `next_retry_at`.

**Imports.** An event of an import (`Origin.Import`) is delivered only when the import was started with `webhooks=true`, which its correlation id tells (4.11, B-92). The body's `origin` is then `import`.

**Codes.** `FCMS1560` an invalid field, `FCMS1561` an invalid URL, `FCMS1562` a URL that is not public, `FCMS1563` an unknown event, `FCMS1564` no such webhook, `FCMS1565` no such delivery and `FCMS1566` a name that is taken. Event ids 9240 to 9251.

**Deleted sites as built ([#3001](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3001)).** The plugin hears `SiteDeletedEvent` through the outbox (`webhooks.cleanup.site.deleted`, `DeletedSiteWebhooks`) and removes the webhooks of the site with every attempt in their delivery log, in one transaction, and logs how many (event 9251). No `WebhookDeletedEvent` is raised for them, since no one could read the activity of the site; the `site.deleted` row of the instance stands for them. A delivery job of the site that is still scheduled finds its webhook gone and completes without sending (event 9243). A failure is tried again by the outbox, and a second delivery finds nothing.

### 5.3 GraphQL plugin

Row 2.16; decision 24.

HotChocolate schema built from `SchemaModel` at startup: one object type per content type and block, input types for create and update, filter inputs mirroring the REST operators, sort inputs, offset and cursor pagination, population through selection. Mutations per type: create, update, delete, publish, unpublish. One endpoint `/graphql` authenticated like REST; a delivery key sees queries over published content only, a management principal sees mutations its roles allow. Depth and complexity limits, introspection off in Production for unauthenticated callers, optional persisted queries. `IGraphQLSchemaContributor` lets plugins add types.

**As built (B-75).** `FluentCMS.Plugins.GraphQL` holds `GraphQLPlugin`, named `graphql`, on HotChocolate 16 (`HotChocolate.AspNetCore` 16.7.5, Q9). It maps HotChocolate's HTTP endpoint at `/graphql` with `MapGroup("/graphql")`: `POST`, and `GET` for queries, with no subscriptions, uploads, batches, schema download or IDE. The group's sign-in requirement is lifted with `AllowAnonymous()`, so an anonymous caller is the Public role of its site, as on Delivery. The request bridge sets the operation, with `Origin.Api`, or `Origin.Admin` for the admin client; the per-site gate answers 404 (`FCMS1550`) where the plugin is disabled; and the group is not in the output cache. The plugin's [README](../../src/plugins/FluentCMS.Plugins.GraphQL/README.md) is the guide, and [ADR 0006](../decisions/0006-one-graphql-schema-per-instance.md) records the choice of one schema per instance.

- **Schema.** A hosted service builds the schema once, when the host starts, from `SchemaModel` and the field type registry, so options that are not valid (`FCMS1640`) or two names of the content schema that give one GraphQL name (`FCMS1641`) stop the host. The schema is named `FluentCMS`, so a host with a HotChocolate schema of its own keeps the default one. A content type `site-settings` is the object type `SiteSettings` with the system fields of the response shape (4.4) beside its own, a component `seo` is `SeoComponent`, a block `hero` is `HeroBlock`, a blocks field is a list of a union (`ArticleSectionsBlock`), and a select is an enum whose values are its options in upper case (`ArticleCategory`), or a `String` when an option cannot be a name. A relation is the target's type. Rich text, `json`, and a field type a plugin stores as a reference, such as media, are the scalar `JSON`; any other field type gets `String`, `Long`, `Decimal`, `Boolean`, `DateTime`, `Date` or `ID` from the JSON schema it gives the OpenAPI document, so a plugin's field type needs no GraphQL code. A hidden field is in no type and a read-only one in no input. A relation inside a block or a repeated component is an `EntryReference` (`id`, `type`), since those are stored whole and never populated. Reverse relations have no field (4.4).
- **Queries.** A collection has `articleList(filter, sort, search, page, offset, pageSize, cursor, locale, status, fallback, richText)`, which answers `{ items, total, page, pageSize, nextCursor }`, and `article(id, locale, status, fallback, richText)` with one more argument per unique field, such as `slug`, as the `by/` route; a singleton has one field. The arguments become the `ContentQuery` REST builds: `status` is published unless `DRAFT` is asked, a missing `locale` is that of the request, `locale` is checked by the locale resolver and `all` is refused (`FCMS2201`), and `total` is counted only when it is selected. An entry that is not there is null.
- **Filters and sort.** `ArticleFilter` has `and`, `or`, `not`, the system fields and every field whose field type has filter operators, each with an input of those operators (`eq ne in nin lt lte gt gte between contains startsWith null notNull`, and `hasBlock`). A single component nests its fields and a relation takes its target's id or, under `where`, its target's fields, which become dotted paths. The reader builds the AST of the bracket parser, operand for operand: a value is the text REST would send, the conditions of a level are joined by `And`, and `null: false` is `notNull`. A test holds the two readers to the same tree for the same conditions, and another the same entries. Sort is a list of `{ field, direction }` over an enum of the type's sort keys.
- **Population.** The selection is the read: every selected field the principal may read goes to `Select`, a relation whose fields are selected goes to `Populate` with those fields as dotted paths, level by level, and a media field, a block list and a repeated component are populated for the references inside them. The content service batches each level and refuses a path deeper than `MaxPopulateDepth` (`FCMS2207`), so the depth cap is REST's. A relation selected for its `id` alone is not populated.
- **Mutations.** `createArticle(input, locale)`, `updateArticle(id, input, expectedVersion, locale)`, `deleteArticle(id)`, `publishArticle(id, expectedVersion, locale)`, which answers `{ entry, warnings }`, and `unpublishArticle(id, locale)` call `CreateAsync`, `PatchAsync`, `DeleteAsync`, `PublishAsync` and `UnpublishAsync`. `update` is a merge patch, as `PATCH`: an input shows which fields it names, so one it leaves out keeps its value, which is the safer reading of an update. The input becomes the JSON document the Management API reads from a body; a relation and a media field are ids, and a block list is JSON, each block with its `type`, since an input cannot be a union. A write returns what the content service returned, read again as a draft only when the selection goes into a relation or a media field. Scheduling, versions, bulk, the trash and discarding a draft stay with the Management API.
- **Enforcement.** The plugin references the SDK and `FluentCMS.Plugins.Abstractions` alone, reaches content through `IContentService`, and decides nothing: the content service refuses a draft to a Delivery key (`FCMS2209`) and every write to a principal without the `management` scope (`FCMS3103`, or `FCMS3102` when not signed in). Before a read, the plugin asks `IContentSurface.ReadableFieldsAsync` (4.4) for each content type the selection reaches. A type the principal may not read is null with `FCMS3104`, the code of REST's 404, at its root field or its relation; a field it may not read is left out of the read and is null with `FCMS2202`, the code REST gives a query that names it. Each is reported once in a response, and the rest is answered.
- **Errors.** A root field catches the exceptions of the content service and reports each as a GraphQL error at its path, described by `IProblemDescriber` (4.13): the REST detail as `message`, and `code`, `status` and the validation `errors` with their field paths in `extensions`, with the `current` entry of a version conflict (`FCMS3105`). Exception details are never included, in any environment; an unexpected failure is `FCMS3199` and is logged.
- **Limits.** `FluentCms:GraphQL` has `MaxDepth` (12), `MaxFieldCost` and `MaxTypeCost` (1000 each, HotChocolate's cost analysis, where a root field costs 10, a populated relation or media field 1 per entry of its page, and every other field nothing), `MaxRequestSize` (256 KiB, refused with 413 and `FCMS3111` as REST refuses a body over its cap) and `ExecutionTimeout` (30 seconds). A query over the depth or the cost limit is refused before a field runs (`FCMS1644`, `FCMS1645`). The page size limits are REST's, enforced by the content service (`FCMS2207`), and the list field tells the cost analysis the default page size. Fields run one at a time, since the content service and its context belong to the request. The endpoint is limited by `RequireCallerRateLimit()` (4.10).
- **Introspection.** Off for the schema and allowed per request: always in Development, and elsewhere for a signed-in caller for whom `IPermissionChecker` allows `schema.read` in the site of the request; `FluentCms:GraphQL:OpenIntrospection` overrides the environment. Any other caller gets `FCMS1643`.
- **Persisted queries.** `PersistedQueries` names a folder whose `{id}.graphql` files are read once at start, and a request names one by `id`. `OnlyPersisted` refuses a request that carries a query of its own (`FCMS1646`); an unknown id is `FCMS1647`.
- **Contributors.** `IGraphQLSchemaContributor.Configure(ISchemaBuilder)`, in the plugin's own package, is called once for every contributor registered as a singleton, after the content types. The architecture tests let a plugin reference the package for it (3.4).
- **Left for later.** Relay connections, a typed `Media` object (the media plugin can contribute one once it takes the dependency), typed block inputs, the `fallbacks` map of a read and `locale: all`. A plugin group takes the Delivery CORS policy, which allows `GET` and `HEAD`, so a browser on another origin cannot `POST` to `/graphql` until a group can name its own methods.

**Codes.** `FCMS1640` options that are not valid, `FCMS1641` a name conflict, `FCMS1642` a single entry named by no argument or by several, `FCMS1643` introspection not allowed, `FCMS1644` a query too deep, `FCMS1645` a query too costly, `FCMS1646` only persisted queries, `FCMS1647` no such persisted query and `FCMS1648` an execution that timed out; `FCMS1649` to `FCMS1659` are free. Event ids 9320 to 9322, of 9320 to 9329.

### 5.4 MCP plugin

Rows 9.1 to 9.13; 9.16 to 9.21 as Should; decisions 50 to 52.

**Server.** Streamable HTTP at `/mcp` through `ModelContextProtocol.AspNetCore`, enabled per site, in-process. Authentication: bearer tokens from the authorization server or API keys. RFC 9728 protected resource metadata at `/.well-known/oauth-protected-resource`; the authorization server metadata is OpenIddict's discovery document; dynamic client registration (RFC 7591) at `/connect/register` is implemented over OpenIddict's application manager. A registration creates a pending client that an administrator approves and assigns a role before tokens are issued; an open-with-rate-limit mode is a setting for trusted setups (Q11).

**Principal.** Every call runs as the authenticated user or key, with the same `IAuthorizationEvaluator`, site and locale scoping. The tool list is filtered to what the principal may do.

**Tools.** For each exposed type (`Mcp:ExposedTypes`, default all readable types, capped): `{type}_list`, `{type}_get`, `{type}_search`, `{type}_create_draft`, `{type}_update_draft`, `{type}_discard_draft`, `{type}_delete`. Shared: `publish`, `unpublish`, `schedule` (require the publish permission), `query` (type plus filter JSON for the long tail), `media_list`, `media_search`, `media_get`, `media_upload` (URL or base64), `media_update`. Input schemas are generated from `SchemaModel` filtered by field permissions.

**Resources and prompts.** `fluentcms://schema`, `fluentcms://schema/{type}`, `fluentcms://sites/{site}/guidelines`, `fluentcms://types/{type}/instructions`. Prompts from `IMcpPromptProvider` implementations and from editors, stored in `cms_mcp_prompt`.

**Safety.** `dryRun` defaults to true on delete and bulk tools and returns the plan with a `confirmationToken` (HMAC of the plan, five-minute validity); execution requires the token; bulk operations need an explicit cap; per-client call limits, payload caps, result truncation with paging.

**Origin tagging.** `Origin.Mcp`, client id and principal on every write.

**Instructions and clients.** `cms_mcp_instruction (site_id, type nullable, text, updated_*)` feeds resources and tool descriptions. `/api/management/v1/mcp/clients` lists OpenIddict applications flagged as MCP clients with last activity and revoke; `/mcp/prompts` and `/mcp/instructions` manage the rest.

**Server as built** ([#2977](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2977)). `FluentCMS.Plugins.Mcp` is the plugin `mcp`. It references the SDK, `FluentCMS.Plugins.Abstractions` and `ModelContextProtocol.AspNetCore` 2.4.0, has no table, and holds no tool, resource or prompt of its own until B-72 and B-73: it serves what plugins add through `FluentCMS.Agents`.

- **Endpoint.** `POST /mcp`, mapped with `MapProtectedResource("/mcp", ...)` (4.7, "Protected resources as built") and the package's `MapMcp()`. The transport is stateless: every POST is a server of its own with the services of its request, so any instance answers any request (4.15), nothing is kept between requests, and there is no `GET` stream, no session, no `DELETE` and no legacy SSE endpoint. A client of a revision up to `2025-11-25` connects with `initialize` and gets the server's name, title and version and the `tools`, `resources` and `prompts` capabilities, and `ping` answers; a client of `2026-07-28` or later, whose revision has neither, discovers the same and calls without them. The server offers no subscriptions and no list-changed notifications.
- **Callers.** The `FluentCms` scheme: a bearer token with the `mcp` scope, or an API key. A Management key works as it does on the Management API, and a Delivery or Preview key reads what its class reads and writes nothing, since the evaluator lets a principal manage through `Origin.Mcp` with the `mcp` or the `management` scope (4.8). The 401 and 403 answers, the metadata documents and the `resource` parameter are core's (4.7).
- **Principal.** The request bridge opens the operation of a request with `Origin.Mcp`, the client id of the token (the key id for a key) and the authenticated principal, in the site and locale the resolvers of core give (`X-Cms-Site` or `?site=`, `X-Cms-Locale` or `?locale=`, the host, or the binding of a key). Every provider call runs inside it, so a version and an activity row written through a tool carry the origin, the client and the principal. The plugin reads no database context and asks no permission, which an architecture test holds it to (3.4).
- **Providers.** `IMcpToolProvider`, `IMcpResourceProvider` and `IMcpPromptProvider` (`FluentCMS.Agents`, in the SDK, with no reference to the protocol package), added with `AddMcpTools<T>()`, `AddMcpResources<T>()` and `AddMcpPrompts<T>()` on `IPluginServiceBuilder` as scoped services. `IMcpProviders`, which core implements, lists them with the plugin that added each, in plugin order, and the server does not ask the providers of a plugin that `IPluginState` says is disabled for the site of the request. A list request (`McpListRequest`) and a call (`McpToolCall`, `McpResourceRead`, `McpPromptGet`) carry the `OperationContext`, and a provider lists what the principal may use. A tool is an `McpToolDescriptor`: a name of 1 to 128 letters, digits, underscores, hyphens and dots, a description, the JSON Schema of its arguments, an optional title and output schema, and `McpToolAnnotations` (`ReadOnly`, `Destructive`, `Idempotent`, `OpenWorld`), whose default is a tool that changes data and may destroy it; the server writes all four hints out, since a client reads a missing one as the protocol's default. A descriptor is a record, so that the server can copy it with a longer description (B-73). A resource is an `McpResourceDescriptor` with a URI, or a URI template (RFC 6570) when the URI has an expression, which is listed under `resources/templates/list`; a prompt is an `McpPromptDescriptor` with its `McpPromptArgument`s. A call answers `null` when the name or the URI is not the provider's, and the server asks the next provider; when two providers list one name or URI, the one of the earlier plugin is served and the other is logged. A provider that throws while it lists is left out of that answer and logged, so one broken plugin does not empty the list of the others.
- **Results and errors.** A tool answers an `McpToolResult`: text, structured content, or `Error(code, message)`. A failure is a result with `isError`, whose text names the code first (`FCMS3103: ...`) and whose `_meta` carries it as `fluentcms/code`, so a model and a program both read it. An exception a provider lets through is described by `IProblemDescriber` (4.13) and answered the same way, with the code the REST APIs give it and each validation error on a line of its own; for a resource read or a prompt it is a JSON-RPC error with the code in its message, invalid params for a status below 500 and an internal error otherwise. A name or URI no provider answers for is a JSON-RPC error too, as the protocol asks: `FCMS1600` for a tool and `FCMS1602` for a prompt with invalid params, and `FCMS1601` for a resource with the resource-not-found code, or invalid params from revision `2026-07-28` on.
- **Per site.** Where the plugin is disabled for a site, `/mcp` answers 404 (`FCMS1550`) after the caller is checked, and keeps serving the other sites.
- **Options and permission.** `FluentCms:Mcp` has `Name` (`fluentcms`; 1 to 64 letters, digits, hyphens, underscores and dots) and `Title` (`FluentCMS`), which the handshake and the resource metadata carry; a bad value stops the host. The permission `mcp.manage` is in the catalogue for the endpoints of B-71 and B-73.
- **Telemetry.** A tool call is a span of the `FluentCMS` activity source named `mcp {tool}` once a provider answers for the name, and `mcp tool` for a name no provider has, so a name a client made up never names a span. It carries `fluentcms.operation`, `fluentcms.site` and, for a failure, the Error status and `fluentcms.code`, and never an argument or a result. Requests count in `fluentcms.requests` under the surface `mcp`. Log event ids 9280 to 9283: 9280 a tool call with its tool, principal, client, site, outcome and duration, 9281 an exception that says nothing of its cause, 9282 a provider that failed to list, and 9283 a tool, resource or prompt left out of a list.
- **Codes.** `FCMS1600` no such tool, `FCMS1601` no such resource and `FCMS1602` no such prompt; `FCMS1600` to `FCMS1639` are the MCP plugin's.
- **Decisions.** The audience of a token is not narrowed to `/mcp` and the `mcp` scope is the restriction, since the authorization server and the resource are one instance and a client may ask for a token without naming a resource. The server is one per host: the protocol package keeps one set of server options in the container, so a host that maps an MCP endpoint of its own with the same package shares them. `/mcp` takes the Management CORS policy, whose allowed headers do not include the protocol's, so a client that runs in a browser is not served yet.

**Clients as built** ([#2978](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2978)). An MCP client registers itself at core's `/connect/register` (4.7, "Dynamic client registration as built"), and the plugin brings the policy, the Management endpoints and the cleanup; core keeps the clients, since the plugin references neither core nor the OpenIddict stores (3.4).

- **Policy.** The settings area `mcp` (`McpSettings`, permission `settings.mcp.manage`): `clientRegistration` (`approval`, the default, `open` or `off`), `defaultClientRole` (the role of a client under `open`), `maxPendingClients` (100) and `registrationsPerHour` (10). The plugin's `IMcpClientRegistrationPolicy` answers core with the instance defaults of the area on every registration and discovery request, since the instance has one authorization server; a site's own row of the area changes nothing of it. A change reaches another instance within the 30 seconds a settings read is kept (4.13).
- **Management API.** `GET /api/management/v1/mcp/clients` answers `{ items }`, each with `id`, `clientId`, `name`, `status`, `roleId`, `roleName`, `redirectUris`, `scopes`, `registeredAt`, `approvedBy`, `approvedAt` and `lastActivityAt`, the newest registration first. `POST /mcp/clients/{id}/approve` with `{ "role" }`, a role id or the name of an instance-wide role, approves a pending client, `POST /mcp/clients/{id}/reject` rejects one, and both answer the client; `DELETE /mcp/clients/{id}` removes a client of any status with its sessions and tokens and answers 204. `{id}` is the id of the client in the store, not its OAuth client id. `McpClientService` checks `mcp.manage` with `IPermissionChecker` for the instance, so only a grant with no site limit reaches the clients, which belong to no site; it is a named exception to the architecture rule that the plugin evaluates no permission (3.4), which still holds for the server. The role given to a client need not be one the caller holds, since it only limits (4.8).
- **Cleanup.** The recurring job `mcp.client-cleanup` (daily at 04:45) removes the clients that were not approved within 7 days of their registration, the rejected ones included, and logs how many (event 9284). A hosted service stores its schedule once the host has started and tries again while the database is out of reach (9285).
- **Startup report.** `mcp-open-client-registration` is listed while the policy is `open` in Production. The report is made once per start, so a change of the setting shows at the next one.
- **Codes.** `FCMS1603` no such MCP client (404), `FCMS1604` the client is not pending (409), `FCMS1605` an approval without a role (422) and `FCMS1606` no such role (422); `FCMS1607` to `FCMS1611` are kept for this area. The refusals of `/connect/register` are core's and carry no code of the plugin.

**Tools as built** ([#2979](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2979)). The plugin adds two tool providers of its own, `McpContentTools` and `McpMediaTools`, with `AddMcpTools`, as any plugin would.

- **What the caller may do.** The plugin asks no permission, so the list is filtered through a seam of the SDK that core answers: `IEffectiveAccess` (`FluentCMS.Authorization`). `ContentAsync` gives the schema as the principal of the operation may read it in its site and locale, narrowed as the schema group narrows it (4.10) but without `schema.read`, and for each type whether it may read drafts and whether some grant lets it create, update, delete, publish and unpublish, with the readable fields some grant lets it set on a create and on an update, a grant with a row rule included, since the entry is not known. `Search` says whether the `ISearchIndex` slot is filled. `HeldAsync` says which of the permissions named a grant gives the principal in the site, a grant limited to resources included (4.8). It describes and enforces nothing: the content and media services check every call. `IPermissionChecker` stays out of the plugin, as the architecture test asks.
- **REST forms.** The tools answer and read through `IContentSurface` (4.4, "Other surfaces as built"), which this issue gave the JSON of a list, a publish result and a schedule, `ParseQuery` and `ParseSchedule`. A read tool turns its JSON arguments into those parameters, so `{ "title": { "eq": "A" } }` is `filter[title][eq]=A`, the items of `or` and `and` take their index, and the values of `in`, `nin` and `between` are joined by commas with the grammar's escapes; core's parser then builds the query, which is why a tool and a route read the same entries, and a query the grammar refuses carries the code of the REST APIs and names the parameter.
- **Types.** `FluentCms:Mcp:ExposedTypes` names the types the tools reach, every type when empty, and a name the schema does not have stops the host. Of the exposed types the caller may read, the first `MaxExposedTypes` (10) in the order of the schema, which is by name, get tools of their own; the others are reached through `query`, and a warning (event 9288) names each once per process. A collection has `{type}_list`, `_get`, `_search` (only while `IEffectiveAccess` says the search slot is filled), `_create_draft`, `_update_draft`, `_discard_draft` and `_delete`, each listed with its permission; a singleton has `_get`, `_update_draft` and `_discard_draft`, without an id, and its update creates the entry when it has none and `expectedVersion` is 0 (`FCMS1613` otherwise). `publish`, `unpublish`, `schedule` and `query` take a `type` and are listed while the caller holds their permission for some exposed type, with those types as the `enum` of `type`. A descriptor of a type's tool carries `ContentType`, and `McpRequests.ListToolsAsync` adds the instructions the site saved for that type (B-73, `McpInstructions.ByTypeAsync`) to the description of each such tool that a provider listed for the caller, after the tool's own text; a shared tool and a tool of another type get none, so the instructions of a type reach only a caller its tools are listed for.
- **Calls.** `_update_draft` is `PatchAsync`, a merge patch, because a tool that replaced the draft would clear every field an agent left out. Create and update run in the locale of the connection, and the reads, `publish`, `unpublish`, `schedule` and `_discard_draft` take a `locale`. A read answers the draft by default where the caller may read drafts, as the Management API does, and what is published otherwise, so a Delivery key reads through the same tools. `_delete` calls `DeleteAsync` at once; the dry run of B-74 goes in front of it. A tool of a type the caller may not read, of a type that is not exposed, or of no type is the same unknown tool (`FCMS1600`), and a shared tool answers such a type with `FCMS1612`, so a name tells nothing of the schema. An argument the tool does not take, or of the wrong kind, is a `ValidationException` on its name (`FCMS2108`, `FCMS2101`), answered like a body of the Management API.
- **Schemas.** The input schema of a write tool is built from the narrowed schema: `fields` has the fields the caller may write, without the hidden and read-only ones, each from `IFieldType.OpenApiSchema`, the fragment the OpenAPI document uses, with its references to components and blocks moved under `$defs` and the OpenAPI-only `discriminator` taken out. `additionalProperties` is false. A required field says so in its description and is not in `required`, since a draft may leave it out; a relation names its target type, and on an update a single value may be `null`. The read tools name the readable fields in their descriptions.
- **Media.** `media_list`, `media_search`, `media_get`, `media_upload` and `media_update` call `IMediaService`, which the plugin resolves by its contract and may not find. They are listed and answered only where it is registered and `IPluginState` says the plugin `media` is enabled for the site, to a caller that `HeldAsync` says holds `media.read`, `media.upload` or `media.update`; elsewhere they are unknown tools. `MediaQuery.Search` (new in the SDK, and `search` on `GET /media`) finds a text of 1 to 256 characters in the title, the file name or the tags, whatever the case. The answers are the JSON of the media routes.
- **Upload by URL.** `McpDownloads` takes `https` only, no user name or password, and a literal address only when it is public; a host name is resolved on the connection, every address is checked with `PublicAddresses.IsPublic` (`FluentCMS.Plugins.Abstractions`, the check the webhooks plugin had, now shared), and the socket connects to the addresses checked. Redirects are followed by hand, at most three, each held to the same rules, with no proxy, cookies, credentials or decompression. The stream is opened on its first read, so the media service checks the permission, the folder and the settings before anything is fetched, and it stops reading at the upload limits of the site (`FCMS1582`). `UploadTimeout` (30 seconds) covers the request and the body. A refused URL is `FCMS1614`, a fetch that fails `FCMS1615`, and the log names the host only (event 9289).
- **Hints.** The read tools are read-only and idempotent; `_create_draft` and `media_upload` are not destructive, and `media_upload` is open world; the update, discard, delete, publish, unpublish, schedule and `media_update` tools keep the destructive default, since they overwrite or remove what is there.
- **Plugin list.** `GET /plugins` (4.10) names the tools of each plugin under `contributions.tools`: core asks the tool providers of `IMcpProviders` for the caller of the request, as a tool list does, skipping a plugin disabled for the site and a provider that fails (event 9236).
- **Codes.** `FCMS1612` a type that is not available, `FCMS1613` a singleton without an entry, `FCMS1614` an upload URL refused and `FCMS1615` one not fetched.

**Resources, instructions and prompts as built** ([#2980](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2980)). The plugin now has two tables and serves resources and prompts of its own, through an `IMcpResourceProvider` and an `IMcpPromptProvider` it adds like any plugin.

- **Tables.** `cms_mcp_instruction (id, site_id, type nullable, text, updated_at, updated_by)`, with one row per site where `type` is null, the guidelines, and one per site and type, each held by a unique index, and `cms_mcp_prompt (id, site_id, name, title, description, arguments json, template, updated_at, updated_by)`, unique by site and name. They are the stream `cms_mcp__migrations`, one migration, `InitialMcp`, in a set per provider (4.3, "Plugin migration sets per provider as built"); the plugin references the two EF provider packages and `System.Security.Cryptography.Pkcs` with `PrivateAssets="all"`, as the webhooks plugin does. The sets are checked offline against the model and against each other, and the SQLite set runs in the plugin's tests; the PostgreSQL and SQL Server sets have not been applied to a server.
- **Schema resources.** `fluentcms://schema` and `fluentcms://schema/{type}` are what `ISchemaIntrospection` gives the caller (4.10, "Schema group as built"), as the JSON the Management API serves at `/schema` and `/schema/types/{type}`: a type the caller may not read is left out and answers as an unknown resource (`FCMS1601`), like a type that does not exist, and a field it may not read is left out. A caller without `schema.read` is refused with `FCMS3103`, which the plugin learns from `IProblemDescriber` when it lists, so that such a caller is not listed the schema either.
- **Guidelines and instructions.** `fluentcms://sites/{site}/guidelines` names the site by its id and is served for the site of the request only, to every caller of it; another site's are read with a request to that site. `fluentcms://types/{type}/instructions` is served to a caller that reads the type, by the same question to `ISchemaIntrospection`, so that no name of another type shows. What is saved is listed under `resources/list`, and the two forms with `{type}` under `resources/templates/list`. The guidelines are also the `instructions` of the answer to `initialize`: `HttpServerTransportOptions.ConfigureSessionOptions` sets `McpServerOptions.ServerInstructions` from the site of the request, on options that are the request's own since the server is stateless, at the cost of one read of the site's instruction rows per request, which the same request's lists reuse. Instructions of a type that is no longer in the schema are left out of the resources and of what the plugin hands to tool descriptions, and stay in the Management list, marked `orphaned`, to be deleted. `McpInstructions` (scoped, internal) is where the rest of the plugin reads them: `GuidelinesAsync(site)` and `ByTypeAsync(site)`.
- **Stored prompts.** `prompts/list` and `prompts/get` serve the stored prompts of the site of the request beside the providers' prompts, to every caller of the site. A name is 1 to 64 lower case letters, digits, underscores, hyphens and dots, so that two names never differ by case alone, which the providers' collations would not agree on. A template names an argument as `{{name}}` and has no expressions: an argument it names must be declared (`FCMS1627`), and a `{{` that opens no argument is refused, so a template reads one way only. `prompts/get` puts each value in as it is, in one pass, as one user message; a required argument that is missing or empty is refused with `FCMS1628`, an optional one is left empty and an undeclared one is ignored. A stored prompt is refused at write time when a provider of a plugin that is enabled in the site lists a prompt of its name to the caller (`FCMS1626`, compared without case); a provider that fails to list is passed over, and where a provider's prompt appears later under a stored name the earlier plugin's is served, as for any two providers.
- **Management API.** `MapManagement("/mcp/instructions")`: `GET` lists the rows of the site, `PUT` and `DELETE` on the group are the guidelines and on `/{type}` the instructions of a type, from `{ "text" }`. `MapManagement("/mcp/prompts")`: `GET` and `POST` on the group, and `GET`, `PUT` and `DELETE` on `/{id}`. Two groups, so that the prefix `/mcp/clients` of B-71 is its own. The services check `mcp.manage` in the site of the request with `IPermissionChecker` (4.13), and a prompt of another site is not found. The architecture test of 3.4 names the model, the two readers and the two services as the plugin's own data, and holds the rest of the plugin, the server included, to reading no database context and asking no permission.
- **Limits.** A text of guidelines or instructions has at most 4000 characters, a template 16000, a prompt 20 arguments and a site 100 stored prompts; a text takes no control character other than a line break or a tab. The texts are data shown to an agent and are never run.
- **Events.** `McpInstructionSavedEvent`, `McpInstructionDeletedEvent`, `McpPromptCreatedEvent`, `McpPromptUpdatedEvent` and `McpPromptDeletedEvent`, raised after the commit as the webhooks plugin raises its own, with the activity rows `mcp.instruction_saved`, `mcp.instruction_deleted`, `mcp.prompt_created`, `mcp.prompt_updated` and `mcp.prompt_deleted`. The target is `mcp_guidelines`, `mcp_instruction` with the type as its id, or `mcp_prompt` with the id and the name. No event or row holds a text.
- **Codes and log ids.** `FCMS1620` a text that cannot be saved, `FCMS1621` a type that is not in the schema, `FCMS1622` nothing to delete, `FCMS1623` a prompt that cannot be saved, `FCMS1624` no such stored prompt, `FCMS1625` a name another stored prompt of the site has, `FCMS1626` a name a provider's prompt has, `FCMS1627` a template that names an undeclared argument and `FCMS1628` a required argument that is missing. Log event ids 9291 a change of instructions, 9292 a change of a stored prompt, 9293 a stored prompt left out because its arguments cannot be read and 9294 the rows of a deleted site removed.
- **Deleted sites** ([#3001](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3001)). The plugin hears `SiteDeletedEvent` through the outbox (`mcp.cleanup.site.deleted`, `DeletedSiteMcpData`) and removes the site's guidelines, type instructions and stored prompts, through `RemoveAllAsync` of the two services, which check no permission and raise no event, since no one could read the activity of the site. A failure is tried again by the outbox, and a second delivery finds nothing.

**Safety as built** ([#2981](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2981)). No table, no migration and no package: the plugin still references the SDK and `FluentCMS.Plugins.Abstractions` only, and reads `IDataProtectionProvider` from the shared framework, as the media and webhooks plugins do. The one seam is `IContentSurface.ToJson(BulkResult)` (4.4), so that the `bulk` tool answers as the bulk route does.

- **Dry runs.** `{type}_delete` and `bulk` take `dryRun`, true unless the call says false, and `confirmationToken` (`McpDryRuns`, scoped). A dry run changes nothing and answers `{ "dryRun", "type", "action", "count", "entries", "references", "confirmationToken", "expiresAt" }`. `entries` holds `id`, `title`, `status` and `version` of each entry, read through `IContentService.QueryAsync` as the caller, the draft where it may read drafts, in the order of the ids. The schema names no title, so `title` is the text field called `title` or `name`, or else the first text field. `references` are the entries whose relation would break, `{ "type", "id", "title", "field" }`: one query by relation field of each exposed type the caller may read (`filter[field][in]`, 4.4, "Reverse relations as built"), against the drafts for a delete and against what is published for an unpublish, none for a publish, 50 at most, with `moreReferences` when there may be more. Relations inside components and blocks are not looked for, and the query runs in the locale of the connection. A plan without an entry carries no token.
- **Token.** `McpConfirmations` (singleton). The token is `v1.{expires}.{entries}.{key}.{mac}`: the Unix second it ends, the SHA-256 of the ids, versions and statuses of the entries in the order of their ids, the key wrapped by Data Protection, and an HMAC-SHA256 with that key over one JSON object of the tool, the arguments (without `dryRun` and `confirmationToken` and without null values, the keys of every object in ordinal order), the principal (kind and id), the client id, the site, the locale of the operation, the end and the hash of the entries. The key is 256 random bits an instance makes on its first token and wraps once under the purpose `FluentCMS.Plugins.Mcp.ConfirmationKey`; an instance that reads a token with another wrapped key unwraps it with the key ring, so every instance that shares the ring (4.7) accepts the token of another, and one with another ring refuses it. Only the ring makes a wrapped key of that purpose, so a caller cannot bring a key of its own. The time is `TimeProvider`'s and a token lasts five minutes; the MAC is compared in constant time and checked before the end and the entries are read.
- **Run.** With `dryRun` false the server reads the plan again and checks the token against it: missing is `FCMS1630`, a token of another tool, arguments, principal, client, site or locale, or one this ring did not make, `FCMS1631`, an expired one `FCMS1632`, and a plan that has changed, which is an entry with a new version or status or another set of entries, `FCMS1633`. Nothing runs for any of them, and each is logged (event 9296). The token is not spent, since no instance keeps state: it confirms again for its five minutes while the plan stands, which a run that changed the entries ends. The check and the write are not one transaction, so a change between them is not seen; a delete can be undone from the trash, and a bulk publish passes the draft version of the plan as `ExpectedVersion`, so a draft changed since is a conflict. The plugin asks no permission: a dry run reads as the caller, and the service refuses the run of a caller without the permission.
- **Bulk.** The shared tool `bulk` takes `type`, `action` (`publish`, `unpublish`, `delete`), `ids` or `filter`, a required `limit` and `locale`. It is listed while `IEffectiveAccess` says a grant gives the caller one of the three operations for some exposed type, with those actions and types as the `enum`s. `limit` is at most `FluentCms:Mcp:MaxBulkItems` (100, up to `BulkRequest.MaxItems`); a limit over it, more ids than the limit, and a filter that matches more than the limit are `FCMS1635`, with nothing run. The filter is the list's, read by `ParseQuery`, and an empty one is refused. The plugin reads the filter itself, for the plan and again for the run, at one entry more than the limit, and then calls `BulkAsync` with the entries of the plan as `Items`, never with the filter, so the run touches what was confirmed and no other entry; an entry the caller may read and not change fails on its own (`FCMS3103`), where `BulkAsync` by filter would have left it out. The answer is `IContentSurface.ToJson(BulkResult)`, with `notFound` for the ids that name no entry the caller reads, which are not run.
- **Call limits.** `McpRequests.CallToolAsync` counts each call before a provider is asked (`McpCallLimits`, singleton): per client, which is the client id of the token or the key id of an API key, and the principal where there is neither. `Limits:CallsPerMinute` (120) holds every call and `Limits:WritesPerMinute` (30) the calls of tools that are not read-only. Whether a tool is read-only is what its provider lists for the caller: the server asks the enabled providers in order until one lists the name, remembers a read-only one for a minute for that principal and site, and counts a name no provider lists as a write. A bucket is the generic cell rate algorithm of core's limits (4.10) on `TimeProvider`, per instance. A call over a limit is answered as a tool error, `FCMS1634`, with the seconds to retry in its text and under `_meta` as `fluentcms/retryAfter`, not as a 429; its span keeps the name `mcp tool`, since no tool answered, and it is logged once a minute per client (event 9295) and not as a tool call. Core's Management rate limit still counts the principal across its clients.
- **Sizes.** An endpoint filter on `/mcp` refuses a body over `MaxRequestBytes` (1 MiB) by its `Content-Length` before the protocol reads it, and lowers the server's body limit for a body sent in chunks; the answer is the 413 and `FCMS3111` of core's cap (4.10), which runs first, so the lower of the two holds. `{type}_list`, `{type}_search` and `query` page as the engine pages, with its default and largest page size in the input schema. A list whose JSON is over `MaxResultBytes` (256 KiB) is read again at the number of entries that fit, by its offset or its cursor, so that the engine makes the cursor of the entry it ends at, and answered with `truncated`, that number as `pageSize` and the `nextCursor`; an entry is never cut, and one that is over the limit alone is answered alone. A text of more than `MaxListTextLength` characters (500) is shortened in those lists with a marker that names its length and `{type}_get`, which answers it whole; `query` leaves the texts of a type over `MaxExposedTypes` whole, since that type has no `_get`. The media lists are pages of at most 100 assets and are not cut.
- **Hints.** `{type}_delete` and `bulk` are destructive, and their descriptions say that a confirmation is needed and how to give it.
- **Codes and log ids.** `FCMS1630` a run without a token, `FCMS1631` a token that is not of the call, `FCMS1632` an expired token, `FCMS1633` a plan that has changed, `FCMS1634` a client over its call limit and `FCMS1635` a bulk call over its limit. Log event ids 9295 a client over a limit and 9296 a confirmation refused.
- **Decisions.** The limits are per client as the issue asks, so a client id that many users share is one bucket, and a principal with several clients has one bucket for each, under core's limit per principal. A dry run counts as a call of its tool, a write. The token carries the status of each entry beside its version, so that an unpublish ends its own token. `tools/list` is not paged, and results of tools other plugins add are not cut.

**The stdio proxy (B-91).** `fluentcms mcp --url <instance>` (6.1) carries an MCP client that speaks stdio only to `/mcp`, with a management API key; the plugin is unchanged.

### 5.5 AI assist plugin

Rows 9.14, 9.15; 9.16 to 9.20 as Should; decision 53.

Microsoft.Extensions.AI `IChatClient` with providers for OpenAI, Azure OpenAI, Anthropic, Ollama and OpenAI-compatible endpoints, configured per site with keys from configuration or Data Protection encrypted settings. Actions: rewrite, shorten, expand, fixGrammar, title, summary, seo, translate, altText. Endpoint `POST /api/management/v1/ai/actions/{action}` with `{ type, field, locale, text or documentId, options }` returning `{ suggestion, usage }`, optionally streamed over SSE. Results are suggestions; nothing is written to content by the plugin. `cms_ai_usage (site_id, user_id, action, provider, model, prompt_tokens, completion_tokens, duration_ms, succeeded, created_at)`. Permission `ai.use`; per-type and per-field opt-in in settings.
