**As built (B-83, [#2994](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2994)).** `FluentCMS.Plugins.AiAssist` holds `AiAssistPlugin`, named `ai`, on Microsoft.Extensions.AI (`Microsoft.Extensions.AI` 10.11.0, `Microsoft.Extensions.AI.OpenAI` 10.11.0, which brings `OpenAI` 2.14.0, `Anthropic` 13.8.0 and `OllamaSharp` 5.5.2, Q9). It references the SDK and `FluentCMS.Plugins.Abstractions`, and the reference host adds it. The plugin's [README](../../src/plugins/FluentCMS.Plugins.AiAssist/README.md) is the guide.

- **Settings.** The settings area `ai` (`AiSettings`, permission `settings.ai.manage`), per site with instance defaults: `provider` (`openAi`, `azureOpenAi`, `anthropic`, `ollama`, `openAiCompatible`, or `none`, the default, which leaves AI assist off), `model`, `endpoint`, `apiKey` (a secret of the settings, 4.13, so it is written and never returned), `maxOutputTokens` (1024), `requestTimeout` (60, in seconds), `callsPerMinute` (20) and `fields`, the opt-in: a map of content type to the fields that take actions. A field that takes actions is a text, long text or rich text field of a content type that is neither hidden nor read-only. A key that the settings do not hold comes from `FluentCms:Ai:{Provider}:ApiKey`. `FluentCms:Ai` (`AiOptions`) also has `AllowedEndpoints`, `MaxInputLength` (20,000) and `UsageRetention` (90 days), and a value that is not valid stops the host.
- **Endpoints.** An endpoint is an absolute `http` or `https` URL with no user, query or fragment. One that a site names is used only when it is one of `FluentCms:Ai:AllowedEndpoints`, by scheme, host, port and path, so that a site administrator cannot point the server at an address inside the network (`FCMS1684`, and event 9366 names the host). An endpoint is checked against the list at every call and not only at a save, so one that was saved while it was on the list is refused once the host runs with a list that no longer holds it; the list itself is read when the host starts, so a change to it applies after a restart. The clients follow no redirect, so an allowed endpoint cannot lead the server elsewhere, and they keep no cookies. OpenAI and Anthropic use their own endpoints unless one is set, and the other providers need one (`FCMS1685`, as for a missing model or key). The configuration's key for `OpenAi` or `Anthropic` goes to the provider's own endpoint only: a site that names another endpoint for one of them holds its own key. A key of the instance defaults is held to the same idea. A site that first saves its settings without a key keeps the key of the defaults, since a secret that is not sent is kept (4.13, "Settings as built"), so a site administrator could otherwise name another allowed endpoint and have the operator's key sent there. The default factory therefore reads the instance defaults when a site's settings hold a key: one that equals the key of the defaults is sent only to the provider and the endpoint of the defaults, and a site that names another is refused (`FCMS1685`) until it sets a key of its own. Core's settings are unchanged. The key is told by its value as the defaults hold it now, so after it is replaced in the defaults a site's older copy counts as the site's own, and the replaced key is to be revoked at the provider.
- **Providers.** OpenAI, Azure OpenAI and any OpenAI-compatible endpoint go through `Microsoft.Extensions.AI.OpenAI`; Azure through its OpenAI-compatible endpoint, with the deployment as the model and an API key, so `Azure.AI.OpenAI` is not added. Anthropic goes through the `IChatClient` of the `Anthropic` package, whose own spans are turned off since its environment can ask them to carry the messages, and Ollama through `OllamaSharp`. A call is one request: the retries of the packages are off, so that a call is paid for and logged once.
- **Slot.** The plugin declares the provider slot `IAiClientFactory` (4.13): `CreateAsync(site, settings)` gives an `AiClient`, the `IChatClient` with the names of its provider and model, or null for a site with no provider. It is a factory per site, where the plan had one `IChatClient`, because a single client cannot follow the settings of each site; it takes the settings because a slot is a singleton and settings are read in a scope. The default builds the client from the settings, and a host that fills the slot brings any other `IChatClient`, such as one that signs in to Azure with an identity, and decides itself which endpoints it calls. The factory owns its clients, which the plugin does not dispose. The interface is in the plugin, since the SDK takes no Microsoft.Extensions.AI dependency.
- **Actions.** `POST /api/management/v1/ai/actions/{action}`, mapped with `MapManagement("/ai")`, for `rewrite`, `shorten`, `expand`, `fixGrammar`, `title`, `summary`, `seo`, `translate` and `altText`; another action is 404 (`FCMS1681`). The body is `{ "type", "field", "locale", "text" or "documentId", "options" }`, where `type` and `field` name the field the suggestion is for. With `documentId` the plugin reads that field of the draft through `IContentService` as the caller, a rich text field as Markdown. `locale` is an enabled locale of the site and the locale of the request when left out. `translate` takes `options.targetLocale`, an enabled locale of the site, and `altText` takes `options.mediaId` and needs no text: it sends a copy of the image that fits 1024 by 1024 pixels and carries no metadata, read through `IMediaService` as the caller (`GetAsync`, then `OpenImageAsync`, 5.1), where the media plugin is in the host and enabled for the site (`FCMS1691` otherwise, and for an asset that is no image). The answer is `{ "suggestion", "usage" }`, where `usage` is `{ "provider", "model", "promptTokens", "completionTokens", "durationMs" }` and `suggestion` is text, or for `seo` an object with `metaTitle` and `metaDescription`, read from the JSON object the model is asked for, with or without a code fence around it. An answer with no text, or for `seo` without the two, is `FCMS1690` (502). Every answer is `no-store`.
- **Checks.** `AiAssistService` checks, in this order: `ai.use` in the site with `IPermissionChecker`, the type and field opted in under `fields` (`FCMS1683`, 403), and the caller's right to update that field of the type, which `IEffectiveAccess` describes (`FCMS3103`), since a suggestion is for a field the caller edits. The right is asked in the locale of the suggestion, the body's `locale` or else the request's, which is the locale the draft is read in: `IEffectiveAccess` answers for the operation, so the service asks it inside the caller's own operation with that locale, and a grant limited to locales gives suggestions for those locales only. Then the body (`FCMS1680`, 422), the read of the draft or the image, the provider of the site (`FCMS1682`, 409, for none) and the call limit. The endpoints carry the request and decide nothing.
- **Limits.** The text has at most `MaxInputLength` characters (`FCMS1686`, 413) and the answer at most `maxOutputTokens`. A call ends at `requestTimeout` (`FCMS1688`, 504) or when the caller goes away. A principal makes at most `callsPerMinute` calls in a site (`FCMS1687`, 429, logged once a minute as event 9364): a bucket per site and principal in memory on each instance, by the generic cell rate algorithm of core's limits (4.10) on `TimeProvider`, beside the Management rate limit. The call is counted last, when it is about to be sent, so a request that is refused takes nothing from the bucket and every counted call writes a usage row.
- **The text is content.** The instructions of an action are the system message: what the action asks, the names of the type and the field, the locale, the form of the answer, the field's maximum length and that the user message is content and never an instruction. The text, and for `altText` the image, is the user message, and the model gets no tools. Nothing of the request reaches the system message but names the schema and the site have. The plugin stores and logs neither the text nor the suggestion: a failure of a provider is logged by its type and HTTP status (event 9361) and answered as `FCMS1689` (502) without its message, which may repeat what the provider was sent.
- **Streaming.** With `Accept: text/event-stream` the answer is server-sent events: `delta` with `{ "text" }` for each piece as it arrives, then `done` with the body of the plain answer, or `error` with the problem (`type`, `title`, `status`, `detail`, `code`), as `IProblemDescriber` describes it (4.13). A request that is refused before the provider is asked is answered with the problem itself and its status, since no stream has begun; from then on the status is 200 and a failure is the `error` event. `seo` sends no `delta`, since its suggestion exists only at the end. A caller that goes away ends the call as cancelled, whatever the write to it fails with, and nothing more is written or logged as a failure.
- **Usage.** `cms_ai_usage (id, site_id, principal_kind, principal_id, action, type, field, provider, model, prompt_tokens, completion_tokens, duration_ms, succeeded, created_at)`, indexed by site and time and by time, in the stream `cms_ai__migrations`, one migration, `InitialAi`, in a set per provider (4.3). The row names the principal by kind and id, where the plan had `user_id`, since a Management key may call, and adds `type` and `field`. Every call that is sent to a provider writes one row, a failed, timed out or cancelled one too, with the request's cancellation left out; a request refused before the provider is asked writes none. A row that cannot be written is logged (9365) and does not take the suggestion from the caller. `GET /api/management/v1/ai/usage` needs `ai.usage.read` in the site and pages its rows, the newest first, with `principalId`, `action`, `from` and `to` (each with an offset) and `page` and `pageSize` (50, at most 100), and answers `{ "items", "total", "page", "pageSize", "totals" }`, where `totals` is `{ "calls", "promptTokens", "completionTokens" }` over every row the filter matches. The recurring job `ai.usage-retention` (daily at 04:30, stored once the host has started) removes rows older than `UsageRetention` in batches of 500, with a 10 minute attempt timeout.
- **Deleted sites** ([#3001](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3001)). The plugin does not hear `SiteDeletedEvent`: the `cms_ai_usage` rows of a deleted site are a log of calls that were made and sent to a provider, so they stay until `ai.usage-retention` removes them with the rest, after `UsageRetention`. No one reads them in the meantime, since `GET /ai/usage` reads the rows of the site of the request and the site is gone. The site's `ai` settings are core's and go with the site.
- **Per site and telemetry.** Core gates the plugin by site (4.13): where it is disabled, both endpoints answer 404 (`FCMS1550`). A call is a span of the `FluentCMS` activity source named `ai {action}`, with `fluentcms.operation`, `fluentcms.site`, `fluentcms.type`, `fluentcms.provider` and `fluentcms.outcome` (`succeeded`, `failed`, `timeout` or `cancelled`), and for a failure the Error status and `fluentcms.code`. The counter `fluentcms.ai.calls` counts the calls by `fluentcms.operation`, `fluentcms.provider` and `fluentcms.outcome`, on a meter of the plugin's own that is named `FluentCMS` like core's, so that a host that listens to that name hears it.
- **Codes and log ids.** `FCMS1680` a request that cannot be read (422), `FCMS1681` no such action (404), `FCMS1682` no provider (409), `FCMS1683` a field that is not opted in (403), `FCMS1684` an endpoint that is not allowed (409), `FCMS1685` a provider that is not configured (409), `FCMS1686` a text over the input limit (413), `FCMS1687` a caller over its call limit (429), `FCMS1688` a timeout (504), `FCMS1689` a provider that failed (502), `FCMS1690` an answer that cannot be used (502) and `FCMS1691` no image to describe (422); `FCMS1692` to `FCMS1699` are free. Log event ids 9360 a call with its caller, field, provider, outcome, duration and tokens, 9361 a provider that failed, 9362 and 9363 the retention, 9364 a caller over its limit, 9365 a usage row that could not be written, 9366 an endpoint refused and 9367 a failure while streaming, of 9360 to 9379.
- **Left for later.** Token budgets and billing, embeddings and semantic search (9.16) with the `IEmbeddingGenerator` slot, provenance flags on versions (9.18), suggestions on upload (9.20) and AI actions as MCP tools.

**Planned for M7 (10.4, H2 and H8).** B-103 ([#3048](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3048)) cuts the providers to `openAi`, `azureOpenAi` and `openAiCompatible`, which share the client of `Microsoft.Extensions.AI.OpenAI`, and removes the `Anthropic` and `OllamaSharp` packages with their clients. Ollama is reached as `openAiCompatible` through its `/v1` endpoint, which needs no key, and a model without an OpenAI-compatible endpoint through a gateway or the `IAiClientFactory` slot. [#3005](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3005) then replaces the comparison of a site's key with the key of the defaults: core no longer copies an inherited secret into the site's row, and the plugin asks whether the key is the site's own. Until those issues are merged, the text above is what is built.

### 5.6 Multi-site plugin

Rows 7.10 to 7.13 (beyond the core model), 7.15 and 7.18 as Should.

`cms_site_host (site_id, hostname, is_primary)` and a middleware resolving the site from the request host before the core resolver's header and parameter rules. Site creation beyond the default, per-site storage provider selection (Should), cross-site copy with relation remapping (Should). Q6a fixes what is core and what is plugin.

**Multi-site as built (B-80).** `MultiSitePlugin` (`multi-site`) in `FluentCMS.Plugins.MultiSite` references the SDK and `FluentCMS.Plugins.Abstractions` only, and the reference host adds it. It has no option, setting or permission of its own. Its README has the routes and the rules for a host.

- **Table.** `cms_multi_site_host (hostname, site_id, is_primary, created_at)` in the stream `multi-site`, with a set per provider. The table carries the plugin's prefix (`FCMS1523`), so it is not the `cms_site_host` of the plan. `hostname` is the key, since a host name is one site's in the whole instance, and `site_id` has an index and no foreign key, as a plugin's model does not hold core's tables.
- **Host names.** A DNS name in lower case, an internationalized name as its A-label, at most 253 characters in labels of 1 to 63 letters, digits and hyphens. A scheme, a user, a path, a query, a space and a trailing dot are refused, and so is an IP address, which names a server and not a site (`FCMS1660`). A wildcard is `FCMS1661` and a port `FCMS1662`. A name that a site has is `FCMS1663` (409), for the same site too.
- **Primary host.** A site with hosts has exactly one primary host: its first host, whatever the request says, until another is added or patched with `isPrimary: true`, which takes its place. `isPrimary: false` on the primary host and the removal of the primary host while the site has others are refused (`FCMS1665`, 409), so the primary host is removed last. No index holds this on every database, since a filter on a boolean is written differently on each. The writes of host names run one at a time on every instance together, under the lock `fluentcms:multi-site-hosts` of `IDistributedLock`, and each is one `SaveChanges`. A write that does not get the lock within 45 seconds is refused (`FCMS1666`, 503) and changes nothing. The primary host is data for a front end, and the backend redirects nothing by it.
- **Resolver.** The plugin registers an `ISiteHostResolver`, so the order of 4.6 holds as built and no middleware runs ahead of the header and the parameter: an explicit header or parameter wins over the host name, and an administrator on one host name manages any site. The host is `HttpRequest.Host` without its port, which is the forwarded host when the reverse proxy options take it (4.15). It is looked up in the stored form: lower case, an internationalized name as its A-label, and without the root dot of a fully qualified name. Each instance reads the table whole and keeps it for 30 seconds (`SiteHostMap.CacheDuration`). A write drops the copy at once, so the instance that wrote resolves by the change on the next request and another instance within 30 seconds. A read of the table that fails fails the request, because the default site would otherwise answer on another site's host name. The resolver is the instance's and is not gated per site.
- **Routes.** `MapManagement("/sites")`, beside the routes of core: `POST /sites` `{ "name", "slug", "defaultLocale" }` answers 201 with the site and `DELETE /sites/{id}` 204, both through `ISiteAdministration` (4.6). `GET /sites/{id}/hosts` answers `{ "items" }` with the primary host first, `POST /sites/{id}/hosts` `{ "hostname", "isPrimary" }` 201 with `{ "siteId", "hostname", "isPrimary", "createdAt" }`, `PATCH /sites/{id}/hosts/{hostname}` `{ "isPrimary": true }` the host, and `DELETE /sites/{id}/hosts/{hostname}` 204. A host of another site is not found through this one (`FCMS1664`), and an unknown site is `FCMS2301`. Every route needs `sites.manage` for the instance, not for the site: the host names are one namespace, and a host name decides which site every request on it reads. Where the plugin is disabled for the site of a request, the routes answer 404 (`FCMS1550`).
- **Events.** A host change raises `SiteUpdatedEvent` with the site as its source, which drops core's copy of the site and evicts its Delivery copies, and writes `site.host-added`, `site.host-updated` or `site.host-removed` through `IActivityLog`, with the host name in the details. A `PATCH` that changes nothing raises nothing. The plugin hears `SiteDeletedEvent` in process and removes the hosts of the site. If that fails, the rows are left: such a host name reads the default site (4.6), and the next site that asks for the name takes it (event 9344).
- **Codes and log ids.** `FCMS1660` to `FCMS1666`, of the range `FCMS1660` to `FCMS1679`. Event ids 9340 to 9344, of 9340 to 9359.

**Decisions as built (B-80).** Settled for [#2991](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2991):

- **Resolver, not middleware.** The site is resolved by `ISiteHostResolver` in the order of 4.6.
- **Permission.** Host names need `sites.manage` for the instance.
- **Media.** The media library is shared by default, through an option of the media plugin (5.1).
- **Out of scope.** Redirects to the primary host, wildcard host names, CORS origins per site, per-site storage providers and cross-site copy (7.15 second half, 7.18).
- **Identity.** The authorization server and the hosted pages stay on the public origin, with one issuer for every site (decision 7). A host name only decides the site of a request.

**Deleted sites as built ([#3001](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3001)).** A site is deleted only when it has no content (`FCMS2303`), and core deletes its own rows with it (4.6). What the first-party plugins keep for the site goes after the commit, each through its own handler of `SiteDeletedEvent`:

- **Multi-site.** The host names, in process, as above, so that a name is free for another site at once.
- **Delivery copies.** Core's eviction evicts `site:{id}` through the outbox (`cache.site.deleted`), with the output cache's `CacheInvalidatedEvent` for a CDN, rather than leaving the copies to expire after `OutputCacheDuration`.
- **Webhooks** (5.2), **MCP** (5.4) **and media** (5.1). The webhooks with their delivery log, the guidelines, instructions and stored prompts, and the library of the site with its files, each through the outbox, so that a failure is tried again, the caller of the delete does not wait for the files, which the 5 second timeout of an in-process handler would cut short, and a second delivery finds nothing or goes on with what is left. A file that cannot be deleted is logged and left to the media cleanup, and does not undo the deletion of the site.
- **AI assist** (5.5). The `cms_ai_usage` rows stay until the retention job removes them, as a log.
- **GraphQL.** Nothing, since it keeps no rows.

### 5.7 Search, SSO providers, rendering

Search (2.23, 8.11): `ISearchIndex` slot in the SDK from the start; the plugin provides PostgreSQL FTS and SQLite FTS5. It was a Should and is built in M7 (10.4, H6).

**Search plugin as planned for M7 (B-104, [#3049](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3049)).** `SearchPlugin`, named `search`, fills the slot and changes nothing in core. It keeps one row for a document, a locale and a state (draft or published) in `cms_search_entry`, in a migration stream of its own, with the text of the fields marked `Searchable`: a `tsvector` with a GIN index on PostgreSQL, an FTS5 table on SQLite, and on SQL Server a match on a part of the text without a full-text catalogue. Every word must match and the last one matches as the start of a word. Handlers of the entry events keep the rows current on the outbox, `SiteDeletedEvent` removes the rows of a site, and the job `search.rebuild` indexes content that existed before the plugin. A type without a `Searchable` field, and a site where the plugin is disabled, refuse `q=`. Ranking by relevance, stemming by language and an external index stay out.

**Search seam as built.** Core defines the seam and the plugin fills it: `ContentQuery.Search`, the `q=` parameter of the Delivery and Management lists, and the `ISearchIndex` slot, whose core default refuses `q=` with `FCMS2211` (4.4, "Search as built"). The index finds ids and the engine narrows the query to them, so the row rule, site, locale and status still decide what a caller reads. `Searchable` (4.2) is the plugin's to read, and how it keeps its index current, such as from the entry events, is its own. Storage hooks in core (`tsvector` with GIN on PostgreSQL, an FTS5 external-content table on SQLite, a full-text catalogue on SQL Server, through `IStorageConventions`) are out of scope for now and stay additive, as does ranking by relevance. The Entra ID, Okta and Keycloak presets of generic OIDC are in core (4.7); other presets and SAML (4.18) are Should. The rendering plugin (Blazor components and Razor tag helpers) is Should and mostly not backend; its server pieces (rich text to HTML, draft preview middleware, sitemap) reuse `RichText/` renderers from core.

## 6. Tooling that targets the backend

Rows 11.1 to 11.6, 11.12, 11.13.

### 6.1 CLI

`fluentcms` dotnet tool; standalone binaries follow after 1.0 (decision 59; 10.3, P12). Works against the host project (`--project`) or a running instance (`--url`, with a management API key in `--token` or `FLUENTCMS_TOKEN`; 10.3, P3). The command line is parsed with System.CommandLine (10.3, P2).

**Project commands run inside the host (10.3, P1).** The command logic is in Core. A host calls `app.RunFluentCmsCommandAsync(args)` between `Build()` and `Run()`; the tool builds the project and starts its assembly with `fluentcms <command>`, so a command runs with the host's own FluentCMS.Core, configuration and plugins, as the system with `Origin.Cli`, and before any hosted service starts. The reference host makes the same call, so `docker run <image> fluentcms migrate` is what an init container runs. `migrations add` and `migrations remove` stay in the tool's own process, because they need EF Core's design-time package, which a host does not get.

| Command | Does | Mode |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `migrations add <name>`, `migrations remove` | content migration stream for the host's provider; `add` reports possible renames and refuses a destructive change without `--allow-destructive` | project, in the tool |
| `migrations list`, `migrations script` | applied and pending migrations per stream; the SQL of a stream | project |
| `migrate [--check]` | applies all streams in order; `--check` lists pending and exits non-zero | project |
| `schema snapshot`, `schema validate`, `schema diff` | snapshot, validation errors, drift between code, an instance, a schema file or the applied history, with rename detection (4.14) | project, `--url` |
| `schema apply [--script [--output]] [--dry-run] [--allow-destructive]` | brings the content tables of a host whose types are data up to its schema, on every provider (4.3) | project |
| `generate csharp`, `generate typescript`, `generate openapi` | from the schema snapshot of a running instance, of the host or of a schema file; `--check` for CI | project, `--url` |
| `export`, `import [--dry-run]` | transfer packages (4.14) | project, `--url` |
| `apikey create`, `apikey list`, `apikey revoke` | a key bound to a role, printed once; the keys; revoking one | project, `--url` |
| `user create` | an active administrator, with the password from a prompt or standard input | project |
| `user invite`, `user add-role` | an invitation by email; a role for a user | project, `--url` |
| `user reset-password` | the reset email, or with `--password-stdin` a new password | project |
| `jobs list`, `jobs retry <id>` | the newest jobs; a dead-lettered job queued again | project, `--url` |
| `jobs run <name> [--inline]` | a run of a recurring job, queued or in the command's process | project |
| `seed [--name]` | runs the registered seeders (4.16) | project |
| `mcp` | stdio to Streamable HTTP proxy for IDE clients | `--url` |
| `backup`, `restore` | SQLite snapshot with the local media files (4.15) | project |
| `doctor` | the startup report without starting the host; with `--url`, an instance's diagnostics | project, `--url` |

`new host` and `new plugin`, which only wrapped `dotnet new`, are dropped: the templates are used through `dotnet new` (6.2).

**The framework as built (B-90, [#3013](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3013)).** The tool (`FluentCMS.Cli`) parses with System.CommandLine 2.0.15, which only it references. Its tree is a command named `fluentcms`, not a `RootCommand`, which would take its name from the assembly, with help and `--version`; `migrate` and `migrations` with `add`, `remove`, `list` and `script` are its commands. Exit codes are 0, 1 for a failure and 2 for bad usage, which prints the parser's errors and the help of the command to standard error. Every project command takes `--project <path>` (the current directory by default), `--assembly <path>` (a built host; nothing is built, and `--project` is only its directory), `--no-build` and `--environment <name>` (`ProjectOptions`). `InstanceOptions` holds `--url` and `--token` for the commands of B-91, B-93, B-97 and B-101: the address is http or https with no user, query or fragment, the key comes from `--token` or `FLUENTCMS_TOKEN`, neither is ever written out, and a missing or invalid one is `FCMS3316` with exit code 2.

A command that runs inside the host is checked by the tool and handed on as arguments: the tool builds the project unless told not to (its notice goes to standard error), reads `TargetPath`, `RootNamespace` and `MSBuildProjectDirectory`, and runs `dotnet exec <TargetPath> fluentcms <command> <options>` (the muxer of `DOTNET_HOST_PATH` when set, `dotnet` from `PATH` otherwise) in the project directory with the caller's environment, `DOTNET_ENVIRONMENT` and `ASPNETCORE_ENVIRONMENT` set from `--environment`, and `Logging__Console__LogToStandardErrorThreshold=Trace` unless the caller set it, so that standard output is the command's own (`ProcessHostLauncher`). The tool's own options are not handed on, and a path such as `--output` is made absolute first. On the console the host writes to it directly; the tool waits for it, and Ctrl+C reaches both. The exit code is the host's. A build that fails is `FCMS3310`, an assembly that is not there `FCMS3311`, and a host that cannot be started `FCMS3312`. The tool never loads the host's assembly for these commands.

Inside the host, `FluentCMS.Core.Commands` has the one public type `FluentCmsCommandExtensions` with `RunFluentCmsCommandAsync(this IHost, string[], CancellationToken)`, which answers null when the arguments do not start with `fluentcms`, and the exit code otherwise. Core reads the words after `fluentcms` with its own small parser (`CommandParser`), which knows the commands of the internal `CommandRegistry`, their options as `--name value` or `--name=value`, their positional arguments and `--help`; bad usage is `FCMS3300` with the usage of the command and exit code 2. Each command is a `HostCommand` (name, description, arguments, options, `RunAsync(CommandContext, CancellationToken)`); the context gives the scope's services, the parsed arguments and the two writers. A command runs in a service scope under `SystemOperation.Begin` with `Origin.Cli` in the default site, and before any hosted service has started, so nothing migrates, validates, seeds, runs jobs or listens. A `CommandException` carries its code and exit code; a `FluentCmsConfigurationException` exits 1 with its message; anything else, a cancellation included, is `FCMS3302` with its type and a message that passed through `SecretScrubber`, and the log (9172) keeps the exception with its stack, scrubbed the same way. A test checks that every command of the registry is in the tool with the same options. `AddFluentCms` registers `CommandEntryPointCheck` first among its hosted services: a host started with arguments that begin with `fluentcms` and that never called the entry point stops in the Starting phase, before the production checks and the migrations, with `FCMS3301`, which names the line to add to `Program.cs`; the migrations at start ask the same, for a host that starts its services concurrently and so does not stop at the first that fails. Codes `FCMS3300` to `FCMS3307` (host) and `FCMS3310` to `FCMS3316` (tool), and log event ids 9170 to 9172, of the ranges `FCMS3300` to `FCMS3319` and 9170 to 9179.

`migrations add` and `migrations remove` stay in the tool's process (`HostProject`). `remove` asks the host with `migrations list --json` whether its database has the newest content migration, and refuses with `FCMS3314` when it has, or with `FCMS3315` when the host cannot answer; `--force` skips the question and leaves the database alone; a host with no content migration is `FCMS3313`. `migrations list --json` writes one line, `{"streams":[{"name","applied","pending"}]}`, which is the form the tool reads.

**The schema tools and doctor as built (B-97, [#3020](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3020)).** `schema snapshot`, `schema validate`, `schema diff` and `doctor` are commands of the host (4.14, 4.15) and of the tool, which adds `--url` and `--token` to `snapshot`, `diff` and `doctor` and keeps them; it never hands them on. With `--url` the tool reads the instance itself through `InstanceClient`, which sends the key as `Authorization: ApiKey <key>`, and diffs and prints with the host's code, which it reaches as a friend assembly of Core. A refused key is `FCMS3330`, which names the permission (`schema.read`, `system.read`), and an instance that cannot be reached or answers another error `FCMS3329`; a host that does not write its snapshot for a diff is `FCMS3331`. `migrations add` takes `--allow-destructive` (4.14). Codes `FCMS3320` to `FCMS3326` (host) and `FCMS3327` to `FCMS3331` (tool); `FCMS3332` to `FCMS3334` are free. No log event id was added.

**The commands that administer an instance as built (B-91, [#3014](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3014)).** In project mode they are `HostCommand`s that call the services the Management endpoints call (`ApiKeyService`, `UserAdministration`, `InvitationService`, `PasswordResetService`, `JobAdministration`, the seed runner), as the system with `Origin.Cli`, so their events and activity rows carry that origin and reach the log through the outbox, which a running instance delivers. With `--url` the tool calls the Management API with `Authorization: ApiKey <key>` from `--token` or `FLUENTCMS_TOKEN`, as the key's principal, so the API decides what the key may do; a refusal prints the API's problem with its code first (the errors of a validation problem one per line) and exits 1, an instance that cannot be reached is `FCMS3329`, as for the schema tools, and an answer that is not FluentCMS's `FCMS3361`. The project options and `--url` exclude each other. A role is named by `--role` and `--site` (the site's role, or an instance-wide one without `--site`), and a site by its slug; with `--url` the roles come from `GET /roles` and the sites from `GET /me/sites`, the sites the key holds a grant in. List commands print a table, and with `--json` one line in the shape of the Management API, which is the same in both modes.

- `apikey create --name --class --role [--site] [--types <list>] [--expires <date>]` writes the key alone on standard output, once, and what describes it to standard error; with `--json` the line is the API's answer, which holds the key as `secret`. A key is bound to one role (4.7), so `--role` is required, and the issue's `--scopes` is `--types`, the content types the key is bound to, since a key's only scope is its class. With `--site` the role may be the site's or an instance-wide one, which the binding narrows. `--expires` is UTC unless it names an offset. `apikey list` never shows a key again; `apikey revoke <id>` keeps the row.
- `user create --email --role [--site] [--password-stdin]` makes an active user whose address is its user name and counts as confirmed (a command applies no migration, so a new database is migrated first, which also seeds the built-in roles), with a password checked by Identity's policy (`UserAdministration.CreateAsync`, which no endpoint calls). The password is read at the terminal without an echo and asked twice, or from the first line of standard input with `--password-stdin`, never from an argument; with standard input redirected and no `--password-stdin` the command refuses (`FCMS3352`, exit 2). The prompt goes to standard error. `user reset-password --email` sends the reset email of the hosted pages, and with `--password-stdin` sets the password as a reset does, which ends the user's sessions. `user invite` and `user reset-password` build the link from `FluentCms:PublicOrigin`, since a command has no request (`FCMS3354` when it is unset). `user create` and `user reset-password` exist in the host only: over HTTP a user is invited, so that a key alone never sets a password, and the API sends no reset email.
- `jobs list [--queue] [--status]` shows the newest 100 jobs. `jobs run <name>` takes the name of a recurring job, whose row holds the payload and queue of its runs (an instance adds its recurring jobs when it first starts), and adds a job due now with `Origin.Cli`, which an instance that works the queue runs. With `--inline` the job is written as already claimed by the command's process, with one attempt, and run there through the job executor with its heartbeat; a failure is dead-lettered (`FCMS3356`, exit 1) rather than left for an instance that may never start, and `jobs retry <id>` queues it again. `jobs run` exists in the host only, since the API has no endpoint that enqueues a job.
- `seed [--name <seeder>]` runs the seeders added with `cms.Seeding.Add` through the runner a start uses, with its claim and versions (4.16), whatever `RunOnStart` says, so a second run does nothing; `--name` limits the run to one seeder. A claim held by another run is `FCMS3358` with exit 1, after what was applied is listed.
- `mcp --url` is a proxy from stdio to Streamable HTTP (decision 51; 5.4): each line of standard input is posted to `/mcp` with the key, and the answer, JSON or server-sent events, is written to standard output one message per line, as UTF-8 whatever the console's encoding is. It keeps the `Mcp-Session-Id` an instance gives and ends that session when standard input ends, and sends the `MCP-Protocol-Version` of the answer to initialize. It reads no content and adds no tool. Messages are forwarded as they come, so that requests a client sends together are not held behind each other. A 401 or 403 ends it with `FCMS3362` and the problem on standard error and exit 1. Any other answer that is not a JSON-RPC 2.0 message (a problem such as a rate limit, an expired session's 404) and a request that does not reach the instance are written to standard error, and the request gets a JSON-RPC error, so that the client does not wait, and the proxy goes on; its HTTP client has no timeout, since a tool call takes as long as it takes. Only protocol messages go to standard output. It opens no GET stream, since the MCP plugin serves Streamable HTTP statelessly and sends its messages on the answer to each POST.

Codes `FCMS3350` to `FCMS3359` are the host's and the tool's (an unknown role, site, recurring job or seeder, and a value that is not valid, are bad usage with exit 2), and `FCMS3361` to `FCMS3363` the tool's, the last a warning on standard error when a key goes over plain http to a machine other than this one; `FCMS3360` and `FCMS3364` to `FCMS3369` are free. A service's refusal inside the host prints each error with its code, as the API does, and exits 1. No migration, package or SDK type was added.

**Schema apply as built (B-98, [#3021](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3021)).** `schema apply` is a command of the host and of the tool, which hands on `--script`, `--output` (made absolute), `--dry-run` and `--allow-destructive`, and refuses `--output` without `--script` and `--script` with `--dry-run`, as the host does (`FCMS3300`). It prints the changes from the newest row of the schema history to the host's schema in the words of `schema diff`, when the history has a row, then one line per operation, a destructive one marked as `schema diff` marks it, and ends with what it applied and the schema hash that the history now records. What it does is in 4.3. A host with content migrations in its set or in its history is `FCMS3335`; a pending system or plugin migration `FCMS3336`, which asks to run `fluentcms migrate` first; a change that loses data `FCMS3337`, refused unless `--allow-destructive` is given and then repeated as a warning; a required column with no empty value for the rows a table has `FCMS3338`; an operation the database refuses `FCMS3339`, after the transaction is rolled back; and a history row that cannot be written after the operations were applied `FCMS3340`. `--dry-run` exits with the code the run would have, so 1 when it would be refused. Codes `FCMS3335` to `FCMS3340`; `FCMS3341` to `FCMS3349` are free. No log event id was added: the operations are the command's output, and the history row logs 9311 as before.

**Transfer and backup commands as built (B-101, [#3024](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3024)).** `export` and `import` take `InstanceOptions` and `ProjectOptions`: with `--url` the tool calls the transfer endpoints itself (`TransferClient`, on the client the tool makes for an instance), and without it hands the command to the host with its paths made absolute (4.14). `backup` and `restore` are project commands only (4.15). The test that every command of the host is in the tool with the same options leaves `--url` and `--token` out of the comparison, as they are the tool's own. Tests give `CommandLine.RunAsync` a client of a test server for the instance. The guide is `docs/backups.md`.

**Generate as built (B-93, [#3016](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3016)).** `generate typescript --output <dir>`, `generate csharp --output <dir> --namespace <name>` and `generate openapi --output <file>` run in the tool. The first two read the schema snapshot of 4.2: with `--schema <file>` from a file, with `--url` from `GET /api/management/v1/schema/snapshot` through `InstanceClient`, as `schema snapshot --url` reads it, and in project mode from the host's `schema snapshot --output`, as `schema diff` reads it, without the database. The text is read back with `SchemaBuilder` over `UncheckedFieldTypes`, which stands in for a field type of a plugin, and the files depend on that text alone, so the three sources give the same bytes, which a test holds for the Blog sample. `--schema` and `--url` together are `FCMS3377` (exit code 2), and a text that is not a snapshot is `FCMS3370`, before any file is written. A field type that core does not know is text of the snapshot that no code checked, and the emitters write it into comments, so one that is not a name (an ASCII letter, then ASCII letters, digits, `.`, `-` and `_`) is also `FCMS3370`, naming the field and not the type ([#3064](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3064)); the field types of core and of the first-party plugins are names. An instance that refuses the key or cannot be reached is `FCMS3330` or `FCMS3329`, as for the schema tools. `generate openapi` saves `/openapi/v1.json` of an instance, or in project mode the document `IOpenApiDocumentProvider` builds in the host without listening, which the hidden host command `openapi document --output <file>` writes (`FCMS3379` when the host has none). A hidden command is left out of the host's usage and has no command of its name in the tool.

- TypeScript: one module, `fluentcms.ts`, which imports `@fluentcms/client` as the namespace `fluentcms`, so no name of the schema clashes with an export: an interface per content type (extending `Entry`, with the system fields), component (`SeoComponent`) and block type (`HeroBlock`, with `type` as its literal and an optional `id`); a blocks field is the union of its block types, a relation `Relation<Author, "author">`, the reference or the populated entry, a media field `MediaReference`, rich text `RichText`, a select the union of its options, and a localized type has `fallbacks` with its localizable fields. The builders are those the C# emitter gives, by name, with their members in camel case (`ArticleFields.sections.hero.heading.eq("x")`), and `createClient(options)` binds each content type to a collection or singleton client.
- C#: a class per content type (`[ContentType("article")]`, deriving from `Entry`), component (`SeoComponent`) and block type (`HeroBlock`, deriving from the generated `Block`, which names its derived types for `type` and reads one it does not know as itself), with `JsonPropertyName` on every property; a relation is `Relation<Author>`, a media field `MediaReference`, rich text and JSON `JsonElement`, and a localized type has `{Name}Fallbacks`. The builders are those of the emitter of B-95 over the field types of `FluentCMS.Client`, so that `ArticleFields.Title.Contains("x")` reads the same in a host and in a client. Every file carries the schema hash in its header and is written only when it changed; a file with the header of an earlier run that is no longer generated, such as the class of a removed type, is removed.
- `--check` writes nothing, and exits 1 with `FCMS3372`, naming each file that differs, is missing or is no longer generated, when the files on disk are not what the schema gives. A field type without a generated type is JSON or `unknown` with `FCMS3375`, and a name that gives no identifier or is taken gets nothing, with `FCMS3376`; both are warnings and the command exits 0. Other codes: `FCMS3371` for a request for the OpenAPI document of an instance that fails (with the status and the problem's code, never the key), `FCMS3373` for files that cannot be written, `FCMS3374` for a namespace that is not one and `FCMS3378` for a host that wrote no snapshot or document. Codes `FCMS3370` to `FCMS3379` of the range `FCMS3370` to `FCMS3389`.
- The clients: `FluentCmsClient` over the caller's `HttpClient`, which carries the key or the bearer token, and `FluentCmsClient` of `@fluentcms/client` over `fetch` with the headers given; an answer that is not a success throws `FluentCmsProblemException` or `FluentCmsProblem` with the status, `code`, `title`, `detail`, `errors` and `traceId` of the problem. A write leaves out the system fields and sends a relation or a media field as its id, and an update sends `If-Match` with the version read. Both write a query as the bracket grammar of 4.4 in the order and with the escaping of the server's writer, and are held to one list of filters and the query strings they give, `clients/filter-cases.json`, whose cases on the Blog sample's fields a test sends to its Delivery API. A moment is written in UTC with `Z` and no trailing zeros in its fraction, which a JavaScript `Date` and a `DateTimeOffset` both give.

### 6.2 Templates

`fluentcms`: an API host with `Program.cs` (with the command entry point of 6.1), `appsettings.json`, a sample `Article` type, the design-time factory and an empty `Migrations/` folder, a tool manifest that pins `fluentcms`, references to the generator and the analyzers, a Dockerfile and a README; options for provider and sample content. It ships no generated migration, since a migration set belongs to one provider. `fluentcms-plugin`: a server project with manifest, settings, a hook, an endpoint, a migration and a test project on `FluentCMS.Testing`; the admin half is added when the admin exists.

**Templates as built (B-94, [#3017](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3017)).** `templates/FluentCMS.Templates.csproj` packs `templates/fluentcms` and `templates/fluentcms-plugin` as the template package `FluentCMS.Templates`, and compiles nothing. At pack time it copies the template files to `obj/` and stamps them: `{{version:FluentCMS}}` becomes the version being packed, so every FluentCMS package and the `fluentcms` tool of a created project have the version of the templates package, and `{{version:<package>}}` the version of that package in `Directory.Packages.props`, so the third-party packages of a created project follow the repository's. An unknown package fails the pack. No package is published; CI installs the templates from a folder feed.

`dotnet new fluentcms -n <name> [--provider sqlite|postgresql|sqlserver] [--sample none|blog]` (defaults `sqlite` and `none`) writes a web project: `Program.cs` registers the `SchemaModel` of the project's types, built with the container's `IFieldTypeRegistry` so that the media field type is known, calls `AddFluentCms`, the command entry point between `Build()` and `UseFluentCms`, `MapFluentCms` and `MapFluentCmsOpenApi`; `CmsSetup` holds what the host and the design-time factory share: `SchemaBuilder.AddContentTypesFromAssembly` over the project, the provider of the option with `ConnectionStrings:FluentCms`, and the media and webhooks plugins with `AddPlugin`, plus `AddBlogSample()` with the sample. `CmsContextFactory` is the `IDesignTimeDbContextFactory<CmsDbContext>` of `migrations add`: it builds the field types from the same services and passes a connection string that is never opened. `--sample none` gives one `Article` (title, slug, rich text body, an indexed date); `--sample blog` gives a copy of the Blog sample's types, settings and seeder in the project (`Blog/`, without the OpenAPI examples), since the sample is not a package. `Migrations/Content` is empty: a migration set belongs to one provider (`FCMS1306`), so the first one is `fluentcms migrations add Initial` for the provider chosen. The tool manifest pins `fluentcms.cli` to the templates' version with `rollForward` off. The generator and the analyzers are package references with `PrivateAssets="all"`. `appsettings.json` has `Data Source=fluentcms.db` on SQLite and no connection string on the servers; `appsettings.Development.json` names a local server without a password, which comes from user secrets (the project has a `UserSecretsId`) or `ConnectionStrings__FluentCms`. The first launch profile runs in Development on `https://localhost:5443` and `http://localhost:5080`, since the authorization server answers on HTTPS only, and there SQLite needs no further step: schema sync creates the tables at start. The Dockerfile is the reference host's, built from the project folder: SQLite sets `ConnectionStrings__FluentCms` to `/data/fluentcms.db`, the media files go to `/data/media`, it has no health check, since the host has no probe, and no compose file comes with it (B-99). A `.gitignore`, a `.dockerignore` and a README of the first ten minutes complete it. The source names of both templates have a hyphen (`FluentCms-App`, `FluentCms-Plugin1`), so that the template engine tells a path from a namespace: a name that is not a namespace, such as `my-site`, keeps it in file names, project paths and the Dockerfile, and gets `my_site` in code.

`dotnet new fluentcms-plugin -n <name>` writes a solution with `src/<name>` and `tests/<name>.Tests`. The last segment of the name, letters and digits, gives the class prefix (`Acme.Reviews` gives `ReviewsPlugin`), its kebab case the plugin's name, settings area and routes (`reviews`), and that name with underscores its tables (`cms_reviews_note`). The plugin has the manifest with `SdkRange = "[1.0.0,2.0.0)"` and the assembly's version, a permission `{name}.notes.manage`, a settings class with `MaxNotes`, an `IAfterPublishHook<ContentDocument>`, a note table through `PluginDbContext<TPlugin>` with its stream of three sets (`Sqlite`, `PostgreSql`, `SqlServer`, each `Initial{Prefix}`), and `GET` and `POST /api/management/v1/{name}/notes`, which check the permission with `IPermissionChecker` and the settings of the site. It references `FluentCMS.Sdk` and `FluentCMS.Plugins.Abstractions`, the EF Core providers of the two server sets, the generator and the analyzers with `PrivateAssets="all"`; an architecture test holds the template's project file to that, as it does a first-party plugin. The test project, on `FluentCMS.Testing` with xUnit v3, boots the discovered plugin in the test host and checks its table, the hook's registration, the permission on the endpoints and the settings limit through the Management API, and that each migration set matches the model on its provider; it holds the design-time factory of `dotnet ef`. The sample plugin of the tests stays their fixture and the template is not generated from it.

The `Templates` job of CI packs every package of the build with the version `1.0.0-ci.{run}.{attempt}` into a folder feed, installs `FluentCMS.Templates` from it with a NuGet configuration that takes the FluentCMS packages from that feed only, creates a host for each provider with and without the Blog sample and a plugin, builds each with warnings as errors, runs the plugin's tests, checks that every FluentCMS package and the tool are at the version of the package and that each Dockerfile has the lines of its provider, creates a host and a plugin whose names are not namespaces, starts the two SQLite hosts in Development and reads their sample type through the Delivery API, and on the job's PostgreSQL service runs `fluentcms migrations add Initial` and `fluentcms migrate` and starts the host on that database (`templates/test-templates.sh`, which runs locally too). No FCMS code, event id, migration or SDK member was added.

### 6.3 Test host

`FluentCmsTestHost` on `TestServer` (`WebApplicationFactory` needs the entry point of the application under test, and the CMS library has none): `UseSqlite(inMemory or file)`, `WithContentTypes<...>()`, `WithPlugin<T>()`, fixtures `CreateUserAsync(role)`, `CreateApiKeyAsync(class)`, `AsUser(user)` and `AsKey(key)` HTTP clients, `Content<T>()` for in-process calls, `Seed(...)`. `Seed` steps and the registered seeders (4.16) run before the host reports started. The project's own integration tests use it from milestone M1, which is why it ships early; the Blog sample tests are the first to use a seeder. `RunCommandAsync(args)` runs `fluentcms <args>` as a host's command entry point does and returns the exit code and the two outputs (`TestCommandResult`): before the test host has started, on a host of its own over the same database that is built and never started, and after, against the started host (B-90).

### 6.4 Source generator and analyzers

`FluentCMS.Generators` emits, per content type in the host: a typed filter and sort builder (`ArticleFields.Title.Contains("x")`) and block union helpers. The field types the builders return are in the SDK. `FluentCMS.Analyzers` reports `FCMS0xxx` diagnostics: invalid attribute combinations, reserved names, slug source missing, relation to a non-content type, `Localizable` on a non-localized type, a filtered field without `Indexed` (info). Each ships as a package of its own that a host references.

**Source generator as built (B-95).** `FluentCMS.Generators` is an incremental generator that a host references as an analyzer, as the Blog sample and the reference host do. It finds the classes marked `[Collection]`, `[Singleton]`, `[Component]` or `[Block]` and reads them as the attribute reader does (4.2): the same names (`JsonNamingPolicy.CamelCase` for a field, `KebabCaseLower` for a type, ported because the generator runs on netstandard2.0), the inherited properties first, and only the properties with one field attribute and a public getter and setter. A class the reader refuses gets no builder and no word from the generator, since the host reports it at start and the analyzers in the editor. It emits, in the namespace of the class:

- `{Type}Fields` for a collection or singleton, a static class with a member per field, so that `ArticleFields.Title.Contains("x")` gives the same `FieldFilter` as `new FieldFilter("title", FilterOperator.Contains, "x")` and `ArticleFields.PublishedOn.Descending()` a `SortField`.
- `{Name}ComponentFields` for a component, whose members carry the path of the field that holds it: `ArticleFields.Seo.MetaTitle` is `seo.metaTitle`, at any depth of single components. A repeated component is JSON a filter cannot reach and gets no member.
- `{Name}BlockFields` for a block type, with a member for each scalar field one level inside the list (Q7), and for each blocks field a nested class over `BlocksField` with the block types of the field and their presence: `ArticleFields.Sections.Hero.Heading.Eq("x")` is `sections.hero.heading`, `ArticleFields.Sections.HasHero()` is `hasBlock` with `hero`.

The class names come from the schema names, not the CLR names, so that a client emits the same code from a snapshot; the suffixes keep a component or a block from taking the name of a collection, and a name that still clashes, such as a collection `x-block` beside a block `x`, gives the builder to the first and reports the other with `FCMS0100`. A block type whose name has a hyphen gets no builder (`FCMS0104`), because a segment of a filter path holds only letters, digits and underscores; its block lists still have `Has{Name}()`. The members are named from the field names. The builders are public, so a library that declares content types shares its builders with the host.

The field types are in the SDK, in `FluentCMS.Content`, each a `QueryField` with its path and only the operators the engine accepts for its kind: `TextField` (`text`, `slug`, `email`, `url`), `LongTextField`, `RichTextField`, `NumberField<T>` (`long` for `integer`, `decimal`), `DateField<T>` (`DateOnly` for `date`, `DateTimeOffset` for `dateTime`), `BooleanField`, `SelectField` and `SelectListField` for a single and a multiple select, `RelationField` and `MediaField` by id, `JsonField` with `Key(name)` giving a `JsonKeyField`, `BlocksField`, and inside a block `BlockTextField`, `BlockValueField<T>` and `BlockContainsField`. A field that the engine can sort has `Ascending()` and `Descending()`. A select takes the option as the API shows it (`video`), not the enum member. System fields such as `createdAt` have no builder yet.

The emitter is shared source in `src/FluentCMS.Generators/Emit`: `FieldsEmitter.Emit` takes descriptions of the types (names, kinds, fields with their field type, `Multiple`, component and block types) and the namespace of the field types, and returns the sources and diagnostics. The generator fills the descriptions from symbols; `Snapshot/SchemaDescriptions.cs`, which needs the SDK and is linked rather than compiled into the generator, fills them from a `SchemaModel`, so `generate csharp` (B-93) emits the same text from a snapshot, as a test holds for the Blog sample. Its diagnostics are `FCMS0100` (a type gets no builder: its name gives no class name or the name is taken), `FCMS0101` (info: a field type with no builder, such as a plugin type other than `media`), `FCMS0102` (a member name taken in the builder) and `FCMS0103` (info: a component or block type declared in another project, which gets no builder here) and `FCMS0104` (info: a block type with a hyphen in its name). `FCMS0105` to `FCMS0119` are free. Tests in `tests/FluentCMS.Generators.Tests`, which the analyzers share, check the output for given sources, that an unrelated edit regenerates nothing, that each builder offers exactly the filters and sorts the content service accepts, and that the fields of the Blog builders are the fields of its schema.

**Analyzers as built (B-96).** `FluentCMS.Analyzers` is a package of its own with the analyzers under `analyzers/dotnet/cs` and no library, built on netstandard2.0 against Roslyn 5.0.0 like the generator, so that every .NET 10 SDK from 10.0.100 loads it. The reference host, the Blog sample and the first-party plugins reference it as an analyzer only (`ReferenceOutputAssembly="false"`, with `PrivateAssets="all"` in a plugin, so a package does not depend on it), and an architecture test holds them to that; the layering rules of 3.4 do not count such a reference. `SchemaAttributeAnalyzer` reads each class marked `[Collection]`, `[Singleton]`, `[Component]` or `[Block]` as the attribute reader does (the generator's names, the inherited properties first, a class the host does not discover, such as an abstract one, left alone) and reports, at the attribute or member that causes it, what schema validation refuses, its message naming the `FCMS12xx` code: attribute combinations `FCMS0001` to `FCMS0016` (two kinds, the wrong base, a publish policy on a component or block, modifiers without a field type, two field types, a field without a public getter and setter, select options that do not fit the property, a relation that is not a `Ref<T>`, a component or block field whose class is not one, blocks without types, two maximum lengths, a list of a single-value field type, `[Unique]` on a field stored as a reference or as JSON, a reverse relation outside a content type), names `FCMS0020` to `FCMS0023` (reserved field, reverse relation and system column names, a type on a system table, and the limits of 3.3 for type and field names, tables, flattened columns and reference paths), a missing slug source `FCMS0030`, a relation target that is not a content type `FCMS0040`, all errors, and `Localizable` on a field of a type that is not localized `FCMS0050`, a warning. `FilterIndexAnalyzer` reports, as information `FCMS0060`, a filter or sort of a B-95 builder on a field with a column of its own and neither `Indexed` nor `Unique`, where the builder is used; it stands for no code. A base class's property is reported once at its declaration. What needs the whole schema, such as a duplicate type name, the nesting depth, a type on another type's locale table, a field type no plugin registers and the storage of a media field, whose plugin the analyzers cannot see, and what the reader finds only by running code, such as a condition, is left to the host. The reserved names and storage limits are copied from the SDK and core, and a test holds them to the originals. The help link of a rule is its section in `docs/analyzers.md`, the error reference of the compile-time codes; `ErrorCodeRegistry` lists the codes a client sees and not these. Tests in `tests/FluentCMS.Generators.Tests` give each rule a source it reports, checked with `Microsoft.CodeAnalysis.CSharp.Analyzer.Testing` for place and severity, which `SchemaBuilder` refuses with the code the message names, and the corrected source, which the analyzers accept and which builds a `SchemaModel`; the Blog sample and the generator's kitchen are reported clean. No code fixes yet.

**Left for after 1.0 (10.3, P12).** The reflection-free `ContentDocument` mapper waits for a benchmark that shows the mapper of the content service as a cost, and the rule for obsolete SDK members for the first member that is obsolete.

**Client generation (10.3, P9).** Q15 decided an own TypeScript emitter and left the C# generator to B-93. Both are own emitters that read the schema snapshot, not the OpenAPI document: the snapshot has what the types need and exists without a running instance. The C# emitter is shared source with the source generator, so the builders read the same in a host and in a client. The generated code compiles against `FluentCMS.Client` and `@fluentcms/client` (2.4), which M6 builds with only what generated code needs and does not publish. No MSBuild target runs `generate` on build (10.3, P14).

**Client generation as built (B-93).** The tool links `Emit/*.cs` and `Snapshot/SchemaDescriptions.cs` of `FluentCMS.Generators` rather than referencing the analyzer, and calls the emitter with the namespace of `FluentCMS.Client`, whose field types have the members of the SDK's, so the C# builders of a client are the text a host gets with the field types' namespace changed, as a test holds for the Blog sample. The TypeScript emitter is the tool's own and gives the same builders: none for a type whose name gives no class, a block type with a hyphen or a repeated component. Both emitters write a field type into a comment, and into the `FCMS3375` warning, only when it passes the check that `generate` holds a snapshot to (6.1), and the C# emitter hands the emitter of B-95 no field whose type fails it, since that emitter's `FCMS0101` warning names the type; such a field gets no builder either way ([#3064](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3064)). Every other text of a snapshot that reaches the code is checked or a literal: the names of types, fields and block types only when they give an identifier, which schema validation holds them to as well, and in TypeScript the options of a select and the target of a relation as string literals with JSON's escaping; display names, descriptions, defaults and validation rules are not written. The Blog code that the clients' tests compile is checked in, under `tests/FluentCMS.Client.Tests/Blog` and `clients/typescript/test/blog`, and a test of the tool runs `--check` on both, so it is what the tool writes; the C# compiles on its own against `FluentCMS.Client` with every warning an error, and the TypeScript with `tsc` in strict mode.

## 8. Milestones

Follows the sequencing note in the decisions log: SDK and core first, because every plugin depends on them.

| Milestone | Goal | Exit criteria |
| --- | --- | --- |
| M0 Foundation | Solution, build and CI exist; nothing runs yet. | `dotnet build` and `dotnet test` green in CI on Linux and Windows; packages pack; the test matrix job runs on SQLite (PostgreSQL and SQL Server deferred, Q19). |
| M1 SDK and core engine | A host can define types in C#, store and query entries in-process with permissions, versions, publishing, scheduling, events and jobs on SQLite (other providers deferred, Q19). No HTTP yet. | The sample `Blog` types round-trip through `IContentService<T>` in the test host; the storage spike's decision is recorded; migrations generate for SQLite. |
| M2 HTTP and identity | Delivery and Management APIs, authorization server with hosted pages, admin users, roles, keys, 2FA, SSO federation, activity log endpoint, OpenAPI, caching, limits, health, observability, startup diagnostics, reference host image. | An editor can be invited, sign in through the hosted pages, obtain a token with PKCE, create and publish content through the Management API, and read it through the Delivery API with a key, all from the Docker image on SQLite (PostgreSQL and SQL Server deferred, Q19). |
| M3 Plugin host, media, webhooks | Plugin contract proven by two first-party plugins. | A plugin project from the server template boots in the test host with its own tables, permissions, settings and job; images upload and transform through signed URLs on local, S3 and Azure storage; a publish triggers a signed webhook with retries. |
| M4 Agents and GraphQL | MCP server and GraphQL plugin. | Claude or an IDE client connects to `/mcp` through OAuth, lists tools filtered by role, drafts an entry and cannot publish without the permission; GraphQL queries and mutations mirror REST. |
| M5 Multi-site, members, AI assist | Hostname-resolved sites, member authentication with external providers, AI actions. | Two sites on different hostnames share types and media with separate content and roles; a member registers, verifies and signs in with Google; an AI action returns a suggestion and logs usage. |
| M6 Tooling and transfer | CLI, templates, source generator, analyzers, export and import, backups. | `dotnet new fluentcms`, `fluentcms migrations add`, `fluentcms migrate`, `fluentcms generate typescript`, `fluentcms export` and `import --dry-run` work end to end on a fresh machine. |
| M7 Hardening and preview | The backend is reviewed, its open follow-ups are settled, and it is published as a first public preview. | `1.0.0-preview.1` of every package of 2.4 is on nuget.org and the image of the reference host is on the registry, both pushed by the `Publish` workflow from a tag on `main`; the `Fresh machine` sequence passes against nuget.org on a clean machine; each migration stream has one migration for each provider; no bug and no High or Medium finding of the review is open. |

The deferrals of Q19 in M0 to M2 ended with [#2711](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2711): PostgreSQL 15 and SQL Server 2019 and 2025 are supported and tested providers, and CI runs the data tests on each beside SQLite (4.3).

M1 and M2 are the critical path. M3 can start once the SDK is stable (end of M1). M6 pieces that unblock earlier milestones (`FluentCMS.Testing`, the content migration command) are pulled into M1.

The exit criteria of M6 are the `Fresh machine` jobs, which run the sequence from packages in a folder feed on clean Linux and Windows runners ([#3023](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3023)): `eng/fresh-machine.ps1` installs the templates and the tool from the feed, makes a Blog host, runs `migrations add` and `migrate` on SQLite and, on Linux, PostgreSQL, compiles the output of `generate typescript`, runs `export`, `import --dry-run` and `import` into a second host and compares an entry through the Delivery API, and builds and tests a plugin; a broken feed must fail it. Nothing is published for it (10.3, P8).

After M6 comes M7, planned on 12 Nov 2026 (10.4). It was foreseen as a hardening milestone with the remaining follow-ups of the M5 review (10.3, P13), and was planned wider: it reviews the code of M4 and M6, brings in the search plugin, cuts the AI assist plugin to OpenAI-compatible endpoints, publishes one migration baseline, and ends with the first public preview of the backend. The technical specification of the admin is drafted beside it, as a document of its own. The Redis pack stays a Should.
